की धारा 9 की उपधारा (3) के खंड (क) द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए, केन्द्रीय सरकार, एतद्द्वारा, श्री रजनीश 
कर्नाटक (जन्म तिथिः 14.7.1970), प्रबंध निदेशक एवं मुख्य कार्यकारी अधिकारी, बैंक आफ इंडिया के कार्यकाल की 
वर्तमान अधिसूचित अवधि, जो दिनांक 28.4.2026 को समाप्त हो रही है, को आगे तीन वर्ष की अवधि के लिए अथवा

का.आ. 770.---औद्योगिक विवाद अधिनियम, 1947 (1947 का 14) की धारा 17 के अनुसरण में केन्द्रीय सरकार 

पूर्व रेलवे के प्रबंधतत्र, संबद्ध नियोजकों और उनके कर्मकारों के बीच अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट औद्योगिक विवाद में केन्द्रीय सरकार 
औद्योगिक अधिकरण / श्रम न्यायालय नं 4 धनबाद के पंचाट (09/2003) प्रकाशित करती है। 

[सं. एल -41011/30/2002-आई आर (बी- 1)] 


सलोनी, उप निदेशक 


MINISTRY OF LABOUR AND EMPLOYMENT 
New Delhi, the 29th April, 2026 
S.O. 770.—In pursuance of Section 17 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central 
Government hereby publishes the Award (Ref.09/2003) of the Cent.Govt.Indus.Tribunal-cum-Labour Court No. 


1,Dhanbad_as shown in the Annexure, in the industrial dispute between the management of Eastern Railway and 
their workmen. 


[No. L-41011/30/2002- IR(B-D] 
SALONI, Dy. Director 
ANNEXURE 
BEFORE THE CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL NO.1,DHANBAD 
In the matter of reference U/S 10 (1) (d)& (2A) of I.D.Act. 1947. 
Reference Case No. 09/2003 


Parties: 
Employer in relation to the management of Divisional Railway 
Manager, Eastern Railway, Mughalsarai, Varanasi (U.P.) 


4058 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 9, 2026/VAISAKHA 19, 1948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 
AND 
Their Workman 


Present: Shri Sachindra Kumar Pandey 


Presiding Officer 
Appearances: 
For the Employers :- Sri S.K. Sinha, Ld. Advocate 
For the workman :- None. 
State : Bihar. Industry:-Railway 


Dated 06/02/2026 
AWARD 


In exercise of powers conferred under clause (d) of sub-section (1) and sub —section (2A) of Section 10 of 
the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947),Government Of India through the Ministry of Labour, vide its Order 
No.L-41011/30/2002 -IR(B-I) dated 18/12/2002 has been pleased to refer the following dispute between the 
employer i.e. management of D.R.M. Eastern Railway, Mughalsarai Division and their workman through General 
Secretary, Gaya Railway Poldar Workers Union, Gaya for adjudication by this Tribunal: 


SCHEDULE 


“Whether the claim of Gaya Railway Station Poldar Workers Union for issuing identity card and 
treating them at par with the licensed porters by railway administration is legal and justified? if so, 
what relief the union is entitled to?”?” 


2. On receiving order no. L-41011/30/2002 -IR(B-I) dated 18/12/2002 Government of India, Ministry of Labour, 
New Delhi for adjudication of the dispute, Reference case no. 09 of 2003 was registered on 01.01.2003 and thereafter 
the notices were sent to the parties with a direction to appear and submit their written statements along with relevant 
documents and witnesses in support of their claims. 


3. After service of notice, both parties appeared previously and filed their authorities, W/S and subsequently 
rejoinder. It further appears that on 22.09.2025 this case was reserved for award but in course of dictating judgement 
it transpired that hearing on behalf of the workman side is necessary and this case put up for argument of workman on 
01.12.2025 but workman failed to appear before this Tribunal and all notices returned back with endorsement 
“refused” as well as re-notice with endorsement “Addressee Left”. 


4. On perusal of the entire case record it transpires that workman/union has lost his interest in this case and 
therefore, for the ends of justice, this case deserves to be dismissed for non prosecution. 


5. Hence, 
ORDERED 


that this case is hereby dismissed for non prosecution and a “No Dispute Award” be drawn up in respect of the 
above reference case. Let the copies of Award in duplicate be sent to the Ministry of Labour & Employment, 
Government of India, New Delhi for information and notification. 


SACHINDRA KUMAR PANDEY, Presiding Officer 
नई दिल्‍ली, 29 अप्रैल, 2026 
का.आ. 771.--औद्योगिक विवाद अधिनियम, 1947 (1947 का 14) की धारा 17 के अनुसरण में केन्द्रीय सरकार 
भारतीय लागत रक्षक के प्रबंधतत्र, संबद्ध नियोजकों और उनके कर्मकारों के बीच अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट औद्योगिक विवाद में 
केन्द्रीय सरकार औद्योगिक अधिकरण » श्रम न्यायालय चेन्नई के पंचाट (3/2019) प्रकाशित करती है। 


[सं. एल-14011/15/2018- आई आर (बी-1)| 


सलोनी, उप निदेशक 


New Delhi, the 29th April, 2026

Award 


The Central Government, Ministry of Labour & Employment vide its Order No. L-14011/15/2018-IR(DU) 
dated 27.11.2018 referred the following Industrial Dispute to this Tribunal for adjudication. 


Con..2 
The schedule mentioned in that order is: 


“Whether the action of the management of Indian Coast Guard, Base Maintenance Unit, Chennai, in 
transferring Sh. P. Ayan Arul Raj, Radio/Radar Fitter to [CGS Mandapam in an non-existing post at ICGS 
Mandapam is legal and justified? If not, to what relief Sh. P.Ayan Arul Raj, Radio/Radar Fitter is entitled 
to?” 


As per the claim statement the petitioner is an Indian Coast Guard Civilian Employees Union, registered under the 
Trade Union Act, 1926 was formed primarily to protect the interest of the workmen and for the welfare of the 
employees apart from maintaining harmonious relationship with the Management. It is submitted that there are 60 
civilian workmen in the establishment and most of them are members of the Union, this being the only union 


4060 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 9, 2026/VAISAKHA 19, 1948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


functioning in the establishment to espouse the cause of the workmen. It is submitted that the Union raised an 
Industrial Dispute before the Assistant Labour Commissioner (C) by letter dated 21.11.2017 under Section 2(K) of 
the Industrial Dispute Act,1947 over the issue relating to the unfair labour practices adopted by the respondent 
Management. It is submitted that the union was registered on 07.07.2017 vide Regn. No.508/TVR under the Trade 
Unions Act, 1947 and thereafter in the General Body Meeting the office bearers were elected. It is submitted that the 
fact relating to the registration of the union and the list of elected office bearers was communicated to the respondent 
Management by letter No.ICGEU/BMU/01/2017, dated 15.07.2017. 


2. It is submitted that the 1 respondent has framed the “Transfer Policy-Civilian” by letter no.CP/0518 dated 
22.11.2006 and circulated to the Regional Commanders for compliance. Para 4(c) deals with Group ‘B’ ‘C’ ‘D’ 
Non-Gazetted personal, wherein it is clearly stated that postings and transfer of these employees are to be kept at 
absolute minimum level keeping in view the administrative requirement and need for manning the posts at various 
stations. It has also been clearly mentioned that in case of transfers being inescapable, volunteers for the postings or 
transfer will be called. As far as possible only volunteers will be transferred to these vacancies. It has also been 
clearly stated that as far as possible the group ‘C’ and ‘D’ employee should not be transferred over long distance 
and efforts should be made to accommodate them near their choice station to the extent possible. 


3. As per the above, Shri P. Ayan Arul Raj was elected as Treasurer of the union. Since the Respondent / 
Management was against the formation of the union, the employee Shri P. Ayan Arul Raj was relieved and directed 
to report for duty at ICGS, Mandapam by letter No.CP/277/17, dated 31.10.2017 


4. The transfer order dated 20.10.2017 was not served on the workman till date. While the petitioner union requested 
that Shri P. Ayan Arul Raj be declared a protected workman, but the Management swiftly reacted by transferring 
Shri P. Ayan Arul Raj and victimized him by adopting unfair labour practice. Hence requested the following relief. 


i) To declare that the transfer order bearing no.1340 dated 20.10.2017 issued by the 37 Respondent 
transferring Shri P. Ayan Arul Raj, Radio/Radar Fitter from Basic Maintenance Unit, Chennai to Indian Coast 
Guard Station, Mandapam is not justified as per the guidelines laid down by the 1“ respondent in the Transfer 
Policy CP/0518 dated 22.11.2006 


ii) And to pass such other award/order as this Hon’ble Central Government Industrial Tribunal-cum-Labour 
Court may deem fit and necessary in the circumstances of the case and thus render justice. 


Petitioner submits the affidavit of Shri P. Ayan Arul Raj and filed following documents 


Exhibit Date Description 
No. 


Ex.W1 07.07.2017 Certificate of Registration under the Trade Unions Act,1926 
Ex.W2 31.10.2017 Relieving Order issued to Shri P. Ayan Arul Raj 


Ex.W3 22.11.2006 Transfer policy issued by the 1“ respondent in 
CP/0518 


Ex.W4 21.11.2017 Industrial Dispute raised by the Union before the Assistant 
Labour Commissioner 


Ex.W5 22.01.2018 Clarification points submitted by the 37 respondent addressed 
to the Assistant Labour Commissioner 


Ex.W6 29.01.2018 Letter submitted by the Union addressed to the Assistant 
Labour Commissioner 


Ex.W7 26.02.2018 Letter submitted by the Union addressed to the Assistant 
Labour Commissioner 


Ex.W8 09.04.2018 Letter submitted by the Union addressed to the Assistant 
Labour Commissioner 


Ex.W9 10.04.2018 Letter submitted by the 37 respondent addressed to the 
Assistant Labour Commissioner 


Ex.W10 | 10.04.2018 Minutes recorded by Assistant Labour Commissioner 
regarding failure of conciliation 


Ex.W11 | 04.05.2018 Conciliation failure report submitted by the Assistant Labour 
Commissioner to the Ministry, New Delhi 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 9, 2026/वैशाख 19, 1948 4061 


5. The respondent in its reply stated that the Indian Coast Guard Civilian Employees Union has not been recognized 
by the Ministry of Defence, as per CGHA letter WE/0302/Welfare dated 09.10.2019.The transfer order of the three 
(3) civilian personal was promulgated vide CGRHQ(E) letter 1340 dated 20.10.17. Accordingly, the individual was 
relieved from Basic Maintenance Unit, Chennai to report at Indian Coast Guard Station, Madapam as per the 
transfer policy and pleaded that the petition be dismissed as devoid of merit. 


6. The Respondent filed the following documents in support of their contention 


Exhibit No. Date Description 

Ex.M1 18.04.2018 Nature of duties carried out by Indian Coast Guard Base 
Maintenance Unit (Chennai) 

Ex.M2 09.10.2019 Service Association and Welfare in Indian Coast Guard 

Ex.M3 22.11.2006 Transfer Policy - Civilians 


7. Heard both learned counsel and perused the records. The point arised for determination is whether the transfer of 
Shri P. Ayan Aurl Raj, Radio/Radar Fitter as ICGS, Mandapam is an not existing post at ICGS, Mandapam is legal 
and justify? If not, what relief is Shri P. Ayan Arul Raj, Radio/Radar fitter is entitled to? 


8. The claimant in its claim statement stated that the following points regarding the transfer policy. 


(a) Posting and transfer are to be kept at the absolute minimum level 
(b) Volunteers for the postings of the transfer called and will be transferred 
(c) Group ‘C’ and ‘D’ employees should not be transferred over long distance 


9. In petition claim the hardships caused to transferring described as under 


(a) Shri P. Ayan Arul Raj was transferred during October,2017 quite impossible for getting admission for his 
school going children at the new station 

(b) The workman unable to shift his family in the midst of the academic year 

(c) The employee kept the family in Chennai for his child to complete the studies in the academic year but the 
Estate Office, CPWD directed him to vacate the quarters and threatened to impose a penalty 

(d) The employee Shri P. Ayan Arul Raj fell sick because of the mental agony and remains on leave on medical 
ground from 1.11.2017 to 28.6.2018 

(e) When the workman reported for duty on 29.06.2018 at the Mandapam station, the 31 Respondent directed 


him to report for duty in Chennai for four days in a week. 


10. On perusal of whole the claim statement, the claimant nowhere stated that Shri P. Ayan Arul Raj was transferred 
to a non-existing post at ICGS Mandapam. On perusal of the claim statement, it appears that the Respondent did not 
violate the transfer policy, as the Mandapam station is not far away from Chennai station. 


11. It is the fundamental duty of transferring employee to seek the permission to overstay at Govt. accommodation by 
mentioning the sufficient reasons for occupation of the allotted house. In claim petition this facts nowhere mention 
that any application mentioning sufficient reasons for over staying at the allotted house was made or not. In this 
situation it can be presumed that no application was made by the transferring employee to the authority. 
Transferring employee Shri P. Ayan Arul Raj fails to submit the medical certificates which might have established 
the genuine grounds of his absence due to medical reasons. 


12. It is stated in the claim petition that the employee was transferred due to his involvement in the union, but the 
claimant fails to submit any evidence that may establish this fact that respondent had showed its jealous against the 
employee. The only vague reason given is that he was an elected as an office bearer in the union, the transfer of an 
employee do not appear as a result of his election to the union. 


13. The claimant has stated that no transfer order was provided to the transferred employee and that only a relieving 
order was provided to the transferee employee. The relieving order issued to Shri P. Ayan Arul Raj dated 
31.10.2017 is available at page no.2 of the paper book in this document, it is mentioned clearly that the Competent 
Authority has approved the transfer of Shri P. Ayan Arul Raj with a direction to report at ICGS Mandapam by 
10.11.2017. The relieving order was issued by the Section Officer in compliance with the order passed by the 
Competent Authority hence, no right of the transfree employee was violated by not providing a separate transfer 
order. 


14. As per the discussion above, the transfer order was not made by the Respondent in violation of transfer policy or 
against the non-existing post, it also not proved that this order was made due to desirous wish of the respondent. 


AWARD 
The claim petition filed by the Petitioner union is dismissed. The reference is answered accordingly. 


SUSHIL KUMAR-I, Presiding Officer

For the Second Party / Respondent : NIL 


नई दिल्‍ली, 29 अप्रैल, 2026 


का.आ. 772.---औद्योगिक विवाद अधिनियम, 1947 (1947 का 14) की धारा 17 के अनुसरण में केन्द्रीय सरकार 

बड़ौदा यूपी ग्रामीण बैंक के प्रबंधतत्र, संबद्ध नियोजकों और उनके कर्मकारों के बीच अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट औद्योगिक विवाद में 
केन्द्रीय सरकार औद्योगिक अधिकरण » श्रम न्यायालय लखनऊ के पंचाट (31/2022) प्रकाशित करती है। 

[सं. एल- 12011/17/2022- आई आर (बी-1)| 

सलोनी, उप निदेशक 


New Delhi, the 29th April, 2026 


S.O. 772.—In pursuance of Section 17 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central 
Government hereby publishes the Award (Ref. 31/2022) of the Cent.Govt.Indus.Tribunal-cum-Labour Court 
Lucknow_ as shown in the Annexure, in the industrial dispute between the management of Baroda UP Gramin Bank 
and their workmen. 


[No. L-12011/17/2022- IR (8-1) | 
SALONI, Dy. Director 


ANNEXURE 

CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL-CUM-LABOUR COURT. LUCKNOW 

PRESENT 

JUSTICE ANIL KUMAR PRESIDING OFFICER 
I. D. No. 31/2022 
Ref. No. L-12011/17/2022-IR(B-D dated: 02.06.2022 
BETWEEN 
The General Secretary, All India Ex-Servicemen Bank Employees’ Federation Rajaipuram Lucknow-226017 
And 

The Chairman, Baroda UP Gramin Bank, Hqrs, Shivpuri Colony, Taramandal, GORAKHPUR - 273016 

AWARD 


By order No. L-12011/17/2022-IR(B-D) dated: 02.06.2022 the present industrial dispute has been referred 
for adjudication to this CGIT, Lucknow, in exercise of the powers conferred by clause (d) of sub-section (1) and sub- 
section (2A) of Section 10 the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947) by the Central Government, with following 
schedule: 


"Whether the demand of All India Ex Servicemen Bank Employees' Federation with regard to allowing 2 
years weightage to the ex-servicemen while promoting from sub-staff to clerical cadre & Clerical to Officer 
is justified? If yes, what relief the workman is entitled to?" 


Accordingly, an industrial dispute No. 31/2022 has been registered. 
On 24.04.2023 claimant filed claim statement has been filed with following relief: 


“(i) Modification in 02 Yrs Weightage in Promotion circular in Baroda UP Bank be Expunged from its 
circular. 


(ii) Govt policy letter no. 02/8/78-SCT (B) dated 28th January, 1983 and further F. No. 10/49/84-SCT (B) 
dated 13th Aug 1986 be implemented in letter and spirit in Baroda UP Bank. 


(iii) Weightage of all candidates who lost the opportunity of 02 Years be re-stored till they actually avail the 
opportunity along with Shri sanjeev Mandal. 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 9, 2026/वैशाख 19, 1948 4063 


(iv) Weightage to Ex-Serviceman, who could not succeed in the in promotion exam or could not actually 
avails the opportunity due to any reason/exigencies, their chances be retained as unused for future 
promotion examination.” 


On behalf of the respondent statement of defense filed on 08.10.2023 denying claim of the workman; wherein 
preliminary objection has been taken by the respondent. 


Thereafter, workman filed rejoinder on 04.04.2025 and evidence on affidavit in support of his statement of claim on 
26.05.2025. 


However, from the perusal of record, it is clearly established that claimant/workman did not turn up for cross- 
examination in spite of several opportunities; and it is a case of no evidence from the side of workman/claimant in 
support of his case. 


Thus, in view of the above said facts and the law as laid by the Hon'ble High Court in the case of V. K. Raj Industries 
v. Labour Court (1) and others 1981 (29) FLR 194 as under: 


“It is well settled that if a party challenges the legality of an order, the burden lies upon him to prove 
illegality of the order and if no evidence is produced the party invoking jurisdiction of the Court must fail. 
Whenever a workman raises a dispute challenging the validity of the termination of service if is imperative 
for him to file written statement before the Industrial Court setting out grounds on which the order is 
challenged and he must also produce evidence to prove his case. If the workman fails to appear or to file 
written statement or produce evidence, the dispute referred by the State Government cannot be answered in 
favour of the workman and he would not be entitled to any relief.” 


In the case of M/s Uptron Powertronics Employees’ Union, Ghaziabad through its Secretary v. Presiding Officer, 
Labour Court (I), Ghaziabad and others 2008 (118) FLR 1164 Hon’ble Allahabad High Court has held as under: 


“The law has been settled by the Apex Court in case of Shanker Chakravarti v. Britannia Biscuit Co. Ltd., 
V.K. Raj Industries v. Labour Court and Ors., Airtech Private Limited v. State of U.P. and Ors. 1984 (49) 
FLR 38 and Meritech India Ltd. v. State of U.P. and Ors. 1996 FLR that in the absence of any evidence led 
by or on behalf of the workman the reference is bound to be answered by the court against the workman. In 
such a situation it is not necessary for the employers to lead any evidence at all. The obligation to lead 
evidence to establish an allegation made by a party is on the party making the allegation. The test would be, 
who would fail if no evidence is led.”’ 


And by the Hon’ble Allahabad High Court in the case of District Administrative Committee, U.P. P.A.C.C.S.C. 
Services v. Secretary-cum-G.M. District Co-operative Bank Ltd. 2010 (126) FLR 519; wherein it has been held as 
under: 


“The submission is that even if the petitioner failed to lead the evidence, burden was on the shoulders of the 
respondent to prove the termination order as illegal. He was required to lead evidence first which he failed. 
A perusal of the impugned award also does not show that any evidence either oral or documentary was led 
by the respondent. In the case of no evidence, the reference has to be dismissed.” 


As the claimant/workman did not turn up for cross-examination, so it is a case of no evidence filed on behalf of 
workman in support of case, liable to be dismissed. 


For the foregoing reasons, the case is dismissed and; and the workman is not entitled for any relief. 
Award as above. 
Lucknow. 
09" February, 2026. 
Justice ANIL KUMAR, Presiding Officer 


नई दिल्‍ली, 29 अप्रैल, 2026

उतर पूर्व रेलवे के प्रबंधतत्र, संबद्ध नियोजकों और उनके कर्मकारों के बीच अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट औद्योगिक विवाद में केन्द्रीय

AWARD

adjudication to this CGIT, Lucknow, in exercise of the powers conferred by clause (d) of sub-section (1) and sub- 
section (2A) of Section 10 the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947) by the Central Government, with following 
schedule: 


"Whether the demand of the Union that the action of NE Railway in not regularizing the service of Shri Ram 
Jiawan s/o Late Shri Sahdeo who worked as casual booking clerk from 1.1.93 to 31.10.2015 and non 
payment of retirement benefits if legal and justified and what direction are require in the case?”’ 


Accordingly, an industrial dispute No. 37/2019 has been registered. 
On 14.01.2020 claimant filed claim statement has been filed with following relief: 


“That, from the facts and circumstances as mentioned above it is most respectfully prayed that this Hon'ble 
Tribunal may very kindly be pleased to answer this reference in favour of workmen and against the 
management in the interest of justice.” 


On behalf of the respondent statement of defense filed on 04.02.2021 denying claim of the workman. 


Thereafter, workman filed rejoinder on 21.11.2022 and evidence on affidavit in support of his statement of claim on 
18.01.2023. 


In the meantime respondent filed application to amend written statement on 10.05.2024, allowed vide order dated 
17.02.2025; and workman was given opportunity to file rejonder. 


However, from the perusal of record, it is clearly established that claimant/workman neither filed any rejoinder nor 
turned up for cross-examination in spite of several opportunities; and it is a case of no evidence from the side of 
workman/claimant in support of his case. 


Thus, in view of the above said facts and the law as laid by the Hon'ble High Court in the case of V. K. Raj Industries 
v. Labour Court (1) and others 1981 (29) FLR 194 as under: 


“It is well settled that if a party challenges the legality of an order, the burden lies upon him to prove 
illegality of the order and if no evidence is produced the party invoking jurisdiction of the Court must fail. 
Whenever a workman raises a dispute challenging the validity of the termination of service if is imperative 
for him to file written statement before the Industrial Court setting out grounds on which the order is 
challenged and he must also produce evidence to prove his case. If the workman fails to appear or to file 
written statement or produce evidence, the dispute referred by the State Government cannot be answered in 
favour of the workman and he would not be entitled to any relief.” 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(1)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 9, 2026/वैशाख 19, 1948 4065 


In the case of M/s Uptron Powertronics Employees’ Union, Ghaziabad through its Secretary v. Presiding Officer, 
Labour Court (I), Ghaziabad and others 2008 (118) FLR 1164 Hon’ble Allahabad High Court has held as under: 


“The law has been settled by the Apex Court in case of Shanker Chakravarti v. Britannia Biscuit Co. Ltd., 
V.K. Raj Industries v. Labour Court and Ors., Airtech Private Limited v. State of U.P. and Ors. 1984 (49) 
FLR 38 and Meritech India Ltd. v. State of U.P. and Ors. 1996 FLR that in the absence of any evidence led 
by or on behalf of the workman the reference is bound to be answered by the court against the workman. In 
such a situation it is not necessary for the employers to lead any evidence at all. The obligation to lead 
evidence to establish an allegation made by a party is on the party making the allegation. The test would be, 
who would fail if no evidence is led.” 


And by the Hon’ble Allahabad High Court in the case of District Administrative Committee, U.P. P.A.C.C.S.C. 
Services v. Secretary-cum-G.M. District Co-operative Bank Ltd. 2010 (126) FLR 519; wherein it has been held as 
under: 


“The submission is that even if the petitioner failed to lead the evidence, burden was on the shoulders of the 
respondent to prove the termination order as illegal. He was required to lead evidence first which he failed. 
A perusal of the impugned award also does not show that any evidence either oral or documentary was led 
by the respondent. In the case of no evidence, the reference has to be dismissed.” 


As the workman did not file any evidence on affidavit in support of his case, so it is a case of no evidence filed on 
behalf of workman in support of case, liable to be dismissed. 


For the foregoing reasons, the case is dismissed and; and the workman is not entitled for any relief.

का.आ. 774.--औद्योगिक विवाद अधिनियम, 1947 (1947 का 14) की धारा 17 के अनुसरण में केन्द्रीय सरकार 
पूर्वांचल ग्रामीण बैंक के प्रबंधतत्र, संबद्ध नियोजकों और उनके कर्मकारों के बीच अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट औद्योगिक विवाद में 
केन्द्रीय सरकार औद्योगिक अधिकरण » श्रम न्यायालय लखनऊ के पंचाट (04/2015) प्रकाशित करती है। 
[सं. एल 12012/15/2015- आई आर (बी-)]

Government hereby publishes the Award (Ref. 04/2015) of the Cent.Govt.Indus.Tribunal-cum-Labour Court 
Lucknow_as shown in the Annexure, in the industrial dispute between the management of Purvanchal Gramin Bank 
and their workmen. 


[No. L-12012/15/2015- IR (8-1) | 
SALONI, Dy. Director

AWARD 


By order No.L-12012/15/2015 (IR(B-D) Dated: 17.02.2015, the present Industrial Dispute has been referred 
for adjudication in exercise of the powers conferred by clause (d) of sub-section (1) and sub-section (2A) of Section 
10 the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947) by the Central Government, with following schedule: 


क्या प्रबंधन; पर्वांचल ग्रामीण बैंक (पूर्वांचल FA), गोरखपुर FART श्री रविन्द्र प्रसाव गुप्ता को विनाक 30-04-2006 व्‌ 
आगे काम करने के उपरांत नॉकरी से निकल दिया जाना न्यायोचित एवं बैध हैं? यवि नहीं तो वावी किस राहत को पाने 
Fl BREN हैं?" 

Accordingly, the present I.D case no.04/2015, registered before this tribunal. 

FACTS OF THE CASE 

On 06.04.2015, claimant filed written statement stating therein the following facts in brief as under: 


Workman/claimant was initially appointed in the month of May 1995, on post of jamadaar cum chaprasi on casual 
basis and the said capacity he started working and discharging his duties till 29.04.2006. 


On 30.04.2006 his services were terminated/retrenched orally without following the provisions as provided u/s 25F of 
the Industrial Dispute Act 1947, and the schedule framed therein here an after referred as Act. 


He further pleaded that impugned action on the part of the respondent thereby terminating/retrenchment his services 
of the workman, is only to law because retain service of his juniors so the same is clear violation of Section 25G & 
25H of the Act. 


Workman/claimant since the date of retrenchment is without any job and at the verge of starvation. 


Accordingly it is prayed by claimant, that order of termination/retrenchment may be set aside and he may reinstated 
back in his services, from the date he was retrenched, with all services benefits should be given to him. 


On behalf of respondent written statement was filed on 06.04.2016, in which the following objections/preliminary 
objections has been taken:- 


1). The present reference is devoid of any merits and bad in the eyes of law and as such not maintainable. 
2). The reference order is itself not clear in regard alleged date of termination. 


3). No cause of dispute rather an Industrial Dispute existed on the alleged date of termination, i.e. 30.4.2006, between 
the Opposite Parties and Ravindra Prasad Gupta. 


Further it has been pleaded by the respondent in their written statement as under:- 


a). The present case has been filed after an inordinate delay of more than 7 years before the Learned Regional Labour 
Commissioner, Lucknow and reference is made by the Central Government dated 17.02.2015 i.e. after a lapse of 9 
years from the alleged date of termination. 


b). The Learned Regional Labour Commissioner, Lucknow, without proper application of mind, contrary to the 
settled principles of law acting like a postman send the report to the Central Government for referring the matter for 
adjudication. 


c). As per the provisions of section 2 A sub section (3), of the Industrial Disputes’ Act, 1947, any application for 
adjudicating any dispute or difference between a workman and his employer connected with, or arising out of 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 9, 2026/वैशाख 19, 1948 4067 


discharge, dismissal, retrenchment or termination shall be made to the Labour Court or Tribunal before the expiry of 
three years from the date of such discharge, dismissal, retrenchment or termination. 


d). A limitation of three years for raising an industrial dispute has been provided in the Industrial Disputes' Act, 1947, 
itself and provisions of any extension of limitation period has not been provided in the Industrial Disputes' Act, 1947, 
by the legislature. 


e). On the basis of the above mentioned averments, it is requested that the present reference dated 17.02.2015, should 
be rejected as bad in the eyes of law and as such not maintainable. 


On behalf of respondent evidence on affidavit of Shri Ashotosh Anand, Chief Manager, HRM of the bank filed, he 
was cross examined on 04.05.2015. 


On behalf of workman on 27.06.2016 rejoinder affidavit has been filed denying the averments as taken by respondent. 


On 05.11.2020, workman/claimant has filed evidence on affidavit (Examination in Chief) and he was cross examined 
on 07.01.2021, by respondent. 


During the pendency of case as per notification dated 26.11.2019, moved by the Ministry Of Finance (Department of 
Financial Services), relevant portion quoted herein below:- 


Baroda Uttar Pradesh Gramin Bank, Kashi Gomti Samyut Gramin Bank and Purvanchal Bank (hereinafter 
referred to as "the transferor Regional Rural Banks") sponsored by the Bank of Baroda, Union Bank of India 
and State Bank of India in the State of Uttar Pradesh are hereby amalgamated into a single Regional Rural 
Bank which shall be called as Baroda U.P. Bank (hereinafter referred to as "the transferee Regional Rural 
Bank") with its head office at Gorakhpur under the sponsorship of Bank of Baroda. 


Accordingly as per the said notification respondent 10.1 0 & 1B were imp leaded as party, In view of the law laid 
down by Hon’ble Madras High Court in case of Palanisamy R vs. Presiding Officer, Labour Court, 2001 
LawSuit(Mad) 152, and also in the case of Shri Kanchi Kamakoti Peetam Charitable Trust Hospital vs. Industrial 
Tribunal & Others, 2004 (100) FLR 1166. 


I have heard Shri Parvez Alam legal representative of claimant, and Shri Adarsh Jagdhari learned counsel for 
respondent. 


After hearing the parties/their legal representative and Advocate, the first point to be considered the point to be 
consider in the present case:- 


A). whether the reference dated 17.02.2015, is vague and on the basis of the same no cause of action arises on the 
part of workman/claimant to file present claim petition. 


In order to decide the said dispute it would be appropriate to go through Section 10 of the Industrial Dispute Act. 
10. Section 10 of Industrial Disputes Act reads thus: 


"10. Reference of disputes to Boards, Courts or Tribunals. (1) [Where the appropriate Government is of 
opinion that any industrial dispute exists or is apprehended, it may at any time), by order in writing,- 


(a) refer the dispute to a Board for promoting a settlement thereof; or 
(b) refer any matter appearing to be connected with or relevant to the dispute to a Court for inquiry; or 


[(c) refer the dispute or any matter appearing to be connected with, or relevant to, the dispute, if it relates to 
any matter specified in the Second Schedule, to a Labour Court for adjudication; or 


(d) refer the dispute or any matter appearing to be connected with, or relevant to, the dispute, whether it 
relates to any matter specified in the Second Schedule or the Third Schedule, to a Tribunal for adjudication: 


Provided that where the dispute relates to any matter specified in the Third Schedule and is not likely to 
affect more than one hundred workmen, the appropriate Government may, if it so thinks fit, make the 
reference to a Labour Court under clause (c):]" 


11. Plain reading of aforesaid provision would show that when industrial dispute exists or is apprehended, appropriate 
Government can refer dispute to Court for enquiry or if relates to any matter specified in Second Schedule to Labour 
Court for adjudication or make Reference to Tribunal, if dispute relates to matters specified in Second Schedule or 
Third Schedule. It is, therefore, obvious that Reference can be made only when industrial disputes exists or is 
apprehended by Competent Authority. 


13. The aforesaid legal position would make explicit that existence of industrial dispute is a paramount and important 
consideration while dealing with Reference. 


Only existent or apprehended industrial disputes can be referred for consideration of Court or Tribunal. However, at 
the time of taking decision whether Reference to be made or not, appropriate Government need not venture into 


4068 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 9, 2026/VAISAKHA 19, 1948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


merits of dispute, as making Reference is only administrative function. Only because Reference is made under 
Section 10(1), Court or Tribunal is not precluded from entering into aspects of delay and laches or to record findings 
as to existence or non-existence of dispute. 


In case of Prabhakar v. Joint Director, Sericulture Department and "another, 2015 (147) FLR 341 (SC), the 
Hon’ble Supreme Court considered and held the impact of delay and laches in Reference made under Section 10(1) of 
Industrial Disputes Act and observed in paragraph No. 14 as under: 


"40. An industrial dispute has to be referred by the appropriate Government for adjudication and the 
workman cannot approach the Labour Court or Industrial Tribunal directly, except in those cases which are 
covered by Section 2-A of the Act. Reference is made under Section 10 of the Act in those cases where the 
appropriate Government forms an opinion that ‘any industrial dispute exists or is apprehended’. The words 
‘industrial dispute exists' are of paramount importance unless there is an existence of an industrial dispute 
(or the dispute is apprehended or it is apprehended such a dispute may arise in near future), no reference is 
to be made. Thus, existence or apprehension of an industrial dispute is a sine qua non for making the 
reference. No doubt, at the time of taking a decision whether a reference is to be made or not, the 
appropriate Government is not to go into the merits of the dispute. Making of reference is only an 
administrative function. At the same time, on the basis of material on record, satisfaction of the existence of 
the industrial dispute or the apprehension of an industrial dispute is necessary. Such existence/apprehension 
of industrial dispute, thus, becomes a condition precedent, though it will be only subjective satisfaction 
based on material on record. Since, we are not concerned with the satisfaction dealing with cases where 
there is apprehended industrial dispute, discussion that follows would confine to existence of an industrial 
dispute. Dispute or difference arises when one party make a demand and other party rejects the same. It is 
held by this Court in number of cases that before raising the industrial dispute making of demand is a 
necessary pre-condition. In such a scenario, if the services of a workman are terminated and he does not 
make the demand and/or raise the issue alleging wrongful termination immediately thereafter or within 
reasonable time and raises the same after considerable lapse of period, whether it can be said that industrial 
dispute still exist. Since there is no period of limitation, it gives right to the workman to raise the dispute 
even belatedly. However, if the dispute is raised after a long period, it has to be seen as to whether such a 
dispute still exists? Thus, notwithstanding the fact that law of limitation does not apply, it is to be shown by 
the workman that there is a dispute in praesenti. For this purpose, he has to demonstrate that even if 
considerable period has lapsed and there are laches and delays, such delay has not resulted into making the 
industrial dispute seized to exist. Therefore, if the workman is able to give satisfactory explanation for these 
laches and delays and demonstrate that the circumstances discloses that issue is still alive, delay would not 
come in his way because of the reason that law of limitation has no application. On the other hand, if 
because of such delay dispute no longer remains alive and is to be treated as "dead", then it would be non- 
existent dispute which cannot be referred." 


Thus it can be held on the point in issue that for raising industrial dispute demand is precondition. In case, employee 
fails to make demand, dispute cannot be referred. 


From the perusal of claim statement filed by claimant on 06.05.2005, undisputed facts which emerged out that he was 
initially appointed as casual labour on the post of jamadaar cum chaprasi in the purvanchal gramin bank, Gorakhpur, 
now known as Baroda UP bank, in the branch saleempur dewaria, in the said capacity he worked and discharged his 
duties up till 29.04.2006, his services were retrenched/terminated, so workman raise a Industrial Dispute and by 
reference dated 17.02.2015, the same was referred to this Tribunal for adjudication quoted here an above. 


Further in the written statement filed by respondent, a preliminary objections/plea was taken that the reference in 
question is vague as alias date of termination as mentioned in the reference dated 30.04.2006, is incorrect and as 
matter of fact on record, thereafter workman work with the respondent/bank. 


In this regard the workman/Shri Ravindra Prashad Gupta in his evidence on affidavit (examination in chief), and on 
27.01.2021 he was cross examined, in which he has stated as under:- 


Act 2006 42012 केबीच में Act HH नहीं किया क्योंकि बैंक ने निकत्नविया 27 2006 से पहले मैं बैंक में काम कर रहा 
था| 


2013 के लिए काम का पैसा मित्र गया। इस पैसे का कोर्ड विवाद नहीं है। 2006 तक जो काम किया था उसका भी पैसा 
मिना था।/2013 के बाव से अब तक बैंक में काम नहीं किया हैं। 2006 में निकानने के बाव 2013 में बैंक ने GE बुलाया 
HR एक बार2013 में मिलाने के बाव बैंक ने नहीं बुनाया। इसलिए नहीं गया। 2013 में Ast बुलाया गया His चिट॒ठी 
नहीं जारी हुई थी. जबानी कहा गया। 


Accordingly from the cross examination of the workman, it is clearly established that he has worked as a casual 
employer in the bank in the year 2013, as such in view of the provision of Section 10(1) of the Industrial Dispute Act, 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(1)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 9, 2026/वैशाख 19, 1948 4069 


as quoted here an above as well the facts which is stated u/s of the Section 10(1) of the Act, no dispute was in 
existence which is paramount condition for making a reference in question not as per law. 


Further in the present case from the language of reference it is clearly established that the appropriate government has 
referred to decide the dispute that is the action on the part of respondent thereby retrenching the services of 
Shri Ravindra Prasad Gupta/claimant, on 30.06.2006, is as per law and valid, if not then what relief he is granted. 


As stated above in the present case the workman/Shri Ravindra Prasad Gupta, has himself admitted the facts that after 
retrenchment of his services on 30.06.2006, he did not worked for the period 2006 to 2012, thereafter he had worked 
in the bank in 2013 and for the said period he has been worked been paid salary and after 2013 he was not allowed to 
work by respondent/bank, so action on the part of respondent thereby retrenching his services is in contravention 
Section 25F of the Act, is vague in nature as per settle position of law, further in order to established by a workman 
that his services were retrenched not in accordance with provisions of Section 25F of the Act, workman has to prove 
that he has worked continuously 240 days in last 12 months in preceding calendar year, from the date of his 
retrenchment.(See in the case of Lenin Kumar Ray vs. M/s Express Publications (Madurai) Ltd, SLP (C) No.5660 
of 2023, decided on 21.10.2024). 


In the present case admittedly neither by way of pleading nor by way of evidence the workman has established that he 
has worked 240 days continuously in last 12 months in preceding calendar year, from prior to date of retrenchment of 
his services as casual employer after 2013 in the year, as such he is not entitled for any relief. 


It is relevant to mention herein Shri Parvez Alam after arguing at some length he admitted the said facts and request 
that the present claim petition may dismissed as not pressed with a liberty to the claimant to raise his grievance fresh 
before appropriate authority, as such if workman is so advice he may raise his grievances in the matter in question as 
per law. 


AWARD 


For the foregoing reasons the workman is not entitled for any relief as per the Reference No. L-12012/15/2015 (R(B- 
1)) Dated: 17.02.2015, and the same is answered accordingly. 


Award as above. 
Lucknow 17" February, 2026 
Justice ANIL KUMAR, Presiding Officer

का.आ. 775.---औद्योगिक विवाद अधिनियम, 1947 (1947 का 14) की धारा 17 के अनुसरण में केन्द्रीय सरकार 
उतर पूर्व रेलवे के प्रबंधतत्र, संबद्ध नियोजकों और उनके कर्मकारों के बीच अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट औद्योगिक विवाद में केन्द्रीय 
सरकार औद्योगिक अधिकरण / श्रम न्यायालय लखनऊ के पंचाट (12/2024) प्रकाशित करती है। 
[सं. एल- 12025/01/2026- आई आर (बी-1)-84] 
सलोनी, उप निदेशक 


New Delhi, the 29th April, 2026 


99.0. 775.—In pursuance of Section 17 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central 
Government hereby publishes the Award (Ref. 12/2024) of the Cent.Govt.Indus.Tribunal-cum-Labour Court 
Lucknow_ as shown in the Annexure, in the industrial dispute between the management of North Eastern Railway 
and their workmen. 


[No. L-12025/01/2026- IR (B-I)-84] 

SALONI, Dy. Director 

ANNEXURE 
CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL —CUM- LABOUR COURT, LUCKNOW 
PRESENT 
JUSTICE ANIL KUMAR 
PRESIDING OFFICER 
1.0. No. 12/2024 
Ref. No. K-10/2/2022-IR dated 21.10.2022 


4070 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 9, 2026/VAISAKHA 19, 1948 [PART II—SEc. 3(ii)] 
Krishan Chandra Diwedi, Bankatia Inguri Bazar Odeoria, Deoria, Uttar Pradesh-274704 
AND 
GENERAL MANAGER, NER, Gorakhpur, Uttar Pradesh-274704 
AWARD 


By order No. K-10/2/2022-IR dated 21.10.2022 the present industrial dispute has been referred for 
adjudication to this Tribunal in exercise of the powers conferred by clause (d) of sub-section (1) and sub-section (2A) 
of Section 10 the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947) by the Central Government, with following schedule: 


"Whether the action of the management of North Eastern Railway, Gorakhpur and Lucknow in terminating 
the services of Shri Krishna Chandra Dwivedi, Casual Labour w.e.f. 31.12.1976 is justified and legal? If not 
to what relief the workman is entitled to” 


Accordingly, an industrial dispute No. 12/2024 has been registered on 24.04.2024. 


From the perusal of record, the position which emerge out is that the till date the claimant/workman has not filed any 
statement of claim in spite of repeated opportunity given to him, in order to establish his claim, as per the reference 
dated 21.10.2022. 


So in view of the said facts, as well as the law laid by the Hon’ble High Court in the case of V. K. Raj Industries v. 
Labour Court (I) and others 1981 (29) FLR 194 as under: 


“Tt is well settled that if a party challenges the legality of an order, the burden lies upon him to prove illegality 
of the order and if no evidence is produced the party invoking jurisdiction of the Court must fail. Whenever a 
workman raises a dispute challenging the validity of the termination of service if is imperative for him to file 
written statement before the Industrial Court setting out grounds on which the order is challenged and he must 
also produce evidence to prove his case. If the workman fails to appear or to file written statement or produce 
evidence, the dispute referred by the State Government cannot be answered in favour of the workman and he 
would not be entitled to any relief.” 


In the case of M/s Uptron Powertronics Employees’ Union, Ghaziabad through its Secretary v. Presiding Officer, 
Labour Court (I), Ghaziabad and others 2008 (118) FLR 1164 Hon’ble Allahabad High Court has held as under: 


“The law has been settled by the Apex Court in case of Shanker Chakravarti v. Britannia Biscuit Co. Ltd., V.K. 
Raj Industries v. Labour Court and Ors., Airtech Private Limited v. State of U.P. and Ors. 1984 (49) FLR 38 
and Meritech India Ltd. v. State of U.P. and Ors. 1996 FLR that in the absence of any evidence led by or on 
behalf of the workman the reference is bound to be answered by the court against the workman. In such a 
situation it is not necessary for the employers to lead any evidence at all. The obligation to lead evidence to 
establish an allegation made by a party is on the party making the allegation. The test would be, who would fail 
if no evidence is led.” 


And by the Hon’ble Allahabad High Court in the case of District Administrative Committee, U.P. P.A.C.C.S.C. 
Services v. Secretary-cum-G.M. District Co-operative Bank Ltd. 2010 (126) FLR 519; wherein it has been held as 
under: 


“The submission is that even if the petitioner failed to lead the evidence, burden was on the shoulders of the 
respondent to prove the termination order as illegal. He was required to lead evidence first which he failed. A 
perusal of the impugned award also does not show that any evidence either oral or documentary was led by the 
respondent. In the case of no evidence, the reference has to be dismissed.” 


As the workman has not filed any statement of claim/oral/documentary evidence, so the present case is liable to be 
dismissed. 


For the foregoing reasons, the case is dismissed and; and the workman is not entitled for any relief. 
Award as above. 
Lucknow. 
097 February, 2026 
Justice ANIL KUMAR, Presiding Officer

का.आ. 776.---औद्योगिक विवाद अधिनियम, 1947 (1947 का 14) की धारा 17 के अनुसरण में केन्द्रीय सरकार

औद्योगिक अधिकरण » श्रम न्यायालय लखनऊ के पंचाट (65/2021) प्रकाशित करती है। 

[सं. एल- 41011/17/2021- आई आर (बी-1)| 

सलोनी, उप निदेशक 


New Delhi, the 29th April, 2026

Government hereby publishes the Award (Ref. 65/2021) of the Cent.Govt.Indus.Tribunal-cum-Labour Court 
Lucknow_ as shown in the Annexure, in the industrial dispute between the management of Northern Railway and 
their workmen. 


[No. L-41011/17/2021- IR (8-1) |

I. D. No. 65/2021 
Ref. No. L-41011/17/2021-IR(B-I) dated: 15.04.2021 
BETWEEN 
Shri Parvez Alam, Mandal Sangthar Mantri, Uttar Railway, Karmchari U 283/63 Kha, Gadhi Konoura (Premwati 
Nagar), Post-ivianak Nagar, Lucknow-22601 1 


AND 
1. The Sr. DEE, O/o DRM, Northern Railway, Hazratganj, Lucknow-226001 
2. The Sr. DPO, O/o DRM, Northern Railway, Hazratganj, Lucknow-226001 
AWARD 


By order No. L-41011/17/2021-IR(B-I) dated: 15.04.2021 the present industrial dispute has been referred for 
adjudication to this CGIT, Lucknow, in exercise of the powers conferred by clause (d) of sub-section (1) and sub- 
section (2A) of Section 10 the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947) by the Central Government, with following 
schedule: 


"1. Whether the claim of Uttar Railway Karmchari union regarding promotion of the workman Shri Ram 
Kewal Saurabh w.e.f. 2008 on the post of Tech-III grade of power group is legal and justified in eye of law 
or not? 


2. Whether action of the management of NR. Lucknow is to delay the process of promotion and grant the 
same after 10 years in 2019 to the workman Shri Ram Kewal Saurabh without providing financial benefit 
with retrospective effect is illegal and unjustified in the eye of law or not?” 


Accordingly, an industrial dispute No. 65/2021 has been registered. 


On 13.12.2021 claimant filed claim statement, with following relief: 


श्रीमान्‌ जी से विनम निवेवन हैं कि कर्मकार राम केवल ANH को 50% पदोन्नति कोटे से वेतन आदि सभी हित नाभों 
सहित FF 2007 १2009 से पदोन्नति प्रवान करने हेतु अवार्ड पारित करने की कृपा करें। महान कृपा होगी।” 
On behalf of the respondent statement of defense filed on 14.02.2023 denying claim of the workman. 


Thereafter, workman filed rejoinder & list of documents on 26.04.2023; and evidence on affidavit dated 24.04.2023, 
in support of his statement of claim. 


4072 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 9, 2026/VAISAKHA 19, 1948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


However, from the perusal of record, it is clearly established that claimant/workman did not turn up for cross- 
examination in spite of several opportunities; and it is a case of no evidence from the side of workman/claimant in 
support of his case. 


Thus, in view of the above said facts and the law as laid by the Hon'ble High Court in the case of V. K. Raj Industries 
ए. Labour Court (1) and others 1981 (29) FLR 194 as under: 


“Tt is well settled that if a party challenges the legality of an order, the burden lies upon him to prove illegality 
of the order and if no evidence is produced the party invoking jurisdiction of the Court must fail. Whenever a 
workman raises a dispute challenging the validity of the termination of service if is imperative for him to file 
written statement before the Industrial Court setting out grounds on which the order is challenged and he must 
also produce evidence to prove his case. If the workman fails to appear or to file written statement or produce 
evidence, the dispute referred by the State Government cannot be answered in favour of the workman and he 
would not be entitled to any relief.” 


In the case of M/s Uptron Powertronics Employees’ Union, Ghaziabad through its Secretary v. Presiding Officer, 
Labour Court (I), Ghaziabad and others 2008 (118) FLR 1164 Hon’ble Allahabad High Court has held as under: 


“The law has been settled by the Apex Court in case of Shanker Chakravarti v. Britannia Biscuit Co. Ltd., V.K. 
Raj Industries v. Labour Court and Ors., Airtech Private Limited v. State of U.P. and Ors. 1984 (49) FLR 38 
and Meritech India Ltd. v. State of U.P. and Ors. 1996 FLR that in the absence of any evidence led by or on 
behalf of the workman the reference is bound to be answered by the court against the workman. In such a 
situation it is not necessary for the employers to lead any evidence at all. The obligation to lead evidence to 
establish an allegation made by a party is on the party making the allegation. The test would be, who would fail 
if no evidence is led.” 


And by the Hon’ble Allahabad High Court in the case of District Administrative Committee, U.P. P.A.C.C.S.C. 
Services v. Secretary-cum-G.M. District Co-operative Bank Ltd. 2010 (126) FLR 519; wherein it has been held as 
under: 


“The submission is that even if the petitioner failed to lead the evidence, burden was on the shoulders of the 
respondent to prove the termination order as illegal. He was required to lead evidence first which he failed. A 
perusal of the impugned award also does not show that any evidence either oral or documentary was led by the 
respondent. In the case of no evidence, the reference has to be dismissed.” 


As the workman did not turn up for cross-examination, so it is a case of no evidence filed on behalf of workman in 
support of case, liable to be dismissed. 


For the foregoing reasons, the case is dismissed and; and the workman is not entitled for any relief. 
Award as above. 
Lucknow. 
09" February, 2026. 
Justice ANIL KUMAR, Presiding Officer 


नई दिल्‍ली, 30 अप्रैल, 2026 


का.आ. 777.--औद्योगिक विवाद अधिनियम 1947 (1947 का 14 ) की धारा 17 के अनुसरण में केन्द्रीय सरकार 


प्रशासनिक कमांडर, स्टेशन मुख्यालय (नागरिक सुरक्षा), पंचकुला के प्रबंधतत्र, संबद्ध नियोजकों और उनके कर्मकारों के बीच 

अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट औद्योगिक विवाद में केन्द्रीय सरकार औद्योगिक अधिकरण /श्रम न्यायालय Well के 
पंचाट (130/2019) प्रकाशित करती है। 

[सं. एल- 12025/01/2026- आई आर (बी-1)-89] 

सलोनी, उप निदेशक 


New Delhi, the 30th April, 2026 


S.O. 777.—In pursuance of Section 17 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central

Chandigarh-II_ as shown in the Annexure, in the industrial dispute between the management of Admn. Commander 
Station Head Quarter ( Civil Defence), Panchkula and their workmen. 


[No. L-12025/01/2026- IR (B-I)-89] 
SALONI, Dy. Director 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 9, 2026/वैशाख 19, 1948 4073 
ANNEXURE 


IN THE CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL-CUM-LABOUR COURT-II, 
CHANDIGARH 


(Present:- Mr. Kamal Kant, Presiding Officer). 
ID No.130/2019 
Registered on:-16.10.2019 
Rakesh S/o Sh. Mool Chand, R/o 730/5 Bapu Dham colony Sector 26, Chandigarh. 
-------- Applicant/Workman 
Versus 
1. Admn. Commander Station Head Quarter (Civil Defence), Chandi Mandir Cantt. Panchkula, Haryana. 
2. M/s Sun Rise Facilitator Pvt. Ltd., 2253/1 on Floor, Pipli Wala Town, Mani Mazra, Chandigarh. 
----Respondents 
Present:- Sh. Jang Bahadur, AR for workman. 
Sh. Satinder Singh, AR for respondent no.1. 
Respondent no.2 already ex parte. 
Award :04.02.2026 


1.Workman has filed the present claim petition under Section 2A of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 
(hereinafter called the Act) against his termination w.e.f. 27.10.2018 claiming reinstatement into service along 
with continuity of service and full back wages. 


2.It is maintained by the workman in the claim petition that that he was appointed as sweeper/safai karamchari 
on 04.09.2016 by respondent 10.1 at minimum wages prescribed by the Central Government. But the said 
minimum wages were never paid to him. Management has unlawfully deducted 12% EPF share, 1.75% ESI 
and Rs.300/- as TDS from hissalary despite of the fact that his total wages comes under the ambit of taxfree 
limit. He was working under respondent no.1 directly, but respondent no.1 in order to avoid its liability always 
shows his employment in record under different contractors, which tantamount to unfair labour practice. 


3.It is further maintained that workman including 200 other safai karamcharis of respondent 10.1 raised the 
demand for non-deduction of TDS from the wages paid by respondent 10.1. However, their demand was not 
accepted. Other workers working under respondent 10.1 formed union and subsequently his services were 
dispensed with w.e.f. 27.10.2018. It is also maintained that respondent 10.1 retained juniors in service and also 
made fresh appointments after termination of his services and no compensation was paid under Section 25F of 
the Act. Thus, there is violation of Section 25F, 25G and 25H of the Act. Hence this petition. 


4.Notice of the petition was given to respondents. 


5.Respondent 10.1 filed reply to the claim petition maintaining therein that the workman has not come to the 
Court with clean hands as he was appointed as sweeper by respondent no.2 (contractor) at minimum wage rate 
as prescribed by the Central Government. As per Government Policy dated 18.03.2011 (Ex.R-1), Non- 
Cantonment Military Stations have been authorized to outsource conservancy safaiwala. The answering 
respondent entered into contract with various private agencies for outsourcing of sweepers (safai karamcharis). 
In July 2018, the answering respondent entered into contract with M/s AA Foundation for safety and 
M/s Sunrise Facilitator Pvt. Ltd. through e-tendering for outsourcing of safai karamcharis to provide 
conservancy services in Chandi Mandir Military Station for the financial year 2018-19. Respondent no. 2 
submits bill for payment for the days the safai karamcharis performed their duties and answering respondent is 
only responsible to forward these bills to PCDA Western Command Chandigarh to release payment directly to 
the contractor’s account. Salary is paid to the workman by the contractor. Compliance of PF, ESI Rules etc. is 
carried out by the contractor as per contract agreement. Answering respondent has no responsibility of 
disbursement of wages and deduction of EPF and ESI etc. The workman and other safai karamcharis formed 
union in the headquarter, which is not permitted and also carried out demonstration inside the premises of 
Station Headquarter on 27.10.2018 and failed to perform his duties. Thereafter, answering respondent asked 
respondent no.2 to ask the workers to perform their duties. Answering respondent has no role or responsibility 
for termination services of the workman. There is no relationship of employer and employee between the 
answering respondent and the workman. In view of Army Headquarter Letter, the civilian employees of the 
answering respondent cannot organize themselves into trade unions as station headquarter is an administrative 
office of the Government of India. In spite of that, workman along with other safai karamcharis formed union 
unauthorizedly and indulge in unlawful activities. It is maintained that workman is the employee of contractor 


4074 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 9, 2026/VAISAKHA 19, 1948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 
and therefore, termination is the prerogative of the contractor. It is prayed that the claim be dismissed. 


6.Respondent 10.2 also filed reply maintaining therein that workman worked with it w.e.f. 05.07.2018 to 
27.10.2018. Respondent no.2 has not terminated the services of workman. Workman was paid minimum 
wages as declared by the Centre Government from time to time. Workman himself left the job at his own will 
without notice. It is prayed that the claim may be dismissed. 


Evidence of workman:- 


7.In order to prove his case, workman examined himself as WW1 and tendered into evidence his affidavit 
WWI/A and has been cross examined by the AR for respondent 10.1. AR for workman closed the evidence on 
30.07.2024. 


Evidence of respondent no.1:- 


8.Respondent 10.1 examined Colonel K P Singh S/o Late Colonel P. P. Singh, OIC, Civic Cell, Station Head 
Quarter, Chandi Mandir Panchkula as MW1, who tendered into evidence his affidavit Ex.MW1/A along with 
documents Ex.R1 to Ex.R4. AR for respondent 10.1 closed the evidence on 02.02.2026. 


Sr. No. Document Dated Exhibit 
1. Policy 18.03.2011 Ex.R-1 
2: Agreement with respondent no.2 05.07.2018 Ex.R-2 
35 Copy of ECR of EPFO Ex.R-3 
4. Letter of Head Quarter 22.04.1980 Ex.R-4 


9.It is pertinent to mention here that respondent no.2 was proceeded ex parte on 20.04.2023. 


Submissions of workman:- 


10. While arguing the case, AR for workman contended that in this case, workman was appointed by 
respondent 10.1 under a camouflage agreement with respondent 10.2. The workman was appointed directly by 
respondent 10.1 on 04.09.2016 as sweeper and his services were dispensed with on 27.10.2018 without any 
reason in violation of Section 25F, 25G and 25H of the Act as no compensation was paid to the workman and 
junior to him were retained in service and fresh appointment were made after termination of the workman. So 
far as the agreements between the respondent 10.1 & 2 are concerned, they are totally not tenable and is unfair 
labour practice. The work performed by the workman is of perennial in nature. The contractor was not having 
any license. The contractor has left after three months. No domestic inquiry was held before terminating the 
service of the workman, which is mandatory under the Act. 


Submissions of respondent no.1:- 


11. On the other hand, AR for respondent no.1 contended that the workman was engaged by respondent 
no.2 (contractor) as per agreement executed between respondent 10.1 & 2 on outsource basis and there is no 
relationship of employer and employee between respondent 10.1 and the workman. Wages were paid by the 
respondent 10.2 to the workman and there was no role of respondent 10.1 in appointing or terminating the 
services of the workman. Workman is employee of respondent no.2. If there is any violation of provision of 
the Act, then it is done by respondent no.2. As per Ex.R3, all dues were paid by the contractor with regard to 
EPF and ESI etc. The workman and other safai karamcharis formed union in the Headquarter, which is not 
permitted as per Ex.R4. Respondent 10.1 being the Government Agency, workman is trying to take benefit of 
his appointment by respondent 10.2 and has filed a false claim after concealing material facts and claim of the 
workman is not maintainable. 


12. I have given due consideration to the arguments advanced by AR for both the parties. 


Findings:- 


13. The first contention regarding the applicant is that whether applicant comes within the definition of 
workman as is defined in Section 2(s) of the Act. I may mention that applicant was appointed as sweeper as 
per his claim petition and affidavit submitted before the Tribunal. In plain words the applicant was performing 
his duties as labourer/unskilled worker. He was not in supervisory or administrative post requiring him to 
perform only administrative duties. While interpreting Section 2(s) Hon’ble Supreme Court in the case of 


Devinder Singh Vs. Municipal Council, Sanaur, AIR 2011 Supreme Court 2532, has observed as follows:- 
“The source of employment, the quantum of recruitment, the terms & conditions of 


employment/ contract of service, the quantum of wages/ pay and mode of payment are 
not at all relevant for deciding whether or not a person is a workman within the 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 9, 2026/वैशाख 19, 1948 4075 


meaning of Section 2(s) of the Act. The definition of workman also does not make any 
distinction between full time and part time employee or a person appointed on contract 
basis. There is nothing in the plain language of Section 2(s) from which it can be 
inferred that only person employed on regular basis or a person employed for doing 
whole time job is a workman and the one employed on temporary, part time or contract 
basis on fixed wages or as a casual employee or for doing duty for fixed hours is not a 
workman.” 


14. Thus Hon’ble Supreme Court has clarified that the definition of workman also does not make any 
distinction between full time or part time employee or a person appointed on contract basis. There is nothing in 
plain language of Section 2(s) from which it can be infer that only person employed on regular basis or a 
person employed for doing whole time job is a workman and the one employed on temporary, part time or 
contract basis on fixed wages or as a casual employee or for doing duty for fixed hours is not a workman. In 
view of the ratio of law enunciated in the above ruling, in my considered opinion the applicant herein 
admittedly falls within the definition of ‘workman’ under Section 2(s) of the Act. 


15. The Hon’ble Supreme Court after analyzing the catena of cases has laid down in Balwant Raj 


Saluja Vs. Air India Limited in Civil Appeal No.10266 dated 25.08.2014, two well recognized tests to find 
out whether the labours are the contract employees of the principal employer are:- 


1) Whether the principal employer pays the salary instead of contractor and 
2) Whether the principal employer controls and supervise the work of the employees? 


16. The facts regarding the payment of salary by the management or contractor has not been stated in 
the claim petition of the workman. In fact, claim petition is totally silent regarding the payment of wages, 
salary, letter of appointment by respondent no.1 or by contractor or anything likewise. Thus, this basic feature 
for holding the relationship of employer and employee i.e. between respondent 10.1 and workman totally 
lacking not in the pleading but also in the evidence submitted by the workman.However, MW1 Colonel K P 
Singh in his cross examination has stated that it is responsibility of the contractor to pay monthly wages to the 
conservancy staff provided by him. Respondent 10.1 has nothing to do with the minimum wages, DC rate to 
the conservancy staff. Contractor is responsible to give notice to the conservancy staff provided by him. 
Respondent 10.1 has no roll in terminating the services of the workman. Thus, it was the respondent no.2 who 
was required to ensure the payment of wages to the workers engaged by him from time to time. 


17. It is also added here that respondent 10.1 has also placed on record documents Ex.R-1 to Ex.R-4. 
Ex.R-2 is the contract agreement between respondent 10.1 & 2. Thus, from the adduced evidence, it emerges 
that workman was paid salary by the contractor and he was being controlled and supervised by the contractor. 
Thus, there does not exist any relationship of employee and employer between the workman and respondent 
10.1. However, there exists relation of employee and employer between the workman and respondent 10.2. 


18. It is also added here that as per definition of contractor as given in the Section 2(c) of the Contract 
Labour (Regulation and Abolition) Act, 1970 (hereinafter called as Contract Act), respondent no.2 was 
supplying contract labour to the respondent 10.1. Thus, respondent 10.2 would fall under the definition of 
contractor. Section 2(c) of the Contract Act is reproduced as under: 


“Contractor”, in relation to an establishment, means a person who undertakes to 
produce a given result for the establishment, other than a mere supply of goods or 
articles of manufacture to such establishment, through contract labour or who supplies 
contract labour for any work of the establishment and includes a sub-contractor; 


19. TheHon’ble Apex Court in the case of Banglore Water Supply & Sewerage Board Vs. A. Rajappa 
1978(36) FLR 266 dealt at length with the ambit and scope of expression “industry” as defined in Section 2(j) 


of the Act, and has laid down triple test for determining whether a particular establishment is industry or not. 
The triple test is where (a) systematic activity (b) organized by cooperation between employer and employee 
(the direct and substantial element is commercial (c) for the production or distribution of goods and services to 
calculated to satisfy human wants and wishes prima facie is an “Industry” in that enterprise. The workman was 
doing work i.e. systematic activity for respondent 10.1 at the asking of respondent no.2 and there was 
cooperation between the workman and respondent no.2, which involve substantial commercial element for 
rendering service to satisfy human wants. Thus, respondent no.2 would be termed as industry. 


20. Workman in his deposition has clearly stated that there was non-compliance of Section 25F, 25G 
and 25H of the Act. He also stated that he was appointed on 04.09.2016 and he was paid minimum wages 
prescribed by Central Government and was working with respondent 10.1. He was not given any 
compensation before terminating his services on 27.10.2018. In his cross examination, he has further stated 
that his salary used to be credited in his bank account. But he has not placed on record any bank account, in 
which his salary was credited in order to enable this Court to see who was making payment to the workman. 
However, there is no denial that the workman was paid salary by respondent no.2 (contractor). However, from 


4076 


iii. 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 9, 2026/VAISAKHA 19, 1948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


the agreement dated 05.07.2018 (Ex.R-2) placed on file by respondent no.1, it emerges that safai karamcharis 
were provided by respondent no.2 (contractor) to work with respondent no.1. Thus, workman was employee of 
respondent 10.2 and not of respondent 10.1 as has been discussed above. There does not exist any relationship 
of employee and employer between the workman and respondent 10.1. However, there exists relation of 
employee and employer between the workman and respondent 10.2. 


21. Admittedly, there is non-compliance of Section 25F of the Act by respondent no.2. However, as 
regards violation of other provisions i.e. Section 25G and 25H of the Act, there is no evidence lead by the 
workman. Hence, workman is entitled for compensation from respondent 10.2. 


22. It is also added here that there is no iota of evidence that any unfair labour practice used by 
respondent 10.1 by entering into contract with contractors for supply of safai karamcharis and agreement 
executed between respondent 10.1 and 2 cannot be said to be any unfair labour practice. So far as contentions 
of AR for workman that the work performed by the workman is of perennial in nature and no domestic inquiry 
was held before terminating the services of workman are concerned, in this regard, it is pointed out that 
workman was entitled only for compensation under Section 25F of the Act by respondent no.2 (contractor) and 
no domestic inquiry was required to be conducted. Merely because work is of perennial in nature does not 
mean that any right has accrued in favour of the workman to get employment with respondent no.1, which is a 
department of Civil Defence run by Government of India and for that regular recruitment procedure is 
provided. Workman cannot seek backdoor entry into service. Moreover, workman is not employee of 
respondent 10.1 being employee of respondent 10.2 (contractor). 


23. In view of above discussion, it is held that since respondent no.2 (contractor) is currently not 
working with respondent no.1, so keeping in view the tenure of service rendered by the workman and salary 
given to him during the period of his stay with the contractor and non-compliance of Section 25F of the Act, 
this Tribunal is of the considered opinion that workman is entitled for compensation. 
24. Keeping in view the fact and circumstances of the present case, the following scheme of 
compensation is deemed fit by this Tribunal: 

Workman who worked for less than 2 years with contractor shall be entitled for Rs.20,000/-. 

Workman who worked for more than 2 years but less than 5 years with contractor shall be entitled 

Rs.30,000/-. 


Workman who worked for more than 5 years with contractors shall be entitled for Rs.50,000/-. 


25. Present workman employed on 04.09.2016 and was terminated on 27.10.2018 (more than 2 years 
but less than 5 years), so he is entitled for Rs.30,000/-. 
26. Respondent no.2 is directed to pay the workman Rs.30,000/- within one month from the date of the 


notification of the award failing which the respondent no.2 shall further pay interest @6% per annum from the 
date of award till the date of making payment. It is also added here that in case respondent no.1 requires safai 
karamcharis, then workman may be engaged through any contractor on sympathetic ground. 


27. Let copy of this award be sent to Central Government for publication as required under Section 17 
of the Act. 


KAMAL KANT, Presiding Officer 
नई दिल्‍ली, 30 अप्रैल, 2026
