the workman and there was no role of respondent 10.1 in appointing or terminating the services of the workman. 
Workman is employee of respondent no.2. If there is any violation of provision of the Act, then it is done by 
respondent no.2. As per Ex.R3, all dues were paid by the contractor with regard to EPF and ESI etc. The workman 
and other safai karamcharis formed union in the Headquarter, which is not permitted as per Ex.R4. Respondent no.1 
being the Government Agency, workman is trying to take benefit of his appointment by respondent 10.2 and has filed 
a false claim after concealing material facts and claim of the workman is not maintainable. 


12. I have given due consideration to the arguments advanced by AR for both the parties. 
Findings:- 
13. The first contention regarding the applicant is that whether applicant comes within the definition of 


workman as is defined in Section 2(s) of the Act. I may mention that applicant was appointed as sweeper as per his 
claim petition and affidavit submitted before the Tribunal. In plain words the applicant was performing his duties as 


4126 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 9, 2026/VAISAKHA 19, 1948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


labourer/unskilled worker. He was not in supervisory or administrative post requiring him to perform only 
administrative duties. While interpreting Section 2(s) Hon’ble Supreme Court in the case of Devinder Singh Vs. 


Municipal Council, Sanaur, AIR 2011 Supreme Court 2532,has observed as follows:- 


“The source of employment, the quantum of recruitment, the terms & conditions of employment/ contract of 
service, the quantum of wages/ pay and mode of payment are not at all relevant for deciding whether or not a 
person is a workman within the meaning of Section 2(s) of the Act. The definition of workman also does not 
make any distinction between full time and part time employee or a person appointed on contract basis. 
There is nothing in the plain language of Section 2(s) from which it can be inferred that only person 
employed on regular basis or a person employed for doing whole time job is a workman and the one 
employed on temporary, part time or contract basis on fixed wages or as a casual employee or for doing duty 
for fixed hours is not a workman.” 


14. Thus Hon’ble Supreme Court has clarified that the definition of workman also does not make any distinction 
between full time or part time employee or a person appointed on contract basis. There is nothing in plain language of 
Section 2(s) from which it can be infer that only person employed on regular basis or a person employed for doing 
whole time job is a workman and the one employed on temporary, part time or contract basis on fixed wages or as a 
casual employee or for doing duty for fixed hours is not a workman. In view of the ratio of law enunciated in the 
above ruling, in my considered opinion the applicant herein admittedly falls within the definition of ‘workman’ under 
Section 2(s) of the Act. 


15. The Hon’ble Supreme Court after analyzing the catena of cases has laid down in Balwant Raj Saluja Vs. Air 
India Limited in Civil Appeal No.10266 dated 25.08.2014, two well recognized tests to find out whether the labours 
are the contract employees of the principal employer are:- 


1) Whether the principal employer pays the salary instead of contractor and 
2) Whether the principal employer controls and supervise the work of the employees? 


16. The facts regarding the payment of salary by the management or contractor has not been stated in the claim 
petition of the workman. In fact, claim petition is totally silent regarding the payment of wages, salary, letter of 
appointment by respondent 10.1 or by contractor or anything likewise. Thus, this basic feature for holding the 
relationship of employer and employee i.e. between respondent 10.1 and workman totally lacking not in the pleading 
but also in the evidence submitted by the workman. However, MW1 Colonel K P Singh in his cross examination has 
stated that it is responsibility of the contractor to pay monthly wages to the conservancy staff provided by him. 
Respondent 10.1 has nothing to do with the minimum wages, DC rate to the conservancy staff. Contractor is 
responsible to give notice to the conservancy staff provided by him. Respondent 10.1 has no roll in terminating the 
services of the workman. Thus, it was the respondent no.2 who was required to ensure the payment of wages to the 
workers engaged by him from time to time. 


17. It is also added here that respondent 10.1 has also placed on record documents Ex.R-1 to Ex.R-4. Ex.R-2 is 
the contract agreement between respondent no.1 & 2. Thus, from the adduced evidence, it emerges that workman was 
paid salary by the contractor and he was being controlled and supervised by the contractor. Thus, there does not exist 
any relationship of employee and employer between the workman and respondent 10.1. However, there exists relation 
of employee and employer between the workman and respondent 10.2. 


18. It is also added here that as per definition of contractor as given in the Section 2(c) of the Contract Labour 
(Regulation and Abolition) Act, 1970 (hereinafter called as Contract Act), respondent no.2 was supplying contract 
labour to the respondent 10.1. Thus, respondent no.2 would fall under the definition of contractor. Section 2(c) of the 
Contract Act is reproduced as under: 


“Contractor”, in relation to an establishment, means a person who undertakes to produce a given result for the 
establishment, other than a mere supply of goods or articles of manufacture to such establishment, through contract 
labour or who supplies contract labour for any work of the establishment and includes a sub-contractor; 


19. TheHon’ble Apex Court in the case of Banglore Water Supply & Sewerage Board Vs. A. Rajappa 1978(36) 
FLR 266 dealt at length with the ambit and scope of expression “industry” as defined in Section 2(j) of the Act, and 


has laid down triple test for determining whether a particular establishment is industry or not. The triple test is where 
(a) systematic activity (b) organized by cooperation between employer and employee (the direct and substantial 
element is commercial (c) for the production or distribution of goods and services to calculated to satisfy human 
wants and wishes prima facie is an “Industry” in that enterprise. The workman was doing work i.e. systematic activity 
for respondent 10.1 at the asking of respondent 10.2 and there was cooperation between the workman and respondent 
no.2, which involve substantial commercial element for rendering service to satisfy human wants. Thus, respondent 
no.2 would be termed as industry. 


20. Workman in his deposition has clearly stated that there was non-compliance of Section 25F, 25G and 25H of 
the Act. He also stated that he was appointed on 21.10.2016 and he was paid minimum wages prescribed by Central 
Government and was working with respondent 10.1. He was not given any compensation before terminating his 


[भाग 1----खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 9, 2026/वैशाख 19, 1948 4127 


services on 27.10.2018. In his cross examination, he has further stated that his salary used to be credited in his bank 
account. But he has not placed on record any bank account, in which his salary was credited in order to enable this 
Court to see who was making payment to the workman. However, there is no denial that the workman was paid salary 
by respondent no.2 (contractor). However, from the agreement dated 05.07.2018 (Ex.R-2) placed on file by 
respondent no.1, it emerges that safai karamcharis were provided by respondent no.2 (contractor) to work with 
respondent no.1. Thus, workman was employee of respondent no.2 and not of respondent no.1 as has been discussed 
above. There does not exist any relationship of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.1. 
However, there exists relation of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.2. 


21. Admittedly, there is non-compliance of Section 25F of the Act by respondent no.2. However, as regards 
violation of other provisions i.e. Section 25G and 25H of the Act, there is no evidence lead by the workman. Hence, 
workman is entitled for compensation from respondent 10.2. 


22. It is also added here that there is no iota of evidence that any unfair labour practice used by respondent no.1 
by entering into contract with contractors for supply of safai karamcharis and agreement executed between 
respondent 10.1 and 2 cannot be said to be any unfair labour practice. So far as contentions of AR for workman that 
the work performed by the workman is of perennial in nature and no domestic inquiry was held before terminating 
the services of workman are concerned, in this regard, it is pointed out that workman was entitled only for 
compensation under Section 25F of the Act by respondent no.2 (contractor) and no domestic inquiry was required to 
be conducted. Merely because work is of perennial in nature does not mean that any right has accrued in favour of the 
workman to get employment with respondent no.1, which is a department of Civil Defence run by Government of 
India and for that regular recruitment procedure is provided. Workman cannot seek backdoor entry into service. 
Moreover, workman is not employee of respondent 10.1 being employee of respondent 10.2 (contractor). 


23. In view of above discussion, it is held that since respondent no.2 (contractor) is currently not working with 
respondent 10.1, so keeping in view the tenure of service rendered by the workman and salary given to him during the 
period of his stay with the contractor and non-compliance of Section 25F of the Act, this Tribunal is of the considered 
opinion that workman is entitled for compensation. 


24. Keeping in view the fact and circumstances of the present case, the following scheme of compensation is 
deemed fit by this Tribunal: 


1. Workman who worked for less than 2 years with contractor shall be entitled for Rs.20,000/-. 

il. Workman who worked for more than 2 years but less than 5 years with contractor shall be entitled 
Rs.30,000/-. 

ili. Workman who worked for more than 5 years with contractors shall be entitled for Rs.50,000/-. 

25. Present workman employed on 21.10.2016 and was terminated on 27.10.2018 (more than 2 years but less 


than 5 years), so he is entitled for Rs.30,000/-. 


26. Respondent no.2 is directed to pay the workman Rs.30,000/- within one month from the date of the 
notification of the award failing which the respondent 10.2 shall further pay interest @6% per annum from the date of 
award till the date of making payment. It is also added here that in case respondent 10.1 requires safai karamcharis, 
then workman may be engaged through any contractor on sympathetic ground. 


27. Let copy of this award be sent to Central Government for publication as required under Section 17 of the 
Act. 


KAMAL KANT, Presiding Officer 
नई दिल्‍ली, 4 मई, 2026 


का.आ. 791.--औद्योगिक विवाद अधिनियम, 1947 (1947 का 14) की धारा 17 के अनुसरण में केन्द्रीय सरकार 
प्रशासनिक कमांडर, स्टेशन मुख्यालय (नागरिक सुरक्षा), पंचकुला के प्रबंधतत्र, संबद्ध नियोजकों और उनके कर्मकारों के बीच 
अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट औद्योगिक विवाद में केन्द्रीय सरकार औद्योगिक अधिकरण श्रम न्यायालय चंडीगढ़- के 
पंचाट (154/2019) प्रकाशित करती है। 


[सं. एल- 12025/01/2026- आई आर (बी-1)-99] 
सलोनी, उप निदेशक 


4128 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 9, 2026/VAISAKHA 19, 1948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 
New Delhi, the 4th May, 2026 


S.O. 791.—In pursuance of Section 17 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central 
Government hereby publishes the Award (Ref. 154/2019) of the Cent.Govt.Indus.Tribunal-cum-Labour Court 
Chandigarh-II_ as shown in the Annexure, in the industrial dispute between the management of Admn. Commander 
Station Head Quarter ( Civil Defence), Panchkula and their workmen. 


[No. L-12025/01/2026- IR (B-I)-99] 
SALONI, Dy. Director 
ANNEXURE 


IN THE CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL-CUM-LABOUR COURT-II, 
CHANDIGARH 


(Present:- Mr. Kamal Kant, Presiding Officer). 
ID No. 154/2019 
Registered on:-16.10.2019 
Parmal S/o Sh. Lachman R/o Village Mangoli Jattan, Krukshetra, Haryana. 
-------- Applicant/Workman 
Versus 
1. Admn. Commander Station Head Quarter (Civil Defence), Chandi Mandir Cantt. Panchkula, Haryana. 
2. M/s Sun Rise Facilitator Pvt. Ltd., 2253/1 2704 Floor, Pipli Wala Town, Mani Mazra, Chandigarh. 


----Respondents 
Present:-Sh. Jang Bahadur, AR for workman. 
Sh. Satinder Singh, AR for respondent no.1. 
Respondent no.2 already ex parte. 
Award :05.02.2026 
1. Workman has filed the present claim petition under Section 2A of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 


(hereinafter called the Act) against his termination w.e.f. 27.10.2018 claiming reinstatement into service along with 
continuity of service and full back wages. 


2. It is maintained by the workman in the claim petition that that he was appointed as sweeper/safai karamchari 
on 05.10.2016 by respondent 10.1 at minimum wages prescribed by the Central Government. But the said minimum 
wages were never paid to him. Management has unlawfully deducted 12% EPF share, 1.75% ESI and Rs.300/- as 
TDS from hissalary despite of the fact that his total wages comes under the ambit of taxfree limit. He was working 
under respondent 10.1 directly, but respondent 10.1 in order to avoid its liability always shows his employment in 
record under different contractors, which tantamount to unfair labour practice. 


3. It is further maintained that workman including 200 other safai karamcharis of respondent no.1 raised the 
demand for non-deduction of TDS from the wages paid by respondent 10.1. However, their demand was not 
accepted. Other workers working under respondent 10.1 formed union and subsequently his services were dispensed 
with w.e.f. 27.10.2018. It is also maintained that respondent 10.1 retained juniors in service and also made fresh 
appointments after termination of his services and no compensation was paid under Section 25F of the Act. Thus, 
there is violation of Section 25F, 25G and 25H of the Act. Hence this petition. 


4. Notice of the petition was given to respondents. 


2: Respondent no.1 filed reply to the claim petition maintaining therein that the workman has not come to the 
Court with clean hands as he was appointed as sweeper by respondent no.2 (contractor) at minimum wage rate as 
prescribed by the Central Government. As per Government Policy dated 18.03.2011 (Ex.R-1), Non-Cantonment 
Military Stations have been authorized to outsource conservancy safaiwala. The answering respondent entered into 
contract with various private agencies for outsourcing of sweepers (safai karamcharis). In July 2018, the answering 
respondent entered into contract with M/s AA Foundation for safety and M/s Sunrise Facilitator Pvt. Ltd. through e- 
tendering for outsourcing of safai karamcharis to provide conservancy services in Chandi Mandir Military Station for 
the financial year 2018-19. Respondent no.2 submits bill for payment for the days the safai karamcharis performed 
their duties and answering respondent is only responsible to forward these bills to PCDA Western Command 
Chandigarh to release payment directly to the contractor’s account. Salary is paid to the workman by the contractor. 
Compliance of PF, ESI Rules etc. is carried out by the contractor as per contract agreement. Answering respondent 
has no responsibility of disbursement of wages and deduction of EPF and ESI etc. The workman and other safai 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 9, 2026/वैशाख 19, 1948 4129 


karamcharis formed union in the headquarter, which is not permitted and also carried out demonstration inside the 
premises of Station Headquarter on 27.10.2018 and failed to perform his duties. Thereafter, answering respondent 
asked respondent no.2 to ask the workers to perform their duties. Answering respondent has no role or responsibility 
for termination services of the workman. There is no relationship of employer and employee between the answering 
respondent and the workman. In view of Army Headquarter Letter, the civilian employees of the answering 
respondent cannot organize themselves into trade unions as station headquarter is an administrative office of the 
Government of India. In spite of that, workman along with other safai karamcharis formed union unauthorizedly and 
indulge in unlawful activities. It is maintained that workman is the employee of contractor and therefore, termination 
is the prerogative of the contractor. It is prayed that the claim be dismissed. 


6. Respondent no.2 also filed reply maintaining therein that workman worked with it w.e.f. 05.07.2018 to 
27.10.2018. Respondent no.2 has not terminated the services of workman. Workman was paid minimum wages as 
declared by the Centre Government from time to time. Workman himself left the job at his own will without notice. It 
is prayed that the claim may be dismissed. 


Evidence of workman:- 


7. In order to prove his case, workman examined himself as WW1 and tendered into evidence his affidavit 
WWI1/A and has been cross examined by the AR for respondent 10.1. AR for workman closed the evidence on 
30.07.2024. 


Evidence of respondent no.1:- 


8. Respondent 10.1 examined Colonel K P Singh S/o Late Colonel P. P. Singh, OIC, Civic Cell, Station Head 
Quarter, Chandi Mandir Panchkula as MW1, who tendered into evidence his affidavit ExMWI/A along with 
documents Ex.R1 to Ex.R4. AR for respondent 10.1 closed the evidence on 02.02.2026. 


Sr. No. Document Dated Exhibit 
1. Policy 18.03.2011 Ex.R-1 
2 Agreement with respondent no.2 05.07.2018 Ex.R-2 
3. Copy of ECR of EPFO Ex.R-3 
4 Letter of Head Quarter 22.04.1980 Ex.R-4 
9. It is pertinent to mention here that respondent 10.2 was proceeded ex parte on 20.04.2023. 


Submissions of workman:- 


10. While arguing the case, AR for workman contended that in this case, workman was appointed by respondent 
10.1 under a camouflage agreement with respondent 10.2. The workman was appointed directly by respondent 10.1 
on 05.10.2016 as sweeper and his services were dispensed with on 27.10.2018 without any reason in violation of 
Section 25F, 25G and 25H of the Act as no compensation was paid to the workman and junior to him were retained in 
service and fresh appointment were made after termination of the workman. So far as the agreements between the 
respondent 10.1 & 2 are concerned, they are totally not tenable and is unfair labour practice. The work performed by 
the workman is of perennial in nature. The contractor was not having any license. The contractor has left after three 
months. No domestic inquiry was held before terminating the service of the workman, which is mandatory under the 
Act. 


Submissions of respondent no.1:- 


11. On the other hand, AR for respondent 10.1 contended that the workman was engaged by respondent 10.2 
(contractor) as per agreement executed between respondent 10.1 & 2 on outsource basis and there is no relationship 
of employer and employee between respondent 10.1 and the workman. Wages were paid by the respondent 10.2 to 
the workman and there was no role of respondent 10.1 in appointing or terminating the services of the workman. 
Workman is employee of respondent no.2. If there is any violation of provision of the Act, then it is done by 
respondent no.2. As per Ex.R3, all dues were paid by the contractor with regard to EPF and ESI etc. The workman 
and other safai karamcharis formed union in the Headquarter, which is not permitted as per Ex.R4. Respondent no.1 
being the Government Agency, workman is trying to take benefit of his appointment by respondent no.2 and has filed 
a false claim after concealing material facts and claim of the workman is not maintainable. 


12. I have given due consideration to the arguments advanced by AR for both the parties. 
Findings:- 
13. The first contention regarding the applicant is that whether applicant comes within the definition of 


workman as is defined in Section 2(s) of the Act. I may mention that applicant was appointed as sweeper as per his 
claim petition and affidavit submitted before the Tribunal. In plain words the applicant was performing his duties as 


4130 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 9, 2026/VAISAKHA 19, 1948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


labourer/unskilled worker. He was not in supervisory or administrative post requiring him to perform only 
administrative duties. While interpreting Section 2(s) Hon’ble Supreme Court in the case of Devinder Singh Vs. 


Municipal Council, Sanaur, AIR 2011 Supreme Court 2532,has observed as follows:- 


“The source of employment, the quantum of recruitment, the terms & conditions of employment contract of 
service, the quantum of wages/ pay and mode of payment are not at all relevant for deciding whether or not a 
person is a workman within the meaning of Section 2(s) of the Act. The definition of workman also does not 
make any distinction between full time and part time employee or a person appointed on contract basis. 
There is nothing in the plain language of Section 2(s) from which it can be inferred that only person 
employed on regular basis or a person employed for doing whole time job is a workman and the one 
employed on temporary, part time or contract basis on fixed wages or as a casual employee or for doing duty 
for fixed hours is not a workman.” 


14. Thus Hon’ble Supreme Court has clarified that the definition of workman also does not make any distinction 
between full time or part time employee or a person appointed on contract basis. There is nothing in plain language of 
Section 2(s) from which it can be infer that only person employed on regular basis or a person employed for doing 
whole time job is a workman and the one employed on temporary, part time or contract basis on fixed wages or as a 
casual employee or for doing duty for fixed hours is not a workman. In view of the ratio of law enunciated in the 
above ruling, in my considered opinion the applicant herein admittedly falls within the definition of ‘workman’ under 
Section 2(s) of the Act. 


15. The Hon’ble Supreme Court after analyzing the catena of cases has laid down in Balwant Raj Saluja Vs. Air 
India Limited in Civil Appeal No.10266 dated 25.08.2014, two well recognized tests to find out whether the labours 
are the contract employees of the principal employer are:- 


1) Whether the principal employer pays the salary instead of contractor and 
2) Whether the principal employer controls and supervise the work of the employees? 


16. The facts regarding the payment of salary by the management or contractor has not been stated in the claim 
petition of the workman. In fact, claim petition is totally silent regarding the payment of wages, salary, letter of 
appointment by respondent 10.1 or by contractor or anything likewise. Thus, this basic feature for holding the 
relationship of employer and employee i.e. between respondent 10.1 and workman totally lacking not in the pleading 
but also in the evidence submitted by the workman.However, MW1 Colonel K P Singh in his cross examination has 
stated that it is responsibility of the contractor to pay monthly wages to the conservancy staff provided by him. 
Respondent 10.1 has nothing to do with the minimum wages, DC rate to the conservancy staff. Contractor is 
responsible to give notice to the conservancy staff provided by him. Respondent 10.1 has no roll in terminating the 
services of the workman. Thus, it was the respondent no.2 who was required to ensure the payment of wages to the 
workers engaged by him from time to time. 


17. It is also added here that respondent 10.1 has also placed on record documents Ex.R-1 to Ex.R-4. Ex.R-2 is 
the contract agreement between respondent no.1 & 2. Thus, from the adduced evidence, it emerges that workman was 
paid salary by the contractor and he was being controlled and supervised by the contractor. Thus, there does not exist 
any relationship of employee and employer between the workman and respondent 10.1. However, there exists relation 
of employee and employer between the workman and respondent 10.2. 


18. It is also added here that as per definition of contractor as given in the Section 2(c) of the Contract Labour 
(Regulation and Abolition) Act, 1970 (hereinafter called as Contract Act), respondent no.2 was supplying contract 
labour to the respondent 10.1. Thus, respondent no.2 would fall under the definition of contractor. Section 2(c) of the 
Contract Act is reproduced as under: 


“Contractor”, in relation to an establishment, means a person who undertakes to produce a given result for the 
establishment, other than a mere supply of goods or articles of manufacture to such establishment, through contract 
labour or who supplies contract labour for any work of the establishment and includes a sub-contractor; 


19. TheHon’ble Apex Court in the case of Banglore Water Supply & Sewerage Board Vs. A. Rajappa 1978(36) 
FLR 266 dealt at length with the ambit and scope of expression “industry” as defined in Section 2(j) of the Act, and 


has laid down triple test for determining whether a particular establishment is industry or not. The triple test is where 
(a) systematic activity (b) organized by cooperation between employer and employee (the direct and substantial 
element is commercial (c) for the production or distribution of goods and services to calculated to satisfy human 
wants and wishes prima facie is an “Industry” in that enterprise. The workman was doing work i.e. systematic activity 
for respondent 10.1 at the asking of respondent 10.2 and there was cooperation between the workman and respondent 
no.2, which involve substantial commercial element for rendering service to satisfy human wants. Thus, respondent 
no.2 would be termed as industry. 


20. Workman in his deposition has clearly stated that there was non-compliance of Section 25F, 25G and 25H of 
the Act. He also stated that he was appointed on 05.10.2016and he was paid minimum wages prescribed by Central 
Government and was working with respondent 10.1. He was not given any compensation before terminating his 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 9, 2026/वैशाख 19, 1948 4131 


services on 27.10.2018. In his cross examination, he has further stated that his salary used to be credited in his bank 
account. But he has not placed on record any bank account, in which his salary was credited in order to enable this 
Court to see who was making payment to the workman. However, there is no denial that the workman was paid salary 
by respondent no.2 (contractor). However, from the agreement dated 05.07.2018 (Ex.R-2) placed on file by 
respondent no.1, it emerges that safai karamcharis were provided by respondent no.2 (contractor) to work with 
respondent 10.1. Thus, workman was employee of respondent 10.2 and not of respondent 10.1 as has been discussed 
above. There does not exist any relationship of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.1. 
However, there exists relation of employee and employer between the workman and respondent 10.2. 


21. Admittedly, there is non-compliance of Section 25F of the Act by respondent no.2. However, as regards 
violation of other provisions i.e. Section 25G and 25H of the Act, there is no evidence lead by the workman. Hence, 
workman is entitled for compensation from respondent 10.2. 


22, It is also added here that there is no iota of evidence that any unfair labour practice used by respondent no.1 
by entering into contract with contractors for supply of safai karamcharis and agreement executed between 
respondent 10.1 and 2 cannot be said to be any unfair labour practice. So far as contentions of AR for workman that 
the work performed by the workman is of perennial in nature and no domestic inquiry was held before terminating 
the services of workman are concerned, in this regard, it is pointed out that workman was entitled only for 
compensation under Section 25F of the Act by respondent no.2 (contractor) and no domestic inquiry was required to 
be conducted. Merely because work is of perennial in nature does not mean that any right has accrued in favour of the 
workman to get employment with respondent no.1, which is a department of Civil Defence run by Government of 
India and for that regular recruitment procedure is provided. Workman cannot seek backdoor entry into service. 
Moreover, workman is not employee of respondent 10.1 being employee of respondent 10.2 (contractor). 


23. In view of above discussion, it is held that since respondent no.2 (contractor) is currently not working with 
respondent 10.1, so keeping in view the tenure of service rendered by the workman and salary given to him during the 
period of his stay with the contractor and non-compliance of Section 25F of the Act, this Tribunal is of the considered 
opinion that workman is entitled for compensation. 


24. Keeping in view the fact and circumstances of the present case, the following scheme of compensation is 
deemed fit by this Tribunal: 


i. Workman who worked for less than 2 years with contractor shall be entitled for Rs.20,000/-. 


ii. Workman who worked for more than 2 years but less than 5 years with contractor shall be entitled 
Rs.30,000/-. 


iii. Workman who worked for more than 5 years with contractors shall be entitled for Rs.50,000/-. 


25. Present workman employed on 05.10.2016 and was terminated on 27.10.2018 (more than 2 years and less 
than 5 years), so he is entitled for Rs.30,000/-. 


26. Respondent no.2 is directed to pay the workman Rs.30,000/- within one month from the date of the 
notification of the award failing which the respondent 10.2 shall further pay interest (06% per annum from the date of 
award till the date of making payment. It is also added here that in case respondent 10.1 requires safai karamcharis, 
then workman may be engaged through any contractor on sympathetic ground. 


27. Let copy of this award be sent to Central Government for publication as required under Section 17 of the 
Act. 


KAMAL KANT, Presiding Officer 


नई दिल्‍ली, 4 मई, 2026

प्रशासनिक कमांडर, स्टेशन मुख्यालय (नागरिक सुरक्षा), पंचकुला के प्रबंधतत्र, संबद्ध नियोजकां और उनके कर्मकारों के बीच 

अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट औद्योगिक विवाद में केन्द्रीय सरकार औद्योगिक अधिकरण श्रम न्यायालय चंडीगढ़- के 
पंचाट (164/2019) प्रकाशित करती है।

CHANDIGARH

ID No.164/2019 
Registered on:-16.10.2019 
Narender Singh S/o Sh. Tajpal, R/o Dharamsala Rampur Seuri Kalka Panchkula,Haryana. 
-------- Applicant/Workman 
Versus 
1. Admn. Commander Station Head Quarter (Civil Defence), Chandi Mandir Cantt. Panchkula, Haryana. 
2. M/s Sun Rise Facilitator Pvt. Ltd., 2253/1 2704 Floor, Pipli Wala Town, Mani Mazra, Chandigarh. 


----Respondents 
Present:- Sh. Jang Bahadur, AR for workman. 
Sh. Satinder Singh, AR for respondent no.1. 
Respondent no.2 already ex parte. 
Award : 04.02.2026 
1. Workman has filed the present claim petition under Section 2A of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 


(hereinafter called the Act) against his termination w.e.f. 27.10.2018 claiming reinstatement into service along with 
continuity of service and full back wages. 


2. It is maintained by the workman in the claim petition that that he was appointed as sweeper/safai karamchari 
on 05.10.2016 by respondent 10.1 at minimum wages prescribed by the Central Government. But the said minimum 
wages were never paid to him. Management has unlawfully deducted 12% EPF share, 1.75% ESI and Rs.300/- as 
TDS from hissalary despite of the fact that his total wages comes under the ambit of taxfree limit. He was working 
under respondent 10.1 directly, but respondent 10.1 in order to avoid its liability always shows his employment in 
record under different contractors, which tantamount to unfair labour practice. 


3. It is further maintained that workman including 200 other safai karamcharis of respondent no.1 raised the 
demand for non-deduction of TDS from the wages paid by respondent 10.1. However, their demand was not 
accepted. Other workers working under respondent 10.1 formed union and subsequently his services were dispensed 
with w.e.f. 27.10.2018. It is also maintained that respondent 10.1 retained juniors in service and also made fresh 
appointments after termination of his services and no compensation was paid under Section 25F of the Act. Thus, 
there is violation of Section 25F, 25G and 25H of the Act. Hence this petition. 


4. Notice of the petition was given to respondents. 


ae Respondent no.1 filed reply to the claim petition maintaining therein that the workman has not come to the 
Court with clean hands as he was appointed as sweeper by respondent no.2 (contractor) at minimum wage rate as 
prescribed by the Central Government. As per Government Policy dated 18.03.2011 (Ex.R-1), Non-Cantonment

the financial year 2018-19. Respondent no.2 submits bill for payment for the days the safai karamcharis performed 
their duties and answering respondent is only responsible to forward these bills to PCDA Western Command 
Chandigarh to release payment directly to the contractor’s account. Salary is paid to the workman by the contractor. 
Compliance of PF, ESI Rules etc. is carried out by the contractor as per contract agreement. Answering respondent 
has no responsibility of disbursement of wages and deduction of EPF and ESI etc. The workman and other safai 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 9, 2026/वैशाख 19, 1948 4133 


karamcharis formed union in the headquarter, which is not permitted and also carried out demonstration inside the 
premises of Station Headquarter on 27.10.2018 and failed to perform his duties. Thereafter, answering respondent 
asked respondent no.2 to ask the workers to perform their duties. Answering respondent has no role or responsibility 
for termination services of the workman. There is no relationship of employer and employee between the answering 
respondent and the workman. In view of Army Headquarter Letter, the civilian employees of the answering 
respondent cannot organize themselves into trade unions as station headquarter is an administrative office of the

indulge in unlawful activities. It is maintained that workman is the employee of contractor and therefore, termination 
is the prerogative of the contractor. It is prayed that the claim be dismissed. 


6. Respondent no.2 also filed reply maintaining therein that workman worked with it w.e.f. 05.07.2018 to 
27.10.2018. Respondent no.2 has not terminated the services of workman. Workman was paid minimum wages as 
declared by the Centre Government from time to time. Workman himself left the job at his own will without notice. It 
is prayed that the claim may be dismissed. 


Evidence of workman:- 


7. In order to prove his case, workman examined himself as WW1 and tendered into evidence his affidavit 
WWI/A and has been cross examined by the AR for respondent 10.1. AR for workman closed the evidence on 
30.07.2024. 


Evidence of respondent no.1:- 


8. Respondent 10.1 examined Colonel K P Singh S/o Late Colonel P. P. Singh, OIC, Civic Cell, Station Head 
Quarter, Chandi Mandir Panchkula as MW1, who tendered into evidence his affidavit ExMW1/A along with 
documents Ex.R1 to Ex.R4. AR for respondent 10.1 closed the evidence on 02.02.2026. 


Sr. No. Document Dated Exhibit 
1. Policy 18.03.2011 Ex.R-1 
2 Agreement with respondent no.2 05.07.2018 Ex.R-2 
3. Copy of ECR of EPFO Ex.R-3 
4 Letter of Head Quarter 22.04.1980 Ex.R-4 
9. It is pertinent to mention here that respondent no.2 was proceeded ex parte on 20.04.2023. 


Submissions of workman:- 


10. While arguing the case, AR for workman contended that in this case, workman was appointed by respondent 
10.1 under a camouflage agreement with respondent 10.2. The workman was appointed directly by respondent 10.1 
on 05.10.2016 as sweeper and his services were dispensed with on 27.10.2018 without any reason in violation of 
Section 25F, 25G and 25H of the Act as no compensation was paid to the workman and junior to him were retained in 
service and fresh appointment were made after termination of the workman. So far as the agreements between the 
respondent 10.1 & 2 are concerned, they are totally not tenable and is unfair labour practice. The work performed by 
the workman is of perennial in nature. The contractor was not having any license. The contractor has left after three 
months. No domestic inquiry was held before terminating the service of the workman, which is mandatory under the 
Act. 


Submissions of respondent no.1:- 


11. On the other hand, AR for respondent 10.1 contended that the workman was engaged by respondent 10.2 
(contractor) as per agreement executed between respondent 10.1 & 2 on outsource basis and there is no relationship 
of employer and employee between respondent 10.1 and the workman. Wages were paid by the respondent 10.2 to 
the workman and there was no role of respondent 10.1 in appointing or terminating the services of the workman. 
Workman is employee of respondent no.2. If there is any violation of provision of the Act, then it is done by 
respondent no.2. As per Ex.R3, all dues were paid by the contractor with regard to EPF and ESI etc. The workman 
and other safai karamcharis formed union in the Headquarter, which is not permitted as per Ex.R4. Respondent no.1 
being the Government Agency, workman is trying to take benefit of his appointment by respondent 10.2 and has filed 
a false claim after concealing material facts and claim of the workman is not maintainable. 


12. I have given due consideration to the arguments advanced by AR for both the parties. 


4134 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 9, 2026/VAISAKHA 19, 1948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


Findings:- 

13. The first contention regarding the applicant is that whether applicant comes within the definition of 
workman as is defined in Section 2(s) of the Act. I may mention that applicant was appointed as sweeper as per his 
claim petition and affidavit submitted before the Tribunal. In plain words the applicant was performing his duties as 
labourer/unskilled worker. He was not in supervisory or administrative post requiring him to perform only 
administrative duties. While interpreting Section 2(s) Hon’ble Supreme Court in the case of Devinder Singh Vs. 
Municipal Council, Sanaur, AIR 2011 Supreme Court 2532,has observed as follows:- 


“The source of employment, the quantum of recruitment, the terms & conditions of employment/ contract of 
service, the quantum of wages/ pay and mode of payment are not at all relevant for deciding whether or not a 
person is a workman within the meaning of Section 2(s) of the Act. The definition of workman also does not 
make any distinction between full time and part time employee or a person appointed on contract basis. 
There is nothing in the plain language of Section 2(s) from which it can be inferred that only person 
employed on regular basis or a person employed for doing whole time job is a workman and the one 
employed on temporary, part time or contract basis on fixed wages or as a casual employee or for doing duty 
for fixed hours is not a workman.” 


14. Thus Hon’ble Supreme Court has clarified that the definition of workman also does not make any distinction 
between full time or part time employee or a person appointed on contract basis. There is nothing in plain language of 
Section 2(s) from which it can be infer that only person employed on regular basis or a person employed for doing 
whole time job is a workman and the one employed on temporary, part time or contract basis on fixed wages or as a 
casual employee or for doing duty for fixed hours is not a workman. In view of the ratio of law enunciated in the 
above ruling, in my considered opinion the applicant herein admittedly falls within the definition of ‘workman’ under 
Section 2(s) of the Act. 


15. The Hon’ble Supreme Court after analyzing the catena of cases has laid down in Balwant Raj Saluja Vs. Air 
India Limited in Civil Appeal No.10266 dated 25.08.2014, two well recognized tests to find out whether the labours 
are the contract employees of the principal employer are:- 


1) Whether the principal employer pays the salary instead of contractor and 
2) Whether the principal employer controls and supervise the work of the employees? 


16. The facts regarding the payment of salary by the management or contractor has not been stated in the claim 
petition of the workman. In fact, claim petition is totally silent regarding the payment of wages, salary, letter of 
appointment by respondent 10.1 or by contractor or anything likewise. Thus, this basic feature for holding the 
relationship of employer and employee i.e. between respondent 10.1 and workman totally lacking not in the pleading 
but also in the evidence submitted by the workman.However, MW1 Colonel K P Singh in his cross examination has 
stated that it is responsibility of the contractor to pay monthly wages to the conservancy staff provided by him. 
Respondent 10.1 has nothing to do with the minimum wages, DC rate to the conservancy staff. Contractor is 
responsible to give notice to the conservancy staff provided by him. Respondent 10.1 has no roll in terminating the 
services of the workman. Thus, it was the respondent no.2 who was required to ensure the payment of wages to the 
workers engaged by him from time to time. 


17. It is also added here that respondent no.1 has also placed on record documents Ex.R-1 to Ex.R-4. Ex.R-2 is 
the contract agreement between respondent no.1 & 2. Thus, from the adduced evidence, it emerges that workman was 
paid salary by the contractor and he was being controlled and supervised by the contractor. Thus, there does not exist 
any relationship of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.1. However, there exists relation 
of employee and employer between the workman and respondent 10.2. 


18. It is also added here that as per definition of contractor as given in the Section 2(c) of the Contract Labour 
(Regulation and Abolition) Act, 1970 (hereinafter called as Contract Act), respondent no.2 was supplying contract 
labour to the respondent no.1. Thus, respondent no.2 would fall under the definition of contractor. Section 2(c) of the 
Contract Act is reproduced as under: 


“Contractor”, in relation to an establishment, means a person who undertakes to produce a given result for the 
establishment, other than a mere supply of goods or articles of manufacture to such establishment, through 
contract labour or who supplies contract labour for any work of the establishment and includes a sub- 
contractor; 


19. TheHon’ble Apex Court in the case of Banglore Water Supply & Sewerage Board Vs. A. Rajappa 1978(36) 


FLR 266 dealt at length with the ambit and scope of expression “industry” as defined in Section 2(j) of the Act, and 
has laid down triple test for determining whether a particular establishment is industry or not. The triple test is where 
(a) systematic activity (b) organized by cooperation between employer and employee (the direct and substantial 
element is commercial (c) for the production or distribution of goods and services to calculated to satisfy human 
wants and wishes prima facie is an “Industry” in that enterprise. The workman was doing work i.e. systematic activity 
for respondent 10.1 at the asking of respondent 10.2 and there was cooperation between the workman and respondent 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 9, 2026/वैशाख 19, 1948 4135 


10.2, which involve substantial commercial element for rendering service to satisfy human wants. Thus, respondent 
no.2 would be termed as industry. 


20. Workman in his deposition has clearly stated that there was non-compliance of Section 25F, 25G and 25H of 
the Act. He also stated that he was appointed on 05.10.2016 and he was paid minimum wages prescribed by Central 
Government and was working with respondent 10.1. He was not given any compensation before terminating his 
services on 27.10.2018. In his cross examination, he has further stated that his salary used to be credited in his bank 
account. But he has not placed on record any bank account, in which his salary was credited in order to enable this 
Court to see who was making payment to the workman. However, there is no denial that the workman was paid salary 
by respondent no.2 (contractor). However, from the agreement dated 05.07.2018 (Ex.R-2) placed on file by 
respondent no.1, it emerges that safai karamcharis were provided by respondent no.2 (contractor) to work with 
respondent no.1. Thus, workman was employee of respondent no.2 and not of respondent no.1 as has been discussed 
above. There does not exist any relationship of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.1. 
However, there exists relation of employee and employer between the workman and respondent 10.2. 


21. Admittedly, there is non-compliance of Section 25F of the Act by respondent no.2. However, as regards 
violation of other provisions i.e. Section 25G and 25H of the Act, there is no evidence lead by the workman. Hence, 
workman is entitled for compensation from respondent 10.2. 


22. It is also added here that there is no iota of evidence that any unfair labour practice used by respondent 10.1 
by entering into contract with contractors for supply of safai karamcharis and agreement executed between 
respondent 10.1 and 2 cannot be said to be any unfair labour practice. So far as contentions of AR for workman that 
the work performed by the workman is of perennial in nature and no domestic inquiry was held before terminating 
the services of workman are concerned, in this regard, it is pointed out that workman was entitled only for 
compensation under Section 25F of the Act by respondent no.2 (contractor) and no domestic inquiry was required to 
be conducted. Merely because work is of perennial in nature does not mean that any right has accrued in favour of the 
workman to get employment with respondent no.1, which is a department of Civil Defence run by Government of 
India and for that regular recruitment procedure is provided. Workman cannot seek backdoor entry into service. 
Moreover, workman is not employee of respondent 10.1 being employee of respondent 10.2 (contractor). 


23. In view of above discussion, it is held that since respondent no.2 (contractor) is currently not working with 
respondent 10.1, so keeping in view the tenure of service rendered by the workman and salary given to him during the 
period of his stay with the contractor and non-compliance of Section 25F of the Act, this Tribunal is of the considered 
opinion that workman is entitled for compensation. 


24. Keeping in view the fact and circumstances of the present case, the following scheme of compensation is 

deemed fit by this Tribunal: 

1. Workman who worked for less than 2 years with contractor shall be entitled for Rs.20,000/-. 

il. Workman who worked for more than 2 years but less than 5 years with contractor shall be entitled 
Rs.30,000/-. 

ili. Workman who worked for more than 5 years with contractors shall be entitled for Rs.50,000/-. 

25: Present workman employed on 05.10.2016 and was terminated on 27.10.2018 (more than 2 years and less 

than 5 years), so he is entitled for Rs.30,000/-. 

26. Respondent no.2 is directed to pay the workman Rs.30,000/- within one month from the date of the 


notification of the award failing which the respondent 10.2 shall further pay interest @6% per annum from the date of 
award till the date of making payment. It is also added here that in case respondent 10.1 requires safai karamcharis, 
then workman may be engaged through any contractor on sympathetic ground. 


27. Let copy of this award be sent to Central Government for publication as required under Section 17 of the 
Act. 


KAMAL KANT, Presiding Officer 
नई दिल्‍ली, 4 मई, 2026

AWARD. 


1. In the present case, a Reference was received from the appropriate Government vide letter no. L- 
420114/4/2022- (IR (DU)) dated 15/02/2022 under clause (d) of sub-section (1) and sub-section (2A) of section 10 of 
the I.D. Act. 1947 for adjudication of a dispute, terms of which are as under:- 


SCHEDULE 


“Whether the action of the management of NTPC Ltd., Barh, in not disclosing the ACRs rating to the 
workmen concerned as raised by NTPC Employees Union vide letter dated 19/09/2021 is proper, legal and 
justified? If not, whether the action of the management of NTPC Ltd. Barh in not promoting to Sh. Ajay 
Kumar Parjapati, General Secretary of NTPC Employees Union, Barh on the basis of the NTPC Promotion 
Policy to the next higher grade is proper, legal and justified? If not, to what relief the disputant union are 
entitled to and what other directions are necessary in the matter?” 


2. In the endorsement made in reference order, the appropriate Government directed the parties raising the 
dispute to file statement of claim, complete with relevant document, list of reliance and witness with this Tribunal 
within 15 days of receipt of the reference order and to forward a copy of such statement of claims to the opposite 
parties involved in the dispute. Accordingly statement of claim was filed by the workman. 


On perusal of the order dated 30/08/2025 passed by the Hon’ble Patna High Court in CWJC No. 7623 of 
2024 it is crystal clear that the Hon’ble Court have been pleased to set aside the Ref. Order dated 15/02/2022 issued 
by the Government of India, Ministry of Labour & Employment vide no. L-42011/4/2022(IR(DU)) and therefore the 
present Ref. case stands infructuous. The proceeding of this case is accordingly hereby dropped. 


Let a “No Dispute /claim” award be passed and sent to the appropriate Government for publication as required 
u/s 17 of the Industrial Dispute Act, 1947. 


SACHINDRA KUMAR PANDEY, Presiding Officer/Link Officer

The Petitioner has filed this petition u/s.2A of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 directly seeking relief of 
reinstatement into service of the Respondent with consequential benefits. 


25 As per the claim statement, the case of the Petitioner is that she joined as Data Entry and Scanning Operator 
in the O/o. the Postmaster, Srirangam in the year 2008. She was engaged as outsider against a clear vacant post. She 
worked continuously in the Head Office and Divisional Office as Data Entry and Scanning Operator for nine years 
and she was paid allowance as per rules in force. However, she was disengaged from her work during September, 2007 
without any order in writing. She stated that on earlier occasion, the Respondent department has absorbed similarly 
placed persons on completion of 240 days of work continuously in the case of Suguna & Others. The action of the 
Respondent/Management in removing the Petitioner from service without any order or notice amounts to violation of 
principles of natural justice u/s.25F of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947. Therefore, it is prayed that Respondent may 
be directed to reinstate the Petitioner into service with a direction to absorb her on regular basis with back wages and 
other attendant benefits. 


3. In the counter statement, the Respondent stated that Petitioner was engaged only as outsider not 
continuously but on intermittent spells. No order of appointment or engagement was issued to the Petitioner. The 
Petitioner was paid daily wages for the days she worked through money receipt. As per Circle Office letter dated 
23.12.1993 outsiders engaged after 11.02.1988 are not eligible for regularization, even though they have completed 
240 days service in any two years. Since the Department of Posts is not an industry, there is no violation of 
provisions of section 25F of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947. Therefore, the Petitioner is not entitled to any relief 
and the claim of the Petitioner is liable to be dismissed.

For the First Party Petitioner : WW1 Mrs. K.Saradha Devi 
For the 277 Party/Management : MW1 Smt. G.P. Josephine Silvia, 


Superintendent of Post Offices

4. The Petitioner has submitted proof of affidavit in support of her claim and adduced oral evidence as WW1 
and documents filed by her were marked as Ex.W1 to W15. The Respondent has also filed its proof of affidavit and 
Smt G.P Josephine, SPO was examined as MW1 and documents on behalf of respondent were marked as Ex.M1 to 
Ex.M4. 


5. Heard the learned counsel appearing for either side and perused the records. The point that arises for 
consideration is “whether the employee of the Respondent is entitled for reinstatement into service along with all 
benefits against the Respondent/Management? If not to what relief the concerned workman is entitled?” 


6. The Petitioner Mrs. K.Saradha Devi has stated in her claim statement that she had entered into service of the 
Respondent in the year 2008. The fact of continuous service is established by the Petitioner by adducing oral 
evidence as WW1 and filing proof of affidavit . 


7. The Respondent in its reply admitted this fact that Petitioner was engaged with the Department, but stated 
that engagement was on daily wage basis and not against any sanctioned post. The Petitioner was not a permanent 
employee and there was no employer-employee relationship exists between the Respondent and the Petitioner. The 
Respondent further stated that no appointment or engagement order was issued to the Petitioner and as per circular 
dated 01.11.1995 and 08.11.1995, casual labourers in employment upto 01.09.1993 were only eligible to be 
conferred with temporary status and the Petitioner was not working on 01.09.1993 with the Department and therefore, 
she is not entitled for any absorption. 


8. The learned counsel for the Respondent contended that Petitioner was not engaged through employment 
exchange and no appointment letter was issued. He further stated that assumption of the Petitioner that he/she works 
against the sanctioned post of Data Entry Operator is incorrect. The learned counsel further stated that there was no 
relationship of employer-employee between the Respondent and Petitioner, hence the question of notice or protection 
u/s.25F of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 does not arise. 


9. The Petitioner in the claim statement and in the cross examination stated that the work was performed for a period 
of more than eight hours per day continuously. The Respondent witness MW1 in her oral evidence nowhere stated 
that Petitioner did not perform the work about eight or more than 8 hours per day. 


10. The learned counsel appearing on behalf of the Appellant relied on the judgements of Hon'ble Supreme Court 
in Dharam Singh & Ors Vs. State of U.P & Another in Civil Appeal No.8558 of 2018 dated 31.01.2025 and in the 
case of Jaggo Vs. Union of India & Another in SLP(C) No.5580 of 2024 dated 20.12.2024. 


11. In the case of Dharam Singh Vs. State of U.P (supra), the Hon'ble Supreme Court has held as_ under: - 


“17. Before concluding, we think it necessary to recall that the State (here referring to both the Union and the State 
governments) is not a mere market participant but a constitutional employer. It cannot balance budgets on the backs of 
those who perform the most basic and recurring public functions. Where work recurs day after day and year after year, 
the establishment must reflect that reality in its sanctioned strength and engagement practices. The long-term 
extraction of regular labour under temporary labels corrodes confidence in public administration and offends the 
promise of equal protection. Financial stringency certainly has a place in public policy, but it is not a talisman that 
overrides fairness, reason and the duty to organise work on lawful lines. 


18. Moreover, it must necessarily be noted that "ad-hocism" thrives where administration is opaque. The State 
Departments must keep and produce accurate establishment registers, muster rolls and outsourcing arrangements, and 
they must explain, with evidence. why they prefer precarious engagement over sanctioned posts where the work is 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 9, 2026/वैशाख 19, 1948 4141 


perennial. If "constraint" is invoked, the record should show what alternatives were considered, why similarly placed 
workers were treated differently, and how the chosen course aligns with Articles 14, 16 and 21 of the Constitution of 
India. Sensitivity to the human consequences of prolonged insecurity is not sentimentality. It is a constitutional 
discipline that should inform every decision affecting those who keep public offices running.” 


12. In the case of Jaggo Vs. Union of India in SLP No.5580 of 2024 (2024) SCC online 3826 and Shripal & 
Another Vs. Nagar Nigam, Ghaziabad (2025) SCC online 221, the Hon'ble Supreme Court has held that long-term 
“ad hocism’”, is denial of basic parity. The Hon'ble Supreme Court denied the contention of the Respondent that daily 
wagers and temporary employees cannot claim permanent absorption in the absence of statutory Rules. The Hon’ble 
Court further stated that the alleged asserted ban on fresh recruitment, though real, cannot justify indefinite daily-wage 
status or continued unfair practices. 


13. In the present case, the Petitioner has continuously rendered services over several years. The Hon'ble 
Supreme Court has held in the case of Shripal & Another (supra) that Indian Labour Law is strongly disfavors 
perpetual daily-wage or contractual engagements in circumstances, where the work is permanent in nature. 


14. Further, the Hon'ble Supreme Court in the case of Jaggo Vs. Union of India (supra) has observed as under:- 


"22. The pervasive misuse of temporary employment contracts, as exemplified in this case, reflects a broader systemic 
issue that adversely affects workers’ rights and job security. In the private sector, the rise of the gig economy has led to 
an increase in precarious employment arrangements, often characterized by lack of benefits, job security, workers and 
and fair treatment. Such practices have been criticized for exploiting undermining labour standards. Government 
institutions, entrusted with upholding the principles of fairness and justice, bear an even greater responsibility to avoid 
such exploitative employment practices. When public sector entities engage in misuse of temporary contracts, it not 
only mirrors the detrimental trends observed in the gig economy but also sets a concerning precedent that can erode 
public trust in governmental operations. 


25. It is a disconcerting reality that temporary employees, particularly in government institutions, often face 
multifaceted forms of exploitation. While the foundational purpose of temporary contracts may have been to address 
short-term or seasonal needs, they have increasingly become a mechanism to evade long-term obligations owed 
employees. These practices manifest in several ways: 


Misuse of "Temporary" Labels: Employees engaged for work that is essential, recurring, and integral to the 
functioning of an institution are often labelled as "temporary" or "contractual," even when their roles mirror those of 
regular employees. Such misclassification deprives workers of the dignity, security. and benefits that regular 
employees are entitled to, despite performing identical tasks. 


Arbitrary Temporary Termination: employees are frequently dismissed without cause or notice, as seen in the present 
case. This practice undermines the principles of natural justice and subjects workers to a state of constant insecurity, 
regardless of the quality or duration of their service. 


Lack of Career Progression: Temporary employees often find themselves excluded from opportunities for skill 
development, promotions, or incremental pay raises. They remain stagnant in their roles, creating a systemic disparity 
between them and their regular counterparts, despite their contributions being equally significant. 


Using Outsourcing as Institutions Shield: to increasingly resort outsourcing roles performed by temporary employees, 
effectively replacing one set of exploited workers with another. This practice not only perpetuates exploitation but also 
demonstrates a deliberate effort to bypass the obligation to offer regular employment. 


Denial of Basic Rights and Benefits: Temporary employees are often denied fundamental benefits such as pension, 
provident fund, health insurance, and paid leave, even when their tenure spans decades. This lack of social security 
subjects them and their families to undue hardship, especially in cases of illness, retirement, or unforeseen 
circumstances." 


15. The learned counsel for the Respondent argued that no appointment order was issued to the Petitioner, and thus 
no permanent status is allowable to the Petitioner. But the non-issuance of appointment order does not erase 116 
entitlement of the Petitioner since the Petitioner has worked for several years and not days. 


AWARD 


16. In view of the foregoing discussion, the claim petition filed by the Petitioner is liable to be allowed with the 
following directions:- 


The Petitioner’s service shall stand regularized with effect from the date of removal/denial from service and the 
Petitioner shall be reinstated into service within a period of three months with 50% back wages and consequential 
& other attendant benefits. 


4142 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 9, 2026/VAISAKHA 19, 1948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 
The reference is answered accordingly. 
Record be consigned to the record room. 
Place : Chennai 
Date : 23.03.2026 
SUSHIL KUMAR-I, Presiding Officer

सरकार प्रबंधन, बीएसएनएल, पटना के प्रबंधतंत्र के संबद्ध नियोजकों और उनके कर्मचारियों के बीच अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट

का.आ. 799.--औद्योगिक विवाद अधिनियम, 1947 (1947 का 14) की धारा 17 के अनुसरण में, केन्द्रीय 
सरकार महाप्रबंधक, मद्रास फर्टिलाइजर्स लिमिटेड, मनाली, चेन्नई के प्रबंधतंत्र के संबद्ध नियोजकों और महासचिव, 
मद्रास फर्टिलाइजर्स एम्प्लॉई प्रोग्रेसिव यूनियन कर्मचारी के बीच अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट केन्द्रीय सरकार औद्योगिक अधिकरण- 
सह- श्रम न्यायालय, चेन्नई पंचाट (संदर्भ संखया-63/2024) को जैसा कि अनुलग्रक में दिखाया गया है, प्रकाशित करती है। 


[सं. एल- 42025/07/2026/100-आईआर (डीयू)] 
सलोनी, उप निदेशक 


New Delhi, the 4th May, 2026 


99.0. 799.—In pursuance of Section 17 of the Industrial Dispute Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central 
Government hereby publishes the award (Ref. No. 63/2024) of the Central Government Industrial Tribunal cum 
Labour Court, Chennai as shown in the Annexure, in the Industrial dispute between the employers in relation 
to The General Manager, Madras Fertilizers Ltd., Manali, Chennai and The General Secretary, Madras 
Fertilizers Employees Progressive Union, workman. 


[No. L-42025/07/2026/100-IR (DU)] 


SALONI, Dy. Director 


4146 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 9, 2026/VAISAKHA 19, 1948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 
ANNEXURE 
BEFORE THE CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL 
TRIBUNAL- CUM - LABOUR COURT, CHENNAI 
Date:19-03-2026 
Present: SUSHIL KUMAR-II, 
PRESIDING OFFICER 
INDUSTRIAL DISPUTE. No.63/2024 
BETWEEN 
The General Secretary, 
Madras Fertilizers Employees 
Progressive Union, 
No.24A, North Mada Street, 
Thiruvottiyur, 
Chennai-600 019. : 1“ Party/Petitioner 
AND 

The General Manager, 

Madras Fertilizers Ltd 

Post Bag No.2, Manali, 

Chennai-600 068. : 2" Party/Respondent 
Appearance: 
For the 17 Party/ Petitioner हि M/s. R.Pandian, Advocates 
For the 27 Party/ Respondent : None 


The Central Government, Ministry of Labour & Employment, O/o. Deputy Chief Labour Commissioner 
(Central), Chennai vide its order No.8(37)/2024/B4/300069814/ADJ/A1 dated 11-09-2024 referred the following 
Industrial Dispute to this Tribunal for adjudication:- 


2. The schedule mentioned in that order is: 


“Whether the demand of the Madras Fertilizers Employees Progressive Union to consider November, 
2019 as the date of qualification for Mrs.G.Preethi’s Grade-V promotion is legal and justified? If not, to 
what relief the Madras Fertilizers Employees Union is entitled to?” 


3. Brief facts are the case are as under:- 


The Petitioner Union espouse the cause of one of the members of the Petitioner/Union namely Mrs. G. Preethi (Emp. 
No. 3285), who joined the Respondent Organisation on 03.02.2016 as Technical Assistant-Operations (Grade-IV). 
While working as such, she obtained prior approval from the management of MFL for pursuing B.Tech Degree - 
Chemical Engineering (Part Time) and joined Sathya Bhama University in July 2016 and completed her course of 
study in November 2019 (in the Batch -2016-2019) when all the examinations were completed and results were 
declared. It is submitted that though the course of study was completed in November 2019, she was declared to have 
passed in "First Class With Distinction" in the examination held during November 2019 as per the testimonials issued 
to her as 14.02.2020. Thus, the Respondent/Management not considered her for promotion to Grade-V as on 
03.02.2020. 


4. It is further submitted that the said University was unable to conduct Convocation in the earlier part of 2020 
due to lockdown restrictions imposed by the Government of India and followed up by the State of Tamil Nadu. Due 
to the said lockdown restrictions, Mrs.G.Preethi was unable to approach the University and secure the Certificates and 
other testimonials at the appropriate time and it resulted in some delay in submitting the Certificates to the 
Respondent/Management for making entry in the Personnel Folder of the said employee. It is submitted that the said 
employee produced all relevant documents and certificates on 26.08.2020 in the HR Department of the Respondent 
Organisation and caused entry to be made in the relevant registers. It is submitted that the said employee completed 
four years of service in Grade-IV on 03.02.2020 and has also completed her course of study in B.Tech during 
November 2019 and as such, as per the Non-Supervisory Promotion Policy (NSPP) as amended by 18(1) Settlement 
dated 20.09.2018 she is entitled for promotion to Grade-V (time bound promotion), since the NSPP stipulates the 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 9, 2026/वैशाख 19, 1948 4147 


eligibility criteria for promotion to Grade-V as "four years in Grade-IV for Professional Qualification and as 
mentioned in Annexure -2". However, the said employee was not considered for promotion to Grade-V, despite she 
is eligible for the same as she has completed her B.Tech Degree in November 2019 itself, whereas, the selection cut 
of date was fixed as 03.02.2020 stating that the date of issue of the certificate was 14.02.2020 which was 11 days later 
to the cut of/closing date for consideration for promotion to the post of Grade-V, the said assumption of the 
management is legally untenable. Mentioning all the above facts, Mrs.G.Preethi submitted another representation 
dated 26.08.2020 enclosing the copies of her consolidated mark sheet and convocation Certificate in order to avail PQ 
relaxation for promotion to Grade-V and also for sanction of two increments for acquiring B.Tech (Chemical 
Engineering), but was not considered for promotion to Grade-V. 


3: After a delay of more than three years, the HR Department vide letter dated 29.11.2023 informed Mrs. 
G.Preethi about her promotion as Senior Technical Assistant-Operations Gr.V on time bound basis w.e.f 03.02.2021 
as per Non-Supervisory Promotion Policy applicable to "others having minimum qualification" with 5 years of service 
in Grade-IV. Once again Mrs. G.Preethi vide letter dated 22.01.2024 represented her case to HR Department 
justifying her entitlement for promotion to Grade-V as per PQ norms (4 years service in Grade-IV as on the date of 
consideration for promotion i.e., 03.02.2020), as she has already acquired the Engineering Degree in November 2019 
itself. The Petitioner/Union also had taken up the issue of Mrs. G.Preethi's candidature for the post of Senior 
Technical Assistant-Operations (Grade-V), vide its letter dated 26.02.2024 as the Management had not complied with 
the conditions in the Non-Supervisory Promotion Policy-NSPP [vide 18(1) settlement dated 20.09.2018] and 
requested the Management to reconsider her for promotion under PQ on 03.02.2020 as Mrs.G.Preethi attaining the 
Qualification of B.Tech Degree during November 2019 itself. 


6. The Management replied vide letter dated 29.2.24 that Mrs.G.Preethi comes under "Other Qualification" (OQ) and 
that she had not completed 5 years of service in Grade-IV for consideration for promotion to Gr.V. The 
Petitioner/Union strongly objected to the interpretation of Management vide another letter dated 07.03.2024 and that 
their member Mrs.G.Preethi completed her Degree in November 2019 and the same was mentioned in degree 
certificate in Bold font. 


7. But the management reiterated their contention that the employee Mrs.G.Preethi has completed her degree 
only on 14.2.2020, wrongly taking in to consideration the date on which the certificate was generated and 
misconstruing the same as date of acquiring the Degree. The above contention of the Management is unsustainable. 
Hence, the Petitioner/Union raised this dispute to direct the Respondent management to promote Mrs. G.Preethi to 
the post of Senior Technical Assistant Grade-V as on 03.02.2020 under PQ norms. 


Witnesses Examined: 


For the First Party Petitioner : WW1R.Valampuri John 
For the 277 Party/Management : Nil 


Documents Marked: 


On the Petitioner’s side:

Ex.W1 | 20.09.2018 Memorandum of Settlement (Non-supervisory Promotion 
Policy)

Ex.W3_ | 26.08.2020 Representation of Smt. G.Preethi along with B.Tech 
certificate 29.11.2023 


Ex.W4_ | 29.11.2023 File HR/PF 3285/2023 Time bound promotion from Gr.IV 
to Gr.V 


Ex.W5 | 22.01.2024 Representation of Smt. G.Preethi 
Ex.W6_ | 06.02.2024 Reply by Respondent to G.Preethi 
Ex.W7 | 19.02.2024 Reply by Respondent to Petitioner union 


Ex.W8 | 30.05.2024 Letter from Petitioner union raised ID before conciliation 
officer 


Ex.W9 | 06.08.2024 Reply submitted by Respondent 
Ex.W10 | 30.08.2024 Failure report of ALC(C)-II 
Ex.W11 | 11.09.2024 Order of reference by Ministry 


4148 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 9, 2026/VAISAKHA 19, 1948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


Documents Marked: 


_On behalf of Respondent/Management : Nil 


8. On behalf of the Respondent none appeared and they havenot filed any Counter Statement and therefore, 
the Respondent was set ex-parte. Heard the learned counsel appearing on behalf of the Petitioner Union and perused 
the records. The point that arises for determination is whether the demand of the Petitioner union to consider 
November, 2019 as date of qualification for Mrs.G.Preethi? If so, to what relief the Petitioner union is entitled? 


9. On behalf of the Petitioner union one Shri R.Valampuri John, General Secretary of the Union has adduced 
oral evidence as WW 1 and as none appeared on behalf of Respondent, cross examination was closed. On behalf of 
the Petitioner Union documents were marked as Ex.W1 to W11. Ex.W1 is the copy of memorandum of settlement 
for Non-Supervisory Promotion Policy. Ex.W2 & W3 are representation given by Smt.G.Preeti. Ex.W4 is 
promotion letter from Respondent to Smt. G.Preeti from Gr.IV to Gr.V. Ex.W5 18 representation given by 
Smt.G.Preeti. Ex.W6 is reply given by respondent. Ex.W7 is reply given by Respondent to Petitioner Union. Ex.W8 
to १४11 are pertaining to conciliation documents. 


10. On perusal of record from Ex.W3 placed at page 16 of the paper book is a B.Tech in Chemical 
Engineering (Part Time) Certificate issued to Mrs.Preeti.G, it is a fact evident from the record that for the 
examination held in November, 2019, certificate was issued on February,14, 2020. Therefore, Mrs. Preeti.G has 
fulfilled the eligibility criteria of B.Tech degree w.e.f. 14.02.2020 and on that date only she becomes qualified. 
Therefore, November, 2019 cannot be considered as date of qualification for Mrs.G.Preethi. Therefore, the demand 
of the Petitioner/Union to direct the Respondent management to promote Mrs. G.Preethi to the post of Senior 
Technical Assistant Grade-V as on 03.02.2020 under PQ norms is not just and fair. Thus, The appeal filed by the 
Petitioner Union is liable for dismissal. 


11. Further, the Petitioner had already approached the Hon'ble High Court with a Writ Petition numbered as 
W.P.No.20866 of 2019 (WMP 20072 of 2019) & WP 14028 of 2020 for the same cause of action and the same is 
not brought to the notice of this Tribunal on record. In this regard, in the case of Ram Sumer Puri Mahant vs State of 
U.P. And Ors. reported in (1985) 1 SCC 427, Hon'ble Supreme Court held that: "Multiplicity of litigation is not in the 
interest of the parties nor should public time be allowed to be wasted over meaningless litigation. We are, therefore, 
satisfied that parallel proceedings should not continue and the order of the learned Magistrate should be quashed." 
For the above reasons also, the petition filed by the Petitioner Union is not maintainable and thus, same is liable to 
be dismissed. 


AWARD 


The Industrial dispute raised by the Petitioner Union is liable for dismissal. The same is dismissed 
accordingly. 


Record be consigned to the record room. 
Place : Chennai 
Date: 19-03-2026 
SUSHIL KUMAR-I, Presiding Officer 


नई दिल्‍ली, 4 मई, 2026

Award 


The Central Government, Ministry of Labour& Employment vide its Order No. L-42012/51/2017-IR(DU), 
dated 24.10.2017 referred the following Industrial Dispute to this Tribunal for adjudication. 


The schedule mentioned in that order is: 


“Whether the termination of the services as Driver of Sh. Erusappan by M/s Kongu Transport, the 
contractor of Nuclear Power Corporation Ltd., Madras, Atomic Power Station, Kalpakkam without any 
notice is justified? If not, what relief is the workman entitled to?” 


On receipt of the above reference dated 24.10.2017 from the appropriate Government, the dispute was 
registered as ID No. 95/2017, and due notices were issued to both the parties for their appearance and file statements. 
The claim petition was filed on 03.06.2019, and the counter (by Respondent No.1) was filed on 17.09.2019. No 
counter statement has been filed by Respondent No.2 till 19.11.2019 hence Respondent No.2 was set ex parte on 
19.11.2019. 


The case was listed for filing of the Proof Affidavit by the petitioner up to 19.11.2019 and subsequent dates 
till 02.08.2022. No proof affidavit filed was filed by the petitioner thus the petitioner case was closed on 02.08.2022 
and listed for filing of Proof Affidavit if any by the Respondent No.1. No Proof Affidavit was filed by the 
Respondent No.1! either. 


On going through the order sheets as discussed above, it is crystal clear that despite of sufficient 
opportunities though made in favour of the Petitioner, there has been no progress in the proceeding was noticed for a 
substantial period almost approaching 4 years. Since the Petitioner chose not to file any affidavit evidence for his or 
any of the witness and hereby withheld to come to dock to prove his case. This necessarily seeks the Petitioner has 
got no interest to proceed with the case. Accordingly, the case was reserved for Final Order as liable for dismissal for 
default. 


In the fact and circumstance, it is held that that there exists, no dispute for adjudication in consonance to the 
reference. 


In the result, the case stands dismissed for default. 
An Award is passed accordingly. 
SUSHIL KUMAR-II, Presiding Officer

का.आ. 804.--औद्योगिक विवाद अधिनियम, 1947 (1947 का 14) की धारा 17 के अनुसरण में, केन्द्रीय 
सरकार एचपीसीएल के प्रबंधतंत्र के संबद्ध नियोजकों और हिंदुस्तान पेट्रोलियम एम्प्लाइज यूनियन; पेट्रोलियम एम्प्लाइज 
यूनियन के बीच अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट केन्द्रीय सरकार औद्योगिक अधिकरण एवं श्रम न्‍न्यायालय-1, मुंबई, पंचाट (रिफरेन्स 
न.- 10/2014) को जैसा कि अनुलग्रक में दिखाया गया है, प्रकाशित करती है जो केन्द्रीय सरकार को सॉफ्ट कॉपी के साथ
