AWARD 


The present reference has been made by the Central Government by its order No. L-3001 1/48/2013(IR(M)) 
dated 19/02/2014 passed in exercise of the powers conferred by clause (d) of section (1) read with sub-section (2A) of 
Section 10 of the Industrial Dispute Act 1947. The terms of reference as per the schedule to the said order are as 
under: 


“Whether the dispute raised by Air India Aircraft Engineering Association, Mumbai against the management 
of Air India Engineering Services Ltd. over implementation of revised pay structure of Aircraft Maintenance 
Engineers, Claiming that it is anomalous and discriminatory justified and legal? If yes, to what relief the 
concerned Air India Aircraft Maintenance Engineer are entitled to ?” 


In view of the above said facts the claimant/workman has not field any evidence in support of his case on affidavit, in 
spite of several opportunities given to him and taking into consideration the law as laid by the Hon'ble High Court in 
the case of V. K. Raj Industries v. Labour Court (1) and others 1981 (29) FLR 194 as under: 


“It is well settled that if a party challenges the legality of an order, the burden lies upon him to prove illegality of the 
order and if no evidence is produced the party invoking jurisdiction of the Court must fail. Whenever a workman 
raises a dispute challenging the validity of the termination of service if is imperative for him to file written statement 
before the Industrial Court setting out grounds on which the order is challenged and he must also produce evidence 
to prove his case. If the workman fails to appear or to file written statement or produce evidence, the dispute referred 
by the State Government cannot be answered in favour of the workman and he would not be entitled to any relief.” 


In the case of M/s Uptron Powertronics Employees’ Union, Ghaziabad through its Secretary v. Presiding Officer, 
Labour Court (I), Ghaziabad and others 2008 (118) FLR 1164 Hon’ble Allahabad High Court has held as under: 


“The law has been settled by the Apex Court in case of Shanker Chakravarti v. Britannia Biscuit Co. Ltd., V.K. Raj 
Industries v. Labour Court and Ors., Airtech Private Limited v. State of U.P. and Ors. 1984 (49) FLR 38 and 
Meritech India Ltd. v. State of U.P. and Ors. 1996 FLR that in the absence of any evidence led by or on behalf of the 
workman the reference is bound to be answered by the court against the workman. In such a situation it is not 
necessary for the employers to lead any evidence at all. The obligation to lead evidence to establish an allegation 
made by a party is on the party making the allegation. The test would be, who would fail ifno evidence is led.” 


And by the Hon’ble Allahabad High Court in the case of District Administrative Committee, U.P. P.A.C.C.S.C. 
Services v. Secretary-cum-G.M. District Co-operative Bank Ltd. 2010 (126) FLR 519; wherein it has been held as 
under: 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(1)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 9, 2026/वैशाख 19, 1948 4155 


“The submission is that even if the petitioner failed to lead the evidence, burden was on the shoulders of the 
respondent to prove the termination order as illegal. He was required to lead evidence first which he failed. A 
perusal of the impugned award also does not show that any evidence either oral or documentary was led by the 
respondent. In the case of no evidence, the reference has to be dismissed.” 


Since not field any evidence in support of his case on affidavit, has been filed by the union in support of his case, the 
claim is liable to be dismissed. 


For the foregoing reasons, the case is dismissed and the union is not entitled for any relief. 
Accordingly, the reference stands answered in terms aforesaid. 
Award as above. 


Justice ARVIND KUMAR MISHRA, Presiding Officer 


नई दिल्‍ली, 5 मई, 2026 


का.आ. 805.---औद्योगिक विवाद अधिनियम, 1947 (1947 का 14) की धारा 17 के अनुसरण में केन्द्रीय सरकार 
प्रशासनिक कमांडर, स्टेशन मुख्यालय (नागरिक सुरक्षा), पंचकुला के प्रबंधतत्र, संबद्ध नियोजकों और उनके कर्मकारों के बीच 


अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट औद्योगिक विवाद में केन्द्रीय सरकार औद्योगिक अधिकरण श्रम न्यायालय चंडीगढ़-ा के 
Gare (52/2024) प्रकाशित करती है | 


[सं. एल- 12025/01/2026-आईआर (बी-)-101] 
सलोनी, उप निदेशक 
New Delhi, the 5th May, 2026 


S.O. 805.—In pursuance of Section 17 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central 
Government hereby publishes the Award (Ref. 52/2024) of the Cent.Govt.Indus.Tribunal-cum-Labour Court 
Chandigarh-I_ as shown in the Annexure, in the industrial dispute between the management of Admn. Commander 
Station Head Quarter ( Civil Defence), Panchkula _ and their workmen. 


[No. L-12025/01/2026- IR (B-I)-101] 
SALONI, Dy. Director 
ANNEXURE 


IN THE CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL-CUM-LABOUR COURT-I, 
CHANDIGARH 


(Present:- Mr. Kamal Kant, PO-cum-Link Officer). 
ID No. 52/2024 
Registered on:-16.10.2019 
Suresh Kumar S/o Sh. Raj Kumar R/o Village Tikra Post Office Rjera Tehsil Chamba, Himachal Pradesh. 


Versus 

1. Admn. Commander Station Head Quarter (Civil Defence), Chandi Mandir Cantt. Panchkula, Haryana. 
2. M/s Sun Rise Facilitator Pvt. Ltd., 2253/1 one Floor, Pipli Wala Town, Mani Mazra, Chandigarh. 
----Respondents 
Present:-Sh. Jang Bahadur, AR for workman. 
Sh. Satinder Singh, AR for respondent no.1. 
Respondent no.2 already ex parte. 

Award :10.03.2026 


1. Workman has filed the present claim petition under Section 2A of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 
(hereinafter called the Act) against his termination w.e.f. 27.10.2018 claiming reinstatement into service along with 
continuity of service and full back wages. 


4156 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 9, 2026/VAISAKHA 19, 1948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


2. It is maintained by the workman in the claim petition that that he was appointed as sweeper/safai karamchari 
on 14.10.2015 by respondent 10.1 at minimum wages prescribed by the Central Government. But the said minimum 
wages were never paid to him. Management has unlawfully deducted 12% EPF share, 1.75% ESI and Rs.300/- as 
TDS from hissalary despite of the fact that his total wages comes under the ambit of taxfree limit. He was working 
under respondent 10.1 directly, but respondent 10.1 in order to avoid its liability always shows his employment in 
record under different contractors, which tantamount to unfair labour practice. 


3: It is further maintained that workman including 200 other safai karamcharis of respondent 10.1 raised the 
demand for non-deduction of TDS from the wages paid by respondent 10.1. However, their demand was not 
accepted. Other workers working under respondent 10.1 formed union and subsequently his services were dispensed 
with w.e.f. 27.10.2018. It is also maintained that respondent 10.1 retained juniors in service and also made fresh 
appointments after termination of his services and no compensation was paid under Section 25F of the Act. Thus, 
there is violation of Section 25F, 25G and 25H of the Act. Hence this petition. 


4. Notice of the petition was given to respondents. 


५ Respondent no.1 filed reply to the claim petition maintaining therein that the workman has not come to the 
Court with clean hands as he was appointed as sweeper by respondent no.2 (contractor) at minimum wage rate as 
prescribed by the Central Government. As per Government Policy dated 18.03.2011 (Ex.R-1), Non-Cantonment 
Military Stations have been authorized to outsource conservancy safaiwala. The answering respondent entered into 
contract with various private agencies for outsourcing of sweepers (safai karamcharis). In July 2018, the answering 
respondent entered into contract with M/s AA Foundation for safety and M/s Sunrise Facilitator Pvt. Ltd. through e- 
tendering for outsourcing of safai karamcharis to provide conservancy services in Chandi Mandir Military Station for 
the financial year 2018-19. Respondent no.2 submits bill for payment for the days the safai karamcharis performed 
their duties and answering respondent is only responsible to forward these bills to PCDA Western Command 
Chandigarh to release payment directly to the contractor’s account. Salary is paid to the workman by the contractor. 
Compliance of PF, ESI Rules etc. is carried out by the contractor as per contract agreement. Answering respondent 
has no responsibility of disbursement of wages and deduction of EPF and ESI etc. The workman and other safai 
karamcharis formed union in the headquarter, which is not permitted and also carried out demonstration inside the 
premises of Station Headquarter on 27.10.2018 and failed to perform his duties. Thereafter, answering respondent 
asked respondent no.2 to ask the workers to perform their duties. Answering respondent has no role or responsibility 
for termination services of the workman. There is no relationship of employer and employee between the answering 
respondent and the workman. In view of Army Headquarter Letter, the civilian employees of the answering 
respondent cannot organize themselves into trade unions as station headquarter is an administrative office of the 
Government of India. In spite of that, workman along with other safai karamcharis formed union unauthorizedly and 
indulge in unlawful activities. It is maintained that workman is the employee of contractor and therefore, termination 
is the prerogative of the contractor. It is prayed that the claim be dismissed. 


6. Respondent no.2 also filed reply maintaining therein that workman worked with it w.e.f. 05.07.2018 to 
27.10.2018. Respondent no.2 has not terminated the services of workman. Workman was paid minimum wages as 
declared by the Centre Government from time to time. Workman himself left the job at his own will without notice. It 
is prayed that the claim may be dismissed. 


Evidence of workman:- 


7. In order to prove his case, workman examined himself as WW1 and tendered into evidence his affidavit 
WWIH/A and has been cross examined by the AR for respondent 10.1. AR for workman closed the evidence on 
30.07.2024. 


Evidence of respondent no.1:- 


8. Respondent 10.1 examined Colonel K P Singh S/o Late Colonel P. P. Singh, OIC, Civic Cell, Station Head 
Quarter, Chandi Mandir Panchkula as MW1, who tendered into evidence his affidavit ExMWI/A along with 
documents Ex.R1 to Ex.R4. AR for respondent 10.1 closed the evidence on 10.03.2026. 


Sr. No. Document Dated Exhibit 
1. Policy 18.03.2011 Ex.R-1 
2 Agreement with respondent no.2 05.07.2018 Ex.R-2 
3. Copy of ECR of EPFO Ex.R-3 
4 Letter of Head Quarter 22.04.1980 Ex.R-4 


9. It is pertinent to mention here that respondent 10.2 was proceeded ex parte on 20.04.2023. 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 9, 2026/वैशाख 19, 1948 4157 
Submissions of workman:- 


10. While arguing the case, AR for workman contended that in this case, workman was appointed by respondent 
10.1 under a camouflage agreement with respondent 10.2. The workman was appointed directly by respondent 10.1 
on 14.10.2015 as sweeper and his services were dispensed with on 27.10.2018 without any reason in violation of 
Section 25F, 25G and 25H of the Act as no compensation was paid to the workman and junior to him were retained in 
service and fresh appointment were made after termination of the workman. So far as the agreements between the 
respondent 10.1 & 2 are concerned, they are totally not tenable and is unfair labour practice. The work performed by 
the workman is of perennial in nature. The contractor was not having any license. The contractor has left after three 
months. No domestic inquiry was held before terminating the service of the workman, which is mandatory under the 
Act. 


Submissions of respondent no.1:- 


11. On the other hand, AR for respondent 10.1 contended that the workman was engaged by respondent 10.2 
(contractor) as per agreement executed between respondent 10.1 & 2 on outsource basis and there is no relationship 
of employer and employee between respondent 10.1 and the workman. Wages were paid by the respondent 10.2 to 
the workman and there was no role of respondent 10.1 in appointing or terminating the services of the workman. 
Workman is employee of respondent no.2. If there is any violation of provision of the Act, then it is done by 
respondent no.2. As per Ex.R3, all dues were paid by the contractor with regard to EPF and ESI etc. The workman 
and other safai karamcharis formed union in the Headquarter, which is not permitted as per Ex.R4. Respondent no.1 
being the Government Agency, workman is trying to take benefit of his appointment by respondent no.2 and has filed 
a false claim after concealing material facts and claim of the workman is not maintainable. 


12. I have given due consideration to the arguments advanced by AR for both the parties. 


Findings:- 

13. The first contention regarding the applicant is that whether applicant comes within the definition of 
workman as is defined in Section 2(s) of the Act. I may mention that applicant was appointed as sweeper as per his 
claim petition and affidavit submitted before the Tribunal. In plain words the applicant was performing his duties as 
labourer/unskilled worker. He was not in supervisory or administrative post requiring him to perform only 
administrative duties. While interpreting Section 2(s) Hon’ble Supreme Court in the case of Devinder Singh Vs. 
Municipal Council, Sanaur, AIR 2011 Supreme Court 2532,has observed as follows:- 


“The source of employment, the quantum of recruitment, the terms & conditions of employment contract of 
service, the quantum of wages/ pay and mode of payment are not at all relevant for deciding whether or not a 
person is a workman within the meaning of Section 2(s) of the Act. The definition of workman also does not 
make any distinction between full time and part time employee or a person appointed on contract basis. 
There is nothing in the plain language of Section 2(s) from which it can be inferred that only person 
employed on regular basis or a person employed for doing whole time job is a workman and the one 
employed on temporary, part time or contract basis on fixed wages or as a casual employee or for doing duty 
for fixed hours is not a workman.” 


14. Thus Hon’ble Supreme Court has clarified that the definition of workman also does not make any distinction 
between full time or part time employee or a person appointed on contract basis. There is nothing in plain language of 
Section 2(s) from which it can be infer that only person employed on regular basis or a person employed for doing 
whole time job is a workman and the one employed on temporary, part time or contract basis on fixed wages or as a 
casual employee or for doing duty for fixed hours is not a workman. In view of the ratio of law enunciated in the 
above ruling, in my considered opinion the applicant herein admittedly falls within the definition of ‘workman’ under 
Section 2(s) of the Act. 


15. The Hon’ble Supreme Court after analyzing the catena of cases has laid down in Balwant Raj Saluja Vs. Air 
India Limited in Civil Appeal No.10266 dated 25.08.2014, two well recognized tests to find out whether the labours 
are the contract employees of the principal employer are:- 


1) Whether the principal employer pays the salary instead of contractor and 
2) Whether the principal employer controls and supervise the work of the employees? 


16. The facts regarding the payment of salary by the management or contractor has not been stated in the claim 
petition of the workman. In fact, claim petition is totally silent regarding the payment of wages, salary, letter of 
appointment by respondent 10.1 or by contractor or anything likewise. Thus, this basic feature for holding the 
relationship of employer and employee i.e. between respondent 10.1 and workman totally lacking not in the pleading 
but also in the evidence submitted by the workman.However, MW1 Colonel K P Singh in his cross examination has 
stated that it is responsibility of the contractor to pay monthly wages to the conservancy staff provided by him. 
Respondent 10.1 has nothing to do with the minimum wages, DC rate to the conservancy staff. Contractor is 
responsible to give notice to the conservancy staff provided by him. Respondent 10.1 has no roll in terminating the 
services of the workman. Thus, it was the respondent no.2 who was required to ensure the payment of wages to the 
workers engaged by him from time to time. 


4158 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 9, 2026/VAISAKHA 19, 1948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


17. It is also added here that respondent 10.1 has also placed on record documents Ex.R-1 to Ex.R-4. Ex.R-2 is 
the contract agreement between respondent no.1 & 2. Thus, from the adduced evidence, it emerges that workman was 
paid salary by the contractor and he was being controlled and supervised by the contractor. Thus, there does not exist 
any relationship of employee and employer between the workman and respondent 10.1. However, there exists relation 
of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.2. 


18. It is also added here that as per definition of contractor as given in the Section 2(c) of the Contract Labour 
(Regulation and Abolition) Act, 1970 (hereinafter called as Contract Act), respondent no.2 was supplying contract 
labour to the respondent no.1. Thus, respondent no.2 would fall under the definition of contractor. Section 2(c) of the 
Contract Act is reproduced as under: 


“Contractor”, in relation to an establishment, means a person who undertakes to produce a given result for the 
establishment, other than a mere supply of goods or articles of manufacture to such establishment, through contract 
labour or who supplies contract labour for any work of the establishment and includes a sub-contractor; 


19. TheHon’ble Apex Court in the case of Banglore Water Supply & Sewerage Board Vs. A. Rajappa 1978(36) 
FLR 266 dealt at length with the ambit and scope of expression “industry” as defined in Section 2(j) of the Act, and 


has laid down triple test for determining whether a particular establishment is industry or not. The triple test is where 
(a) systematic activity (b) organized by cooperation between employer and employee (the direct and substantial 
element is commercial (c) for the production or distribution of goods and services to calculated to satisfy human 
wants and wishes prima facie is an “Industry” in that enterprise. The workman was doing work i.e. systematic activity 
for respondent 10.1 at the asking of respondent 10.2 and there was cooperation between the workman and respondent 
no.2, which involve substantial commercial element for rendering service to satisfy human wants. Thus, respondent 
no.2 would be termed as industry. 


20. Workman in his deposition has clearly stated that there was non-compliance of Section 25F, 25G and 25H of 
the Act. He also stated that he was appointed on 14.10.2015 and he was paid minimum wages prescribed by Central 
Government and was working with respondent 10.1. He was not given any compensation before terminating his 
services on 27.10.2018. In his cross examination, he has further stated that his salary used to be credited in his bank 
account. But he has not placed on record any bank account, in which his salary was credited in order to enable this 
Court to see who was making payment to the workman. However, there is no denial that the workman was paid salary 
by respondent no.2 (contractor). However, from the agreement dated 05.07.2018 (Ex.R-2) placed on file by 
respondent no.1, it emerges that safai karamcharis were provided by respondent no.2 (contractor) to work with 
respondent 10.1. Thus, workman was employee of respondent 10.2 and not of respondent 10.1 as has been discussed 
above. There does not exist any relationship of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.1. 
However, there exists relation of employee and employer between the workman and respondent 10.2. 


21. Admittedly, there is non-compliance of Section 25F of the Act by respondent no.2. However, as regards 
violation of other provisions i.e. Section 25G and 25H of the Act, there is no evidence lead by the workman. Hence, 
workman is entitled for compensation from respondent no.2. 


22. It is also added here that there is no iota of evidence that any unfair labour practice used by respondent no.1 
by entering into contract with contractors for supply of safai karamcharis and agreement executed between 
respondent 10.1 and 2 cannot be said to be any unfair labour practice. So far as contentions of AR for workman that 
the work performed by the workman is of perennial in nature and no domestic inquiry was held before terminating 
the services of workman are concerned, in this regard, it is pointed out that workman was entitled only for 
compensation under Section 25F of the Act by respondent no.2 (contractor) and no domestic inquiry was required to 
be conducted. Merely because work is of perennial in nature does not mean that any right has accrued in favour of the 
workman to get employment with respondent no.1, which is a department of Civil Defence run by Government of 
India and for that regular recruitment procedure is provided. Workman cannot seek backdoor entry into service. 
Moreover, workman is not employee of respondent 10.1 being employee of respondent 10.2 (contractor). 


23. In view of above discussion, it is held that since respondent no.2 (contractor) is currently not working with 
respondent 10.1, so keeping in view the tenure of service rendered by the workman and salary given to him during the 
period of his stay with the contractor and non-compliance of Section 25F of the Act, this Tribunal is of the considered 
opinion that workman is entitled for compensation. 


24. Keeping in view the fact and circumstances of the present case, the following scheme of compensation is 
deemed fit by this Tribunal: 


1. Workman who worked for less than 2 years with contractor shall be entitled for Rs.20,000/-. 


ii. | Workman who worked for more than 2 years but less than 5 years with contractor shall be entitled 
Rs.30,000/-. 


iii. Workman who worked for more than 5 years with contractors shall be entitled for Rs. 50,000/-. 


25. Present workman employed on 14.10.2015 and was terminated on 27.10.2018 (more than 2 years and less 
than 5 years), so he is entitled for Rs. 30,000/-. 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(1)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 9, 2026/वैशाख 19, 1948 4159 


26. Respondent 10.2 is directed to pay the workman Rs.30,000/- within one month from the date of the 
notification of the award failing which the respondent 10.2 shall further pay interest (06% per annum from the date of 
award till the date of making payment. It is also added here that in case respondent 10.1 requires safai karamcharis, 
then workman may be engaged through any contractor on sympathetic ground. 


27. Let copy of this award be sent to Central Government for publication as required under Section 17 of the 
Act. 


KAMAL KANT, PO-cum-Link Officer 


नई दिल्‍ली, 5 मई, 2026

S.O. 806.—In pursuance of Section 17 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central 
Government hereby publishes the Award (Ref. 51/2024) of the Cent.Govt.Indus.Tribunal-cum-Labour Court

IN THE CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL-CUM-LABOUR COURT-I, 
CHANDIGARH 


(Present:- Mr. Kamal Kant, PO-cum-Link Officer). 
ID No. 51/2024 
Registered on:-16.10.2019 
Rohtash S/o Sh. Udaybir, R/o 189 Ram Pur Seuri, Kalka, Panchkula, Haryana. 


Versus 
1. Admn. Commander Station Head Quarter (Civil Defence), ChandiMandirCantt. Panchkula, Haryana. 
2. M/s Sun Rise Facilitator Pvt. Ltd., 2253/1 2704 Floor, Pipli Wala Town, Mani Mazra, Chandigarh. 
----Respondents 
Present:- Sh. Jang Bahadur, AR for workman. 
Sh. Satinder Singh, AR for respondent 10.1. 
Respondent no.2 already ex parte. 
Award :10.03.2026 


1.Workman has filed the present claim petition under Section 2A of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 
(hereinafter called the Act) against his termination w.e.f. 27.10.2018 claiming reinstatement into service along 
with continuity of service and full back wages. 


2.It is maintained by the workman in the claim petition that that he was appointed as sweeper/safai karamchari 
on 17.10.2016 by respondent 10.1 at minimum wages prescribed by the Central Government. But the said 
minimum wages were never paid to him. Management has unlawfully deducted 12% EPF share, 1.75% ESI 
and Rs.300/- as TDS from hissalary despite of the fact that his total wages comes under the ambit of tax free 


4160 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 9, 2026/VAISAKHA 19, 1948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


limit. He was working under respondent no.1 directly, but respondent no.1 in order to avoid its liability always 
shows his employment in record under different contractors, which tantamount to unfair labour practice. 


3.It is further maintained that workman including 200 other safai karamcharis of respondent 10.1 raised the 
demand for non-deduction of TDS from the wages paid by respondent 10.1. However, their demand was not 
accepted. Other workers working under respondent 10.1 formed union and subsequently his services were 
dispensed with w.e.f. 27.10.2018. It is also maintained that respondent 10.1 retained juniors in service and also 
made fresh appointments after termination of his services and no compensation was paid under Section 25F of 
the Act. Thus, there is violation of Section 25F, 25G and 25H of the Act. Hence this petition. 


4.Notice of the petition was given to respondents. 


5.Respondent 10.1 filed reply to the claim petition maintaining therein that the workman has not come to the 
Court with clean hands as he was appointed as sweeper by respondent no.2 (contractor) at minimum wage rate 
as prescribed by the Central Government. As per Government Policy dated 18.03.2011 (Ex.R-1), Non- 
Cantonment Military Stations have been authorized to outsource conservancy safaiwala. The answering 
respondent entered into contract with various private agencies for outsourcing of sweepers (safai karamcharis). 
In July 2018, the answering respondent entered into contract with M/s AA Foundation for safety and 
M/s Sunrise Facilitator Pvt. Ltd. through e-tendering for outsourcing of safai karamcharis to provide 
conservancy services in Chandi Mandir Military Station for the financial year 2018-19. Respondent no.2 
submits bill for payment for the days the safai karamcharis performed their duties and answering respondent is 
only responsible to forward these bills to PCDA Western Command Chandigarh to release payment directly to 
the contractor’s account. Salary is paid to the workman by the contractor. Compliance of PF, ESI Rules etc. is 
carried out by the contractor as per contract agreement. Answering respondent has no responsibility of 
disbursement of wages and deduction of EPF and ESI etc. The workman and other safai karamcharis formed 
union in the headquarter, which is not permitted and also carried out demonstration inside the premises of 
Station Headquarter on 27.10.2018 and failed to perform his duties. Thereafter, answering respondent asked 
respondent no.2 to ask the workers to perform their duties. Answering respondent has no role or responsibility 
for termination services of the workman. There is no relationship of employer and employee between the 
answering respondent and the workman. In view of Army Headquarter Letter, the civilian employees of the 
answering respondent cannot organize themselves into trade unions as station headquarter is an administrative 
office of the Government of India. In spite of that, workman along with other safai karamcharis formed union 
unauthorizedly and indulge in unlawful activities. It is maintained that workman is the employee of contractor 
and therefore, termination is the prerogative of the contractor. It is prayed that the claim be dismissed. 


6.Respondent 10.2 also filed reply maintaining therein that workman worked with it w.e.f. 05.07.2018 to 
27.10.2018. Respondent no.2 has not terminated the services of workman. Workman was paid minimum 
wages as declared by the Centre Government from time to time. Workman himself left the job at his own will 
without notice. It is prayed that the claim may be dismissed. 


Evidence of workman:- 


7.In order to prove his case, workman examined himself as WW1 and tendered into evidence his affidavit 
WWI/A and has been cross examined by the AR for respondent 10.1. AR for workman closed the evidence on 
30.07.2024. 


Evidence of respondent no.1:- 


8.Respondent 10.1 examined Colonel K P Singh S/o Late Colonel P. P. Singh, OIC, Civic Cell, Station Head 
Quarter, Chandi Mandir Panchkula as MW1, who tendered into evidence his affidavit Ex.MW1/A along with 
documents Ex.R1 to Ex.R4. AR for respondent 10.1 closed the evidence on 10.03.2026. 


Sr. No. Document Dated Exhibit 
1. Policy 18.03.2011 Ex.R-1 
2: Agreement with respondent no.2 05.07.2018 Ex.R-2 
3. Copy of ECR of EPFO Ex.R-3 
4. Letter of Head Quarter 22.04.1980 Ex.R-4 


9.It is pertinent to mention here that respondent no.2 was proceeded ex parte on 20.04.2023. 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 9, 2026/वैशाख 19, 1948 4161 


Submissions of workman:- 

10. While arguing the case, AR for workman contended that in this case, workman was appointed by 
respondent 10.1 under a camouflage agreement with respondent 10.2. The workman was appointed directly by 
respondent 10.1 on 17.10.2016 as sweeper and his services were dispensed with on 27.10.2018 without any 
reason in violation of Section 25F, 25G and 25H of the Act as no compensation was paid to the workman and 
junior to him were retained in service and fresh appointment were made after termination of the workman. So 
far as the agreements between the respondent no.1 & 2 are concerned, they are totally not tenable and is unfair 
labour practice. The work performed by the workman is of perennial in nature. The contractor was not having 
any license. The contractor has left after three months. No domestic inquiry was held before terminating the 
service of the workman, which is mandatory under the Act. 


Submissions of respondent no.1:- 

11. On the other hand, AR for respondent 10.1 contended that the workman was engaged by respondent 
no.2 (contractor) as per agreement executed between respondent 10.1 & 2 on outsource basis and there is no 
relationship of employer and employee between respondent 10.1 and the workman. Wages were paid by the 
respondent 10.2 to the workman and there was no role of respondent 10.1 in appointing or terminating the 
services of the workman. Workman is employee of respondent 10.2. If there is any violation of provision of 
the Act, then it is done by respondent no.2. As per Ex.R3, all dues were paid by the contractor with regard to 
EPF and ESI etc. The workman and other safai karamcharis formed union in the Headquarter, which is not 
permitted as per Ex.R4. Respondent 10.1 being the Government Agency, workman is trying to take benefit of 
his appointment by respondent no.2 and has filed a false claim after concealing material facts and claim of the 
workman is not maintainable. 


12. Ihave given due consideration to the arguments advanced by AR for both the parties. 
Findings:- 
13. The first contention regarding the applicant is that whether applicant comes within the definition of 


workman as is defined in Section 2(s) of the Act. I may mention that applicant was appointed as sweeper as 
per his claim petition and affidavit submitted before the Tribunal. In plain words the applicant was performing 
his duties as labourer/unskilled worker. He was not in supervisory or administrative post requiring him to 
perform only administrative duties. While interpreting Section 2(s) Hon’ble Supreme Court in the case of 
Devinder Singh Vs. Municipal Council, Sanaur, AIR 2011 Supreme Court 2532,has observed as follows:- 
“The source of employment, the quantum of recruitment, the terms & conditions of 
employment/ contract of service, the quantum of wages/ pay and mode of payment are 
not at all relevant for deciding whether or not a person is a workman within the 
meaning of Section 2(s) of the Act. The definition of workman also does not make any 
distinction between full time and part time employee or a person appointed on contract 
basis. There is nothing in the plain language of Section 2(s) from which it can be 
inferred that only person employed on regular basis or a person employed for doing 
whole time job is a workman and the one employed on temporary, part time or contract 
basis on fixed wages or as a casual employee or for doing duty for fixed hours is not a 
workman.” 
14. Thus Hon’ble Supreme Court has clarified that the definition of workman also does not make any 
distinction between full time or part time employee or a person appointed on contract basis. There is nothing in 
plain language of Section 2(s) from which it can be infer that only person employed on regular basis or a 
person employed for doing whole time job is a workman and the one employed on temporary, part time or 
contract basis on fixed wages or as a casual employee or for doing duty for fixed hours is not a workman. In 
view of the ratio of law enunciated in the above ruling, in my considered opinion the applicant herein 
admittedly falls within the definition of ‘workman’ under Section 2(s) of the Act. 
15. The Hon’ble Supreme Court after analyzing the catena of cases has laid down in Balwant Raj 
Saluja Vs. Air India Limited in Civil Appeal No.10266 dated 25.08.2014, two well recognized tests to find 
out whether the labours are the contract employees of the principal employer are:- 
1) Whether the principal employer pays the salary instead of contractor and 
2) Whether the principal employer controls and supervise the work of the employees? 
16. The facts regarding the payment of salary by the management or contractor has not been stated in 
the claim petition of the workman. In fact, claim petition is totally silent regarding the payment of wages, 
salary, letter of appointment by respondent no.1 or by contractor or anything likewise. Thus, this basic feature 
for holding the relationship of employer and employee i.e. between respondent 10.1 and workman totally 
lacking not in the pleading but also in the evidence submitted by the workman.However, MW1 Colonel 
K P Singh in his cross examination has stated that it is responsibility of the contractor to pay monthly wages to 
the conservancy staff provided by him. Respondent 10.1 has nothing to do with the minimum wages, DC rate 
to the conservancy staff. Contractor is responsible to give notice to the conservancy staff provided by him. 
Respondent no.1 has no roll in terminating the services of the workman. Thus, it was the respondent no.2 who 
was required to ensure the payment of wages to the workers engaged by him from time to time. 


4162 


i. 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 9, 2026/VAISAKHA 19, 1948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


17. It is also added here that respondent 10.1 has also placed on record documents Ex.R-1 to Ex.R-4. 
Ex.R-2 is the contract agreement between respondent 10.1 & 2. Thus, from the adduced evidence, it emerges 
that workman was paid salary by the contractor and he was being controlled and supervised by the contractor. 
Thus, there does not exist any relationship of employee and employer between the workman and respondent 
10.1. However, there exists relation of employee and employer between the workman and respondent 10.2. 


18. It is also added here that as per definition of contractor as given in the Section 2(c) of the Contract 
Labour (Regulation and Abolition) Act, 1970 (hereinafter called as Contract Act), respondent no.2 was 
supplying contract labour to the respondent 10.1. Thus, respondent no.2 would fall under the definition of 
contractor. Section 2(c) of the Contract Act is reproduced as under: 


“Contractor”, in relation to an establishment, means a person who undertakes to 
produce a given result for the establishment, other than a mere supply of goods or 
articles of manufacture to such establishment, through contract labour or who supplies 
contract labour for any work of the establishment and includes a sub-contractor; 


19. TheHon’ble Apex Court in the case of Banglore Water Supply & Sewerage Board Vs. A. Rajappa 
1978(36) FLR 266 dealt at length with the ambit and scope of expression “industry” as defined in Section 20) 
of the Act, and has laid down triple test for determining whether a particular establishment is industry or not. 
The triple test is where (a) systematic activity (b) organized by cooperation between employer and employee 
(the direct and substantial element is commercial (c) for the production or distribution of goods and services to 
calculated to satisfy human wants and wishes prima facie is an “Industry” in that enterprise. The workman was 
doing work i.e. systematic activity for respondent 10.1 at the asking of respondent 10.2 and there was 
cooperation between the workman and respondent no.2, which involve substantial commercial element for 
rendering service to satisfy human wants. Thus, respondent no.2 would be termed as industry. 


20. Workman in his deposition has clearly stated that there was non-compliance of Section 25F, 25G 
and 25H of the Act. He also stated that he was appointed on 17.10.2016 and he was paid minimum wages 
prescribed by Central Government and was working with respondent 10.1. He was not given any 
compensation before terminating his services on 27.10.2018. In his cross examination, he has further stated 
that his salary used to be credited in his bank account. But he has not placed on record any bank account, in 
which his salary was credited in order to enable this Court to see who was making payment to the workman. 
However, there is no denial that the workman was paid salary by respondent no.2 (contractor). However, from 
the agreement dated 05.07.2018 (Ex.R-2) placed on file by respondent no.1, it emerges that safai karamcharis 
were provided by respondent no.2 (contractor) to work with respondent no.1. Thus, workman was employee of 
respondent 10.2 and not of respondent 10.1 as has been discussed above. There does not exist any relationship 
of employee and employer between the workman and respondent 10.1. However, there exists relation of 
employee and employer between the workman and respondent 10.2. 


21. Admittedly, there is non-compliance of Section 25F of the Act by respondent no.2. However, as 
regards violation of other provisions i.e. Section 25G and 25H of the Act, there is no evidence lead by the 
workman. Hence, workman is entitled for compensation from respondent no.2. 


22. It is also added here that there is no iota of evidence that any unfair labour practice used by 
respondent 10.1 by entering into contract with contractors for supply of safai karamcharis and agreement 
executed between respondent 10.1 and 2 cannot be said to be any unfair labour practice. So far as contentions 
of AR for workman that the work performed by the workman is of perennial in nature and no domestic inquiry 
was held before terminating the services of workman are concerned, in this regard, it is pointed out that 
workman was entitled only for compensation under Section 25F of the Act by respondent no.2 (contractor) and 
no domestic inquiry was required to be conducted. Merely because work is of perennial in nature does not 
mean that any right has accrued in favour of the workman to get employment with respondent no.1, which is a 
department of Civil Defence run by Government of India and for that regular recruitment procedure is 
provided. Workman cannot seek backdoor entry into service. Moreover, workman is not employee of 
respondent 10.1 being employee of respondent 10.2 (contractor). 


23. In view of above discussion, it is held that since respondent no.2 (contractor) is currently not 
working with respondent no.1, so keeping in view the tenure of service rendered by the workman and salary 
given to him during the period of his stay with the contractor and non-compliance of Section 25F of the Act, 
this Tribunal is of the considered opinion that workman is entitled for compensation. 


24. Keeping in view the fact and circumstances of the present case, the following scheme of 
compensation is deemed fit by this Tribunal: 


Workman who worked for less than 2 years with contractor shall be entitled for Rs.20,000/-. 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 9, 2026/वैशाख 19, 1948 4163 


ii. Workman who worked for more than 2 years but less than 5 years with contractor shall be entitled 
Rs.30,000/-. 


iii. Workman who worked for more than 5 years with contractors shall be entitled for Rs.50,000/-. 


25. Present workman employed on 17.10.2016 and was terminated on 27.10.2018 (more than 2 years 
and less than 5 years), so he is entitled for Rs.30,000/-. 


26. Respondent no.2 is directed to pay the workman Rs.30,000/- within one month from the date of the 
notification of the award failing which the respondent no.2 shall further pay interest @6% per annum from the 
date of award till the date of making payment. It is also added here that in case respondent no.1 requires safai 
karamcharis, then workman may be engaged through any contractor on sympathetic ground. 


27. Let copy of this award be sent to Central Government for publication as required under Section 17 
of the Act. 


KAMAL KANT, PO-cum-Link Officer 


नई दिल्‍ली, 5 मई, 2026

प्रशासनिक कमांडर, स्टेशन मुख्यालय (नागरिक सुरक्षा), पंचकुला के प्रबंधतत्र, संबद्ध नियोजकों और उनके कर्मकारों के बीच

Chandigarh-I_ as shown in the Annexure, in the industrial dispute between the management of Admn. Commander 
Station Head Quarter (Civil Defence), Panchkula and their workmen. 


[No. L-12025/01/2026- IR (B-I)-103] 
SALONI, Dy. Director

Versus 
1. Admn. Commander Station Head Quarter (Civil Defence), Chandi Mandir Cantt. Panchkula, Haryana. 
2. M/s Sun Rise Facilitator Pvt. Ltd., 2253/1 on Floor, PipliWala Town, Mani Mazra, Chandigarh. 
----Respondents 


Present:-Sh. Jang Bahadur, AR for workman. 
Sh. Satinder Singh, AR for respondent no.1. 
Respondent 10.2 already ex parte. 
Award :10.03.2026 


1. Workman has filed the present claim petition under Section 2A of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 
(hereinafter called the Act) against his termination w.e.f. 27.10.2018 claiming reinstatement into service 
along with continuity of service and full back wages. 


4164 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 9, 2026/VAISAKHA 19, 1948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


2. It is maintained by the workman in the claim petition that that he was appointed as sweeper/safaikaramchari 
on 01.04.2013 by respondent 10.1 at minimum wages prescribed by the Central Government. But the said minimum 
wages were never paid to him. Management has unlawfully deducted 12% EPF share, 1.75% ESI and Rs.300/- as 
TDS from hissalary despite of the fact that his total wages comes under the ambit of taxfree limit. He was working 
under respondent 10.1 directly, but respondent 10.1 in order to avoid its liability always shows his employment in 
record under different contractors, which tantamount to unfair labour practice. 


3. It is further maintained that workman including 200 other safai karamchari of respondent 10.1 raised the 
demand for non-deduction of TDS from the wages paid by respondent 10.1. However, their demand was not 
accepted. Other workers working under respondent 10.1 formed union and subsequently his services were dispensed 
with w.e.f. 27.10.2018. It is also maintained that respondent 10.1 retained juniors in service and also made fresh 
appointments after termination of his services and no compensation was paid under Section 25F of the Act. Thus, 
there is violation of Section 25F, 25G and 25H of the Act. Hence this petition. 


4. Notice of the petition was given to respondents. 


Ds Respondent no.1 filed reply to the claim petition maintaining therein that the workman has not come to the 
Court with clean hands as he was appointed as sweeper by respondent no.2 (contractor) at minimum wage rate as 
prescribed by the Central Government. As per Government Policy dated 18.03.2011 (Ex.R-1), Non-Cantonment 
Military Stations have been authorized to outsource conservancy safaiwala. The answering respondent entered into 
contract with various private agencies for outsourcing of sweepers (safai karamchari). In July 2018, the answering 
respondent entered into contract with M/s AA Foundation for safety and M/s Sunrise Facilitator Pvt. Ltd. through e- 
tendering for outsourcing of safai karamchari to provide conservancy services in Chandi Mandir Military Station for 
the financial year 2018-19. Respondent no.2 submits bill for payment for the days the safai karamchari performed 
their duties and answering respondent is only responsible to forward these bills to PCDA Western Command 
Chandigarh to release payment directly to the contractor’s account. Salary is paid to the workman by the contractor. 
Compliance of PF, ESI Rules etc. is carried out by the contractor as per contract agreement. Answering respondent 
has no responsibility of disbursement of wages and deduction of EPF and ESI etc. The workman and other safai 
karamchari formed union in the headquarter, which is not permitted and also carried out demonstration inside the 
premises of Station Headquarter on 27.10.2018 and failed to perform his duties. Thereafter, answering respondent 
asked respondent no.2 to ask the workers to perform their duties. Answering respondent has no role or responsibility 
for termination services of the workman. There is no relationship of employer and employee between the answering 
respondent and the workman. In view of Army Headquarter Letter, the civilian employees of the answering 
respondent cannot organize themselves into trade unions as station headquarter is an administrative office of the 
Government of India. In spite of that, workman along with other safai karamchari formed union unauthorizedly and 
indulge in unlawful activities. It is maintained that workman is the employee of contractor and therefore, termination 
is the prerogative of the contractor. It is prayed that the claim be dismissed. 


6. Respondent no.2 also filed reply maintaining therein that workman worked with it w.e.f. 05.07.2018 to 
27.10.2018. Respondent no.2 has not terminated the services of workman. Workman was paid minimum wages as 
declared by the Centre Government from time to time. Workman himself left the job at his own will without notice. It 
is prayed that the claim may be dismissed. 


Evidence of workman:- 


7. In order to prove his case, workman examined himself as WW1 and tendered into evidence his affidavit 
WWI/A and has been cross examined by the AR for respondent 10.1. AR for workman closed the evidence on 
22.07.2024. 


Evidence of respondent no.1:- 


8. Respondent 10.1 examined Colonel K P Singh S/o Late Colonel P. P. Singh, OIC, Civic Cell, Station Head 
Quarter, Chandi Mandir Panchkula as MW1, who tendered into evidence his affidavit ExMW1I/A along with 
documents Ex.R1 to Ex.R4. AR for respondent 10.1 closed the evidence on 29.01.2026. 


Sr. No. Document Dated Exhibit 
1. Policy 18.03.2011 Ex.R-1 
2 Agreement with respondent no.2 05.07.2018 Ex.R-2 
3. Copy of ECR of EPFO Ex.R-3 
4 Letter of Head Quarter 22.04.1980 Ex.R-4 


9. It is pertinent to mention here that respondent 10.2 was proceeded ex parte on 07.05.2024. 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 9, 2026/वैशाख 19, 1948 4165 
Submissions of workman:- 


10. While arguing the case, AR for workman contended that in this case, workman was appointed by respondent 
10.1 under a camouflage agreement with respondent 10.2. The workman was appointed directly by respondent 10.1 
on 01.04.2013 as sweeper and his services were dispensed with on 27.10.2018 without any reason in violation of 
Section 25F, 25G and 25H of the Act as no compensation was paid to the workman and junior to him were retained in 
service and fresh appointment were made after termination of the workman. So far as the agreements between the 
respondent 10.1 & 2 are concerned, they are totally not tenable and is unfair labour practice. The work performed by 
the workman is of perennial in nature. The contractor was not having any license. The contractor has left after three 
months. No domestic inquiry was held before terminating the service of the workman, which is mandatory under the 
Act. 


Submissions of respondent no.1:- 


11. On the other hand, AR for respondent 10.1 contended that the workman was engaged by respondent 10.2 
(contractor) as per agreement executed between respondent 10.1 & 2 on outsource basis and there is no relationship 
of employer and employee between respondent 10.1 and the workman. Wages were paid by the respondent 10.2 to 
the workman and there was no role of respondent 10.1 in appointing or terminating the services of the workman. 
Workman is employee of respondent no.2. If there is any violation of provision of the Act, then it is done by 
respondent no.2. As per Ex.R3, all dues were paid by the contractor with regard to EPF and ESI etc. The workman 
and other safai karamchari formed union in the Headquarter, which is not permitted as per Ex.R4. Respondent no.1 
being the Government Agency, workman is trying to take benefit of his appointment by respondent no.2 and has filed 
a false claim after concealing material facts and claim of the workman is not maintainable. 


12. I have given due consideration to the arguments advanced by AR for both the parties. 


Findings:- 

13. The first contention regarding the applicant is that whether applicant comes within the definition of 
workman as is defined in Section 2(s) of the Act. I may mention that applicant was appointed as sweeper as per his 
claim petition and affidavit submitted before the Tribunal. In plain words the applicant was performing his duties as 
labourer/unskilled worker. He was not in supervisory or administrative post requiring him to perform only 
administrative duties. While interpreting Section 2(s) Hon’ble Supreme Court in the case of Devinder Singh Vs. 
Municipal Council, Sanaur, AIR 2011 Supreme Court 2532,has observed as follows:- 


“The source of employment, the quantum of recruitment, the terms & conditions of employment contract of 
service, the quantum of wages/ pay and mode of payment are not at all relevant for deciding whether or not a 
person is a workman within the meaning of Section 2(s) of the Act. The definition of workman also does not 
make any distinction between full time and part time employee or a person appointed on contract basis. 
There is nothing in the plain language of Section 2(s) from which it can be inferred that only person 
employed on regular basis or a person employed for doing whole time job is a workman and the one 
employed on temporary, part time or contract basis on fixed wages or as a casual employee or for doing duty 
for fixed hours is not a workman.” 


14. Thus Hon’ble Supreme Court has clarified that the definition of workman also does not make any distinction 
between full time or part time employee or a person appointed on contract basis. There is nothing in plain language of 
Section 2(s) from which it can be infer that only person employed on regular basis or a person employed for doing 
whole time job is a workman and the one employed on temporary, part time or contract basis on fixed wages or as a 
casual employee or for doing duty for fixed hours is not a workman. In view of the ratio of law enunciated in the 
above ruling, in my considered opinion the applicant herein admittedly falls within the definition of ‘workman’ under 
Section 2(s) of the Act. 


15. The Hon’ble Supreme Court after analyzing the catena of cases has laid down in Balwant Raj Saluja Vs. Air 
India Limited in Civil Appeal No.10266 dated 25.08.2014, two well recognized tests to find out whether the labours 
are the contract employees of the principal employer are:- 


1) Whether the principal employer pays the salary instead of contractor and 
2) Whether the principal employer controls and supervise the work of the employees? 


16. The facts regarding the payment of salary by the management or contractor has not been stated in the claim 
petition of the workman. In fact, claim petition is totally silent regarding the payment of wages, salary, letter of 
appointment by respondent 10.1 or by contractor or anything likewise. Thus, this basic feature for holding the 
relationship of employer and employee i.e. between respondent 10.1 and workman totally lacking not in the pleading 
but also in the evidence submitted by the workman.However, MW1 Colonel K P Singh in his cross examination has 
stated that it is responsibility of the contractor to pay monthly wages to the conservancy staff provided by him. 
Respondent 10.1 has nothing to do with the minimum wages, DC rate to the conservancy staff. Contractor is 
responsible to give notice to the conservancy staff provided by him. Respondent 10.1 has no roll in terminating the 
services of the workman. Thus, it was the respondent no.2 who was required to ensure the payment of wages to the 
workers engaged by him from time to time. 


4166 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 9, 2026/VAISAKHA 19, 1948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


17. It is also added here that respondent 10.1 has also placed on record documents Ex.R-1 to Ex.R-4. Ex.R-2 is 
the contract agreement between respondent no.1 & 2. Thus, from the adduced evidence, it emerges that workman was 
paid salary by the contractor and he was being controlled and supervised by the contractor. Thus, there does not exist 
any relationship of employee and employer between the workman and respondent 10.1. However, there exists relation 
of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.2. 


18. It is also added here that as per definition of contractor as given in the Section 2(c) of the Contract Labour 
(Regulation and Abolition) Act, 1970 (hereinafter called as Contract Act), respondent no.2 was supplying contract 
labour to the respondent no.1. Thus, respondent no.2 would fall under the definition of contractor. Section 2(c) of the 
Contract Act is reproduced as under: 


“Contractor”, in relation to an establishment, means a person who undertakes to produce a given result for the 
establishment, other than a mere supply of goods or articles of manufacture to such establishment, through contract 
labour or who supplies contract labour for any work of the establishment and includes a sub-contractor; 


19. TheHon’ble Apex Court in the case of Banglore Water Supply & Sewerage Board Vs. A. Rajappa 1978(36) 
FLR 266 dealt at length with the ambit and scope of expression “industry” as defined in Section 2(j) of the Act, and 


has laid down triple test for determining whether a particular establishment is industry or not. The triple test is where 
(a) systematic activity (b) organized by cooperation between employer and employee (the direct and substantial 
element is commercial (c) for the production or distribution of goods and services to calculated to satisfy human 
wants and wishes prima facie is an “Industry” in that enterprise. The workman was doing work i.e. systematic activity 
for respondent 10.1 at the asking of respondent 10.2 and there was cooperation between the workman and respondent 
no.2, which involve substantial commercial element for rendering service to satisfy human wants. Thus, respondent 
no.2 would be termed as industry. 


20. Workman in his deposition has clearly stated that there was non-compliance of Section 25F, 25G and 25H of 
the Act. He also stated that he was appointed on 01.04.2013 and he was paid minimum wages prescribed by Central 
Government and was working with respondent 10.1. He was not given any compensation before terminating his 
services on 27.10.2018. In his cross examination, he has further stated that his salary used to be credited in his bank 
account. But he has not placed on record any bank account, in which his salary was credited in order to enable this 
Court to see who was making payment to the workman. However, there is no denial that the workman was paid salary 
by respondent no.2 (contractor). However, from the agreement dated 05.07.2018 (Ex.R-2) placed on file by 
respondent 10.1, it emerges that safai karamchari were provided by respondent no.2 (contractor) to work with 
respondent no.1. Thus, workman was employee of respondent no.2 and not of respondent no.1 as has been discussed 
above. There does not exist any relationship of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.1. 
However, there exists relation of employee and employer between the workman and respondent 10.2. 


21. Admittedly, there is non-compliance of Section 25F of the Act by respondent no.2. However, as regards 
violation of other provisions i.e. Section 25G and 25H of the Act, there is no evidence lead by the workman. Hence, 
workman is entitled for compensation from respondent no.2. 


22. It is also added here that there is no iota of evidence that any unfair labour practice used by respondent no.1 
by entering into contract with contractors for supply of safai karamchari and agreement executed between respondent 
10.1 and 2 cannot be said to be any unfair labour practice. So far as contentions of AR for workman that the work 
performed by the workman is of perennial in nature and no domestic inquiry was held before terminating the services 
of workman are concerned, in this regard, it is pointed out that workman was entitled only for compensation under 
Section 25F of the Act by respondent no.2 (contractor) and no domestic inquiry was required to be conducted. Merely 
because work is of perennial in nature does not mean that any right has accrued in favour of the workman to get 
employment with respondent no.1, which is a department of Civil Defence run by Government of India and for that 
regular recruitment procedure is provided. Workman cannot seek backdoor entry into service. Moreover, workman is 
not employee of respondent 10.1 being employee of respondent no.2 (contractor). 


23. In view of above discussion, it is held that since respondent no.2 (contractor) is currently not working with 
respondent 10.1, so keeping in view the tenure of service rendered by the workman and salary given to him during the 
period of his stay with the contractor and non-compliance of Section 25F of the Act, this Tribunal is of the considered 
opinion that workman is entitled for compensation. 


24. Keeping in view the fact and circumstances of the present case, the following scheme of compensation is 
deemed fit by this Tribunal: 


1. Workman who worked for less than 2 years with contractor shall be entitled for Rs.20,000/-. 

il. Workman who worked for more than 2 years but less than 5 years with contractor shall be entitled 
Rs.30,000/-. 

ili. Workman who worked for more than 5 years with contractors shall be entitled for Rs.50,000/-. 

25 Present workman employed on 01.04.2013 and was terminated on 27.10.2018 (more than 5 years), so he is 


entitled for Rs.50,000/-. 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(1)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 9, 2026/वैशाख 19, 1948 4167 


26. Respondent 10.2 is directed to pay the workman Rs.50,000/- within one month from the date of the 
notification of the award failing which the respondent no.2 shall further pay interest @6% per annum from the date of 
award till the date of making payment. It is also added here that in case respondent 10.1 requires safai karamchari, 
then workman may be engaged through any contractor on sympathetic ground. 


27. Let copy of this award be sent to Central Government for publication as required under Section 17 of the 
Act. 


KAMAL KANT, PO-cum-Link Officer 


नई दिल्‍ली, 5 मई, 2026

प्रशासनिक कमांडर, स्टेशन मुख्यालय (नागरिक सुरक्षा), पंचकुला के प्रबंधतत्र, संबद्ध नियोजकों और उनके कर्मकारों के बीच 
अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट औद्योगिक विवाद में केन्द्रीय सरकार औद्योगिक अधिकरण»श्रम न्यायालय चंडीगढ़-ा के 
tare (49/2024) प्रकाशित करती है | 


[a. एल- 12025/01/2026-आईआर (बी-1)-104] 
सलोनी, उप निदेशक 
New Delhi, the 5th May, 2026 


S.O. 808.—In pursuance of Section 17 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central 
Government hereby publishes the Award (Ref. 49/2024) of the Cent.Govt.Indus.Tribunal-cum-Labour Court 
Chandigarh-I_ as shown in the Annexure, in the industrial dispute between the management of Admn. Commander 
Station Head Quarter ( Civil Defence), Panchkula and their workmen. 


[No. L-12025/01/2026- IR (B-I)-104] 
SALONI, Dy. Director 
ANNEXURE 


IN THE CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL-CUM-LABOUR COURT-I, 
CHANDIGARH 


(Present:- Mr. Kamal Kant, PO-cum-Link Officer). 
ID No. 49/2024 
Registered on:-16.10.2019 
Surja W/o Sh. Rajender Kumar R/o 144 Ram Pur Seuri, Kalka, Panchkula, Haryana. 


wannn--- Workman 
Versus 
1. Admn. Commander Station Head Quarter (Civil Defence), Chandi Mandir Cantt. Panchkula, Haryana. 
2. M/s Sun Rise Facilitator Pvt. Ltd., 2253/1 20 Floor, PipliWala Town, Mani Mazra, Chandigarh. 
----Respondents 
Present:-Sh. Jang Bahadur, AR for workman. 
Sh. Satinder Singh, AR for respondent no.1. 
Respondent no.2 already ex parte. 
Award :10.03.2026 

dls Workman has filed the present claim petition under Section 2A of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 


(hereinafter called the Act) against her termination w.e.f. 27.10.2018 claiming reinstatement into service along with 
continuity of service and full back wages. 


2. It is maintained by the workman in the claim petition that that she was appointed as sweeper/safai 
karamchari on 01.07.2014 by respondent 10.1 at minimum wages prescribed by the Central Government. But the said 
minimum wages were never paid to her. Management has unlawfully deducted 12% EPF share, 1.75% ESI and 
Rs.300/- as TDS from his salary despite of the fact that her total wages comes under the ambit of tax free limit. She 


4168 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 9, 2026/VAISAKHA 19, 1948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


was working under respondent 10.1 directly, but respondent 10.1 in order to avoid its liability always shows his 
employment in record under different contractors, which tantamount to unfair labour practice. 


3: It is further maintained that workman including 200 other safai karamcharis of respondent no.1 raised the 
demand for non-deduction of TDS from the wages paid by respondent 10.1. However, their demand was not 
accepted. Other workers working under respondent 10.1 formed union and subsequently her services were dispensed 
with w.e.f. 27.10.2018. It is also maintained that respondent 10.1 retained juniors in service and also made fresh 
appointments after termination of her services and no compensation was paid under Section 25F of the Act. Thus, 
there is violation of Section 25F, 25G and 25H of the Act. Hence this petition. 


4. Notice of the petition was given to respondents. 


5. Respondent no.1 filed reply to the claim petition maintaining therein that the workman has not come to the 
Court with clean hands as she was appointed as sweeper by respondent no.2 (contractor) at minimum wage rate as 
prescribed by the Central Government. As per Government Policy dated 18.03.2011 (Ex.R-1), Non-Cantonment 
Military Stations have been authorized to outsource conservancy safaiwala. The answering respondent entered into 
contract with various private agencies for outsourcing of sweepers (safai karamcharis). In July 2018, the answering 
respondent entered into contract with M/s AA Foundation for safety and M/s Sunrise Facilitator Pvt. Ltd. through e- 
tendering for outsourcing of safai karamcharis to provide conservancy services in Chandi Mandir Military Station for 
the financial year 2018-19. Respondent no.2 submits bill for payment for the days the safai karamcharis performed 
their duties and answering respondent is only responsible to forward these bills to PCDA Western Command 
Chandigarh to release payment directly to the contractor’s account. Salary is paid to the workman by the contractor. 
Compliance of PF, ESI Rules etc. is carried out by the contractor as per contract agreement. Answering respondent 
has no responsibility of disbursement of wages and deduction of EPF and ESI etc. The workman and other safai 
karamcharis formed union in the headquarter, which is not permitted and also carried out demonstration inside the 
premises of Station Headquarter on 27.10.2018 and failed to perform his duties. Thereafter, answering respondent 
asked respondent no.2 to ask the workers to perform their duties. Answering respondent has no role or responsibility 
for termination services of the workman. There is no relationship of employer and employee between the answering 
respondent and the workman. In view of Army Headquarter Letter, the civilian employees of the answering 
respondent cannot organize themselves into trade unions as station headquarter is an administrative office of the 
Government of India. In spite of that, workman along with other safai karamcharis formed union unauthorizedly and 
indulge in unlawful activities. It is maintained that workman is the employee of contractor and therefore, termination 
is the prerogative of the contractor. It is prayed that the claim be dismissed. 


6. Respondent no.2 also filed reply maintaining therein that workman worked with it w.e.f. 05.07.2018 to 
27.10.2018. Respondent no.2 has not terminated the services of workman. Workman was paid minimum wages as 
declared by the Centre Government from time to time. Workman himself left the job at his own will without notice. It 
is prayed that the claim may be dismissed. 


Evidence of workman:- 


7. In order to prove his case, workman examined himself as WW1 and tendered into evidence his affidavit 
WWI/A and has been cross examined by the AR for respondent 10.1. AR for workman closed the evidence on 
30.07.2024. 


Evidence of respondent no.1:- 


8. Respondent 10.1 examined Colonel K P Singh S/o Late Colonel P. P. Singh, OIC, Civic Cell, Station Head 
Quarter, Chandi Mandir Panchkula as MW1, who tendered into evidence his affidavit Ex.MWI/A along with 
documents Ex.R1 to Ex.R4. AR for respondent 10.1 closed the evidence on 10.03.2026. 


Sr. No. Document Dated Exhibit 
1. Policy 18.03.2011 Ex.R-1 
2 Agreement with respondent no.2 05.07.2018 Ex.R-2 
3. Copy of ECR of EPFO Ex.R-3 
4 Letter of Head Quarter 22.04.1980 Ex.R-4 
9. It is pertinent to mention here that respondent 10.2 was proceeded ex parte on 20.04.2023. 


Submissions of workman:- 


10. While arguing the case, AR for workman contended that in this case, workman was appointed by respondent 
10.1 under a camouflage agreement with respondent 10.2. The workman was appointed directly by respondent 10.1 
on 01.07.2014 as sweeper and her services were dispensed with on 27.10.2018 without any reason in violation of 
Section 25F, 25G and 25H of the Act as no compensation was paid to the workman and junior to him were retained in 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 9, 2026/वैशाख 19, 1948 4169 


service and fresh appointment were made after termination of the workman. So far as the agreements between the 
respondent 10.1 & 2 are concerned, they are totally not tenable and is unfair labour practice. The work performed by 
the workman is of perennial in nature. The contractor was not having any license. The contractor has left after three 
months. No domestic inquiry was held before terminating the service of the workman, which is mandatory under the 
Act. 


Submissions of respondent no.1:- 


11. On the other hand, AR for respondent 10.1 contended that the workman was engaged by respondent 10.2 
(contractor) as per agreement executed between respondent 10.1 & 2 on outsource basis and there is no relationship 
of employer and employee between respondent 10.1 and the workman. Wages were paid by the respondent 10.2 to 
the workman and there was no role of respondent 10.1 in appointing or terminating the services of the workman. 
Workman is employee of respondent no.2. If there is any violation of provision of the Act, then it is done by 
respondent no.2. As per Ex.R3, all dues were paid by the contractor with regard to EPF and ESI etc. The workman 
and other safai karamcharis formed union in the Headquarter, which is not permitted as per Ex.R4. Respondent no.1 
being the Government Agency, workman is trying to take benefit of his appointment by respondent 10.2 and has filed 
a false claim after concealing material facts and claim of the workman is not maintainable. 


12. Ihave given due consideration to the arguments advanced by AR for both the parties. 


Findings:- 

13. The first contention regarding the applicant is that whether applicant comes within the definition of 
workman as is defined in Section 2(s) of the Act. I may mention that applicant was appointed as sweeper as per his 
claim petition and affidavit submitted before the Tribunal. In plain words the applicant was performing his duties as 
labourer/unskilled worker. She was not in supervisory or administrative post requiring him to perform only 
administrative duties. While interpreting Section 2(s) Hon’ble Supreme Court in the case of Devinder Singh Vs. 
Municipal Council, Sanaur, AIR 2011 Supreme Court 2532,has observed as follows:- 


“The source of employment, the quantum of recruitment, the terms & conditions of employment/ contract of 
service, the quantum of wages/ pay and mode of payment are not at all relevant for deciding whether or not a 
person is a workman within the meaning of Section 2(s) of the Act. The definition of workman also does not 
make any distinction between full time and part time employee or a person appointed on contract basis. 
There is nothing in the plain language of Section 2(s) from which it can be inferred that only person 
employed on regular basis or a person employed for doing whole time job is a workman and the one 
employed on temporary, part time or contract basis on fixed wages or as a casual employee or for doing duty 
for fixed hours is not a workman.” 


14. Thus Hon’ble Supreme Court has clarified that the definition of workman also does not make any distinction 
between full time or part time employee or a person appointed on contract basis. There is nothing in plain language of 
Section 2(s) from which it can be infer that only person employed on regular basis or a person employed for doing 
whole time job is a workman and the one employed on temporary, part time or contract basis on fixed wages or as a 
casual employee or for doing duty for fixed hours is not a workman. In view of the ratio of law enunciated in the 
above ruling, in my considered opinion the applicant herein admittedly falls within the definition of ‘workman’ under 
Section 2(s) of the Act. 


15. The Hon’ble Supreme Court after analyzing the catena of cases has laid down in Balwant Raj Saluja Vs. Air 
India Limited in Civil Appeal No.10266 dated 25.08.2014, two well recognized tests to find out whether the labours 
are the contract employees of the principal employer are:- 


1) Whether the principal employer pays the salary instead of contractor and 
2) Whether the principal employer controls and supervise the work of the employees? 


16. The facts regarding the payment of salary by the management or contractor has not been stated in the claim 
petition of the workman. In fact, claim petition is totally silent regarding the payment of wages, salary, letter of 
appointment by respondent 10.1 or by contractor or anything likewise. Thus, this basic feature for holding the 
relationship of employer and employee i.e. between respondent 10.1 and workman totally lacking not in the pleading 
but also in the evidence submitted by the workman. However, MW1 Colonel K P Singh in his cross examination has 
stated that it is responsibility of the contractor to pay monthly wages to the conservancy staff provided by him. 
Respondent 10.1 has nothing to do with the minimum wages, DC rate to the conservancy staff. Contractor is 
responsible to give notice to the conservancy staff provided by him. Respondent 10.1 has no roll in terminating the 
services of the workman. Thus, it was the respondent no.2 who was required to ensure the payment of wages to the 
workers engaged by him from time to time. 


17. It is also added here that respondent no.1 has also placed on record documents Ex.R-1 to Ex.R-4. Ex.R-2 is 
the contract agreement between respondent 10.1 & 2. Thus, from the adduced evidence, it emerges that workman was 
paid salary by the contractor and she was being controlled and supervised by the contractor. Thus, there does not exist 
any relationship of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.1. However, there exists relation 
of employee and employer between the workman and respondent 10.2. 


4170 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 9, 2026/VAISAKHA 19, 1948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


18. It is also added here that as per definition of contractor as given in the Section 2(c) of the Contract Labour 
(Regulation and Abolition) Act, 1970 (hereinafter called as Contract Act), respondent no.2 was supplying contract 
labour to the respondent no.1. Thus, respondent no.2 would fall under the definition of contractor. Section 2(c) of the 
Contract Act is reproduced as under: 


“Contractor”, in relation to an establishment, means a person who undertakes to produce a given result for the 
establishment, other than a mere supply of goods or articles of manufacture to such establishment, through contract 
labour or who supplies contract labour for any work of the establishment and includes a sub-contractor; 


19. The Hon’ble Apex Court in the case of Banglore Water Supply & Sewerage Board Vs. A. Rajappa 
1978(36) FLR 266 dealt at length with the ambit and scope of expression “industry” as defined in Section 2(j) of the 


Act, and has laid down triple test for determining whether a particular establishment is industry or not. The triple test 
is where (a) systematic activity (b) organized by cooperation between employer and employee (the direct and 
substantial element is commercial (c) for the production or distribution of goods and services to calculated to satisfy 
human wants and wishes prima facie is an “Industry” in that enterprise. The workman was doing work i.e. systematic 
activity for respondent no.1 at the asking of respondent no.2 and there was cooperation between the workman and 
respondent no.2, which involve substantial commercial element for rendering service to satisfy human wants. Thus, 
respondent no.2 would be termed as industry. 


20. Workman in her deposition has clearly stated that there was non-compliance of Section 25F, 25G and 25H 
of the Act. She also stated that she was appointed on 01.07.2014 and she was paid minimum wages prescribed by 
Central Government and was working with respondent 10.1. She was not given any compensation before terminating 
his services on 27.10.2018. In his cross examination, she has further stated that her salary used to be credited in his 
bank account. But she has not placed on record any bank account, in which her salary was credited in order to enable 
this Court to see who was making payment to the workman. However, there is no denial that the workman was paid 
salary by respondent no.2 (contractor). However, from the agreement dated 05.07.2018 (Ex.R-2) placed on file by 
respondent no.1, it emerges that safai karamcharis were provided by respondent no.2 (contractor) to work with 
respondent no.1. Thus, workman was employee of respondent no.2 and not of respondent no.1 as has been discussed 
above. There does not exist any relationship of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.1. 
However, there exists relation of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.2. 


21. Admittedly, there is non-compliance of Section 25F of the Act by respondent no.2. However, as regards 
violation of other provisions i.e. Section 25G and 25H of the Act, there is no evidence lead by the workman. Hence, 
workman is entitled for compensation from respondent 10.2. 


22, It is also added here that there is no iota of evidence that any unfair labour practice used by respondent no.1 
by entering into contract with contractors for supply of safai karamcharis and agreement executed between 
respondent 10.1 and 2 cannot be said to be any unfair labour practice. So far as contentions of AR for workman that 
the work performed by the workman is of perennial in nature and no domestic inquiry was held before terminating 
the services of workman are concerned, in this regard, it is pointed out that workman was entitled only for 
compensation under Section 25F of the Act by respondent no.2 (contractor) and no domestic inquiry was required to 
be conducted. Merely because work is of perennial in nature does not mean that any right has accrued in favour of the 
workman to get employment with respondent no.1, which is a department of Civil Defence run by Government of 
India and for that regular recruitment procedure is provided. Workman cannot seek backdoor entry into service. 
Moreover, workman is not employee of respondent 10.1 being employee of respondent no.2 (contractor). 


23. In view of above discussion, it is held that since respondent no.2 (contractor) is currently not working with 
respondent 10.1, so keeping in view the tenure of service rendered by the workman and salary given to him during the 
period of his stay with the contractor and non-compliance of Section 25F of the Act, this Tribunal is of the considered 
opinion that workman is entitled for compensation. 


24. Keeping in view the fact and circumstances of the present case, the following scheme of compensation is 

deemed fit by this Tribunal: 

1. Workman who worked for less than 2 years with contractor shall be entitled for Rs.20,000/-. 

il. Workman who worked for more than 2 years but less than 5 years with contractor shall be entitled 
Rs.30,000/-. 

ili. Workman who worked for more than 5 years with contractors shall be entitled for Rs.50,000/-. 


25. Present workman employed on 01.07.2014 and was terminated on 27.10.2018 (More than 2 years and less 
than 5 years), so she is entitled for Rs.30,000/-. 

26. Respondent no.2 is directed to pay the workman Rs.30,000/- within one month from the date of the 
notification of the award failing which the respondent 10.2 shall further pay interest @6% per annum from the date of 
award till the date of making payment. It is also added here that in case respondent 10.1 requires safai karamcharis, 
then workman may be engaged through any contractor on sympathetic ground. 

27. Let copy of this award be sent to Central Government for publication as required under Section 17 of the 
Act. 


KAMAL KANT, PO-cum-Link Officer 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(1)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 9, 2026/वैशाख 19, 1948 4171 


नई दिल्‍ली, 5 मई, 2026

प्रशासनिक कमांडर, स्टेशन मुख्यालय (नागरिक सुरक्षा), पंचकुला के प्रबंधतत्र, संबद्ध नियोजकों और उनके कर्मकारों के बीच

ll; Workman has filed the present claim petition under Section 2A of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 


(hereinafter called the Act) against his termination w.e.f. 27.10.2018 claiming reinstatement into service along with 
continuity of service and full back wages.
