on 18.10.2016 by respondent 10.1 at minimum wages prescribed by the Central Government. But the said minimum 
wages were never paid to him. Management has unlawfully deducted 12% EPF share, 1.75% ESI and Rs.300/- as 
TDS from hissalary despite of the fact that his total wages comes under the ambit of taxfree limit. He was working 
under respondent 10.1 directly, but respondent 10.1 in order to avoid its liability always shows his employment in 
record under different contractors, which tantamount to unfair labour practice.

demand for non-deduction of TDS from the wages paid by respondent 10.1. However, their demand was not

appointments after termination of his services and no compensation was paid under Section 25F of the Act. Thus,

4. Notice of the petition was given to respondents. 


4172 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 9, 2026/VAISAKHA 19, 1948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)]

Court with clean hands as he was appointed as sweeper by respondent no.2 (contractor) at minimum wage rate as

documents Ex.R1 to Ex.R4. AR for respondent 10.1 closed the evidence on 10.03.2026.

Submissions of workman:- 


10. While arguing the case, AR for workman contended that in this case, workman was appointed by respondent 
10.1 under a camouflage agreement with respondent 10.2. The workman was appointed directly by respondent 10.1 
on 18.10.2016 as sweeper and his services were dispensed with on 27.10.2018 without any reason in violation of

(contractor) as per agreement executed between respondent 10.1 & 2 on outsource basis and there is no relationship 
of employer and employee between respondent 10.1 and the workman. Wages were paid by the respondent 10.2 to 


[भाग 1----खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 9, 2026/वैशाख 19, 1948 4173 


the workman and there was no role of respondent 10.1 in appointing or terminating the services of the workman. 
Workman is employee of respondent no.2. If there is any violation of provision of the Act, then it is done by 
respondent no.2. As per Ex.R3, all dues were paid by the contractor with regard to EPF and ESI etc. The workman 
and other safai karamcharis formed union in the Headquarter, which is not permitted as per Ex.R4. Respondent no.1 
being the Government Agency, workman is trying to take benefit of his appointment by respondent no.2 and has filed 
a false claim after concealing material facts and claim of the workman is not maintainable. 


12. I have given due consideration to the arguments advanced by AR for both the parties. 


Findings:- 

13. The first contention regarding the applicant is that whether applicant comes within the definition of 
workman as is defined in Section 2(s) of the Act. I may mention that applicant was appointed as sweeper as per his 
claim petition and affidavit submitted before the Tribunal. In plain words the applicant was performing his duties as 
labourer/unskilled worker. He was not in supervisory or administrative post requiring him to perform only 
administrative duties. While interpreting Section 2(s) Hon’ble Supreme Court in the case of Devinder Singh Vs. 
Municipal Council, Sanaur, AIR 2011 Supreme Court 2532,has observed as follows:- 


“The source of employment, the quantum of recruitment, the terms & conditions of employment contract of 
service, the quantum of wages/ pay and mode of payment are not at all relevant for deciding whether or not a 
person is a workman within the meaning of Section 2(s) of the Act. The definition of workman also does not 
make any distinction between full time and part time employee or a person appointed on contract basis. 
There is nothing in the plain language of Section 2(s) from which it can be inferred that only person 
employed on regular basis or a person employed for doing whole time job is a workman and the one 
employed on temporary, part time or contract basis on fixed wages or as a casual employee or for doing duty 
for fixed hours is not a workman.” 


14. Thus Hon’ble Supreme Court has clarified that the definition of workman also does not make any distinction 
between full time or part time employee or a person appointed on contract basis. There is nothing in plain language of 
Section 2(s) from which it can be infer that only person employed on regular basis or a person employed for doing 
whole time job is a workman and the one employed on temporary, part time or contract basis on fixed wages or as a 
casual employee or for doing duty for fixed hours is not a workman. In view of the ratio of law enunciated in the 
above ruling, in my considered opinion the applicant herein admittedly falls within the definition of ‘workman’ under 
Section 2(s) of the Act. 


15. The Hon’ble Supreme Court after analyzing the catena of cases has laid down in Balwant Raj Saluja Vs. Air 
India Limited in Civil Appeal No.10266 dated 25.08.2014, two well recognized tests to find out whether the labours 
are the contract employees of the principal employer are:- 


1) Whether the principal employer pays the salary instead of contractor and 
2) Whether the principal employer controls and supervise the work of the employees? 


16. The facts regarding the payment of salary by the management or contractor has not been stated in the claim 
petition of the workman. In fact, claim petition is totally silent regarding the payment of wages, salary, letter of 
appointment by respondent 10.1 or by contractor or anything likewise. Thus, this basic feature for holding the 
relationship of employer and employee i.e. between respondent no.1 and workman totally lacking not in the pleading 
but also in the evidence submitted by the workman.However, MW1 Colonel K P Singh in his cross examination has 
stated that it is responsibility of the contractor to pay monthly wages to the conservancy staff provided by him. 
Respondent 10.1 has nothing to do with the minimum wages, DC rate to the conservancy staff. Contractor is 
responsible to give notice to the conservancy staff provided by him. Respondent 10.1 has no roll in terminating the 
services of the workman. Thus, it was the respondent no.2 who was required to ensure the payment of wages to the 
workers engaged by him from time to time. 


17. It is also added here that respondent no.1 has also placed on record documents Ex.R-1 to Ex.R-4. Ex.R-2 is 
the contract agreement between respondent no.1 & 2. Thus, from the adduced evidence, it emerges that workman was 
paid salary by the contractor and he was being controlled and supervised by the contractor. Thus, there does not exist 
any relationship of employee and employer between the workman and respondent 10.1. However, there exists relation 
of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.2. 


18. It is also added here that as per definition of contractor as given in the Section 2(c) of the Contract Labour 
(Regulation and Abolition) Act, 1970 (hereinafter called as Contract Act), respondent no.2 was supplying contract 
labour to the respondent 10.1. Thus, respondent no.2 would fall under the definition of contractor. Section 2(c) of the 
Contract Act is reproduced as under: 


“Contractor”, in relation to an establishment, means a person who undertakes to produce a given result for the 
establishment, other than a mere supply of goods or articles of manufacture to such establishment, through 
contract labour or who supplies contract labour for any work of the establishment and includes a sub- 
contractor; 


4174 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 9, 2026/VAISAKHA 19, 1948 [PART II—SEc. 3(ii)] 
19. TheHon’ble Apex Court in the case of Banglore Water Supply & Sewerage Board Vs. A. Rajappa 1978(36) 


FLR 266 dealt at length with the ambit and scope of expression “industry” as defined in Section 2(j) of the Act, and 
has laid down triple test for determining whether a particular establishment is industry or not. The triple test is where 
(a) systematic activity (b) organized by cooperation between employer and employee (the direct and substantial 
element is commercial (c) for the production or distribution of goods and services to calculated to satisfy human 
wants and wishes prima facie is an “Industry” in that enterprise. The workman was doing work i.e. systematic activity 
for respondent 10.1 at the asking of respondent 10.2 and there was cooperation between the workman and respondent 
no.2, which involve substantial commercial element for rendering service to satisfy human wants. Thus, respondent 
no.2 would be termed as industry. 


20. Workman in his deposition has clearly stated that there was non-compliance of Section 25F, 25G and 25H of 
the Act. He also stated that he was appointed on 18.10.2016 and he was paid minimum wages prescribed by Central 
Government and was working with respondent 10.1. He was not given any compensation before terminating his 
services on 27.10.2018. In his cross examination, he has further stated that his salary used to be credited in his bank 
account. But he has not placed on record any bank account, in which his salary was credited in order to enable this 
Court to see who was making payment to the workman. However, there is no denial that the workman was paid salary 
by respondent no.2 (contractor). However, from the agreement dated 05.07.2018 (Ex.R-2) placed on file by 
respondent no.1, it emerges that safai karamcharis were provided by respondent no.2 (contractor) to work with 
respondent 10.1. Thus, workman was employee of respondent no.2 and not of respondent 10.1 as has been discussed 
above. There does not exist any relationship of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.1. 
However, there exists relation of employee and employer between the workman and respondent 10.2. 


21. Admittedly, there is non-compliance of Section 25F of the Act by respondent no.2. However, as regards 
violation of other provisions i.e. Section 25G and 25H of the Act, there is no evidence lead by the workman. Hence, 
workman is entitled for compensation from respondent 10.2. 


22. It is also added here that there is no iota of evidence that any unfair labour practice used by respondent no.1 
by entering into contract with contractors for supply of safai karamcharis and agreement executed between 
respondent 10.1 and 2 cannot be said to be any unfair labour practice. So far as contentions of AR for workman that 
the work performed by the workman is of perennial in nature and no domestic inquiry was held before terminating 
the services of workman are concerned, in this regard, it is pointed out that workman was entitled only for 
compensation under Section 25F of the Act by respondent no.2 (contractor) and no domestic inquiry was required to 
be conducted. Merely because work is of perennial in nature does not mean that any right has accrued in favour of the 
workman to get employment with respondent 10.1, which is a department of Civil Defence run by Government of 
India and for that regular recruitment procedure is provided. Workman cannot seek backdoor entry into service. 
Moreover, workman is not employee of respondent 10.1 being employee of respondent 10.2 (contractor). 


23. In view of above discussion, it is held that since respondent no.2 (contractor) is currently not working with 
respondent 10.1, so keeping in view the tenure of service rendered by the workman and salary given to him during the 
period of his stay with the contractor and non-compliance of Section 25F of the Act, this Tribunal is of the considered 
opinion that workman is entitled for compensation. 


24. Keeping in view the fact and circumstances of the present case, the following scheme of compensation is 
deemed fit by this Tribunal: 


1. Workman who worked for less than 2 years with contractor shall be entitled for Rs.20,000/-. 

il. Workman who worked for more than 2 years but less than 5 years with contractor shall be entitled 
Rs.30,000/-. 

ili. Workman who worked for more than 5 years with contractors shall be entitled for Rs.50,000/-. 

25. Present workman employed on 18.10.2016 and was terminated on 27.10.2018 (more than 2 years and less 


than 5 years), so he is entitled for Rs.30,000/-. 


26. Respondent no.2 is directed to pay the workman Rs.30,000/- within one month from the date of the 
notification of the award failing which the respondent 10.2 shall further pay interest @6% per annum from the date of 
award till the date of making payment. It is also added here that in case respondent 10.1 requires safai karamcharis, 
then workman may be engaged through any contractor on sympathetic ground. 


27. Let copy of this award be sent to Central Government for publication as required under Section 17 of the 
Act. 


KAMAL KANT, PO-cum-Link Officer 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(1)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 9, 2026/वैशाख 19, 1948 4175 


नई दिल्‍ली, 5 मई, 2026

प्रशासनिक कमांडर, स्टेशन मुख्यालय (नागरिक सुरक्षा), पंचकुला के प्रबंधतत्र, संबद्ध नियोजकों और उनके कर्मकारों के बीच 


अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट औद्योगिक विवाद में केन्द्रीय सरकार औद्योगिक अधिकरण/»श्रम न्यायालय चंडीगढ़-1 के 
Gare (47/2024) प्रकाशित करती है। 


[सं. एल- 12025/01/2026-आईआर (बी-1)-106] 
सलोनी, उप निदेशक 
New Delhi, the 5th May, 2026

Government hereby publishes the Award (Ref. 47/2024) of the Cent.Govt.Indus.Tribunal-cum-Labour Court 
Chandigarh-I_ as shown in the Annexure, in the industrial dispute between the management of Admn. Commander 
Station Head Quarter ( Civil Defence), Panchkula and their workmen. 


[No. L-12025/01/2026- IR (B-I)-106] 
SALONI, Dy. Director

IN THE CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL-CUM-LABOUR COURT-I,

Harikrishan S/o Sh. Gyan Chand R/o Beer Ghaghar, Chandi Mandir, Panchkula, Haryana. 


wannnnn- Workman 
Versus 
1. Admn. Commander Station Head Quarter (Civil Defence), Chandi Mandir Cantt. Panchkula, Haryana. 
2. M/s Sun Rise Facilitator Pvt. Ltd., 2253/1 ond Floor, Pipli Wala Town, Mani Mazra, Chandigarh. 
----Respondents 
Present:-Sh. Jang Bahadur, AR for workman. 
Sh. Satinder Singh, AR for respondent no.1. 
Respondent no.2 already ex parte. 
Award :10.03.2026 

1. Workman has filed the present claim petition under Section 2A of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 


(hereinafter called the Act) against his termination w.e.f. 27.10.2018 claiming reinstatement into service along with 
continuity of service and full back wages. 


2. It is maintained by the workman in the claim petition that that he was appointed as sweeper/safai karamchari 
on 20.10.2016 by respondent 10.1 at minimum wages prescribed by the Central Government. But the said minimum 
wages were never paid to him. Management has unlawfully deducted 12% EPF share, 1.75% ESI and Rs.300/- as 
TDS from hissalary despite of the fact that his total wages comes under the ambit of taxfree limit. He was working 
under respondent 10.1 directly, but respondent 10.1 in order to avoid its liability always shows his employment in 
record under different contractors, which tantamount to unfair labour practice. 


3. It is further maintained that workman including 200 other safai karamcharis of respondent no.1 raised the 
demand for non-deduction of TDS from the wages paid by respondent 10.1. However, their demand was not 
accepted. Other workers working under respondent 10.1 formed union and subsequently his services were dispensed 
with w.e.f. 27.10.2018. It is also maintained that respondent 10.1 retained juniors in service and also made fresh 
appointments after termination of his services and no compensation was paid under Section 25F of the Act. Thus, 
there is violation of Section 25F, 25G and 25H of the Act. Hence this petition. 


4. Notice of the petition was given to respondents. 


4176 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 9, 2026/VAISAKHA 19, 1948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


5. Respondent no.1 filed reply to the claim petition maintaining therein that the workman has not come to the 
Court with clean hands as he was appointed as sweeper by respondent no.2 (contractor) at minimum wage rate as 
prescribed by the Central Government. As per Government Policy dated 18.03.2011 (Ex.R-1), Non-Cantonment 
Military Stations have been authorized to outsource conservancy safaiwala. The answering respondent entered into 
contract with various private agencies for outsourcing of sweepers (safai karamcharis). In July 2018, the answering 
respondent entered into contract with M/s AA Foundation for safety and M/s Sunrise Facilitator Pvt. Ltd. through e- 
tendering for outsourcing of safai karamcharis to provide conservancy services in ChandiMandir Military Station for 
the financial year 2018-19. Respondent no.2 submits bill for payment for the days the safai karamcharis performed 
their duties and answering respondent is only responsible to forward these bills to PCDA Western Command 
Chandigarh to release payment directly to the contractor’s account. Salary is paid to the workman by the contractor. 
Compliance of PF, ESI Rules etc. is carried out by the contractor as per contract agreement. Answering respondent 
has no responsibility of disbursement of wages and deduction of EPF and ESI etc. The workman and other safai 
karamcharis formed union in the headquarter, which is not permitted and also carried out demonstration inside the 
premises of Station Headquarter on 27.10.2018 and failed to perform his duties. Thereafter, answering respondent 
asked respondent no.2 to ask the workers to perform their duties. Answering respondent has no role or responsibility 
for termination services of the workman. There is no relationship of employer and employee between the answering 
respondent and the workman. In view of Army Headquarter Letter, the civilian employees of the answering 
respondent cannot organize themselves into trade unions as station headquarter is an administrative office of the 
Government of India. In spite of that, workman along with other safai karamcharis formed union unauthorizedly and 
indulge in unlawful activities. It is maintained that workman is the employee of contractor and therefore, termination 
is the prerogative of the contractor. It is prayed that the claim be dismissed. 


6. Respondent no.2 also filed reply maintaining therein that workman worked with it w.e.f. 05.07.2018 to 
27.10.2018. Respondent no.2 has not terminated the services of workman. Workman was paid minimum wages as 
declared by the Centre Government from time to time. Workman himself left the job at his own will without notice. It 
is prayed that the claim may be dismissed. 


Evidence of workman:- 


7. In order to prove his case, workman examined himself as WW1 and tendered into evidence his affidavit 
WWI/A and has been cross examined by the AR for respondent 10.1. AR for workman closed the evidence on 
30.07.2024. 


Evidence of respondent no.1:- 


8. Respondent 10.1 examined Colonel K P Singh S/o Late Colonel P. P. Singh, OIC, Civic Cell, Station Head 
Quarter, Chandi Mandir Panchkula as MW1, who tendered into evidence his affidavit Ex.MW1/A along with 
documents Ex.R1 to Ex.R4. AR for respondent 10.1 closed the evidence on 10.03.2026. 


Sr. No. Document Dated Exhibit 
1. Policy 18.03.2011 Ex.R-1 
2 Agreement with respondent no.2 05.07.2018 Ex.R-2 
3. Copy of ECR of EPFO Ex.R-3 
4 Letter of Head Quarter 22.04.1980 Ex.R-4 
9. It is pertinent to mention here that respondent 10.2 was proceeded ex parte on 20.04.2023. 


Submissions of workman:- 


10. While arguing the case, AR for workman contended that in this case, workman was appointed by respondent 
10.1 under a camouflage agreement with respondent 10.2. The workman was appointed directly by respondent 10.1 
on 20.10.2016 as sweeper and his services were dispensed with on 27.10.2018 without any reason in violation of 
Section 25F, 25G and 25H of the Act as no compensation was paid to the workman and junior to him were retained in 
service and fresh appointment were made after termination of the workman. So far as the agreements between the 
respondent 10.1 & 2 are concerned, they are totally not tenable and is unfair labour practice. The work performed by 
the workman is of perennial in nature. The contractor was not having any license. The contractor has left after three 
months. No domestic inquiry was held before terminating the service of the workman, which is mandatory under the 
Act. 


Submissions of respondent no.1:- 


11. On the other hand, AR for respondent 10.1 contended that the workman was engaged by respondent 10.2 
(contractor) as per agreement executed between respondent 10.1 & 2 on outsource basis and there is no relationship 
of employer and employee between respondent 10.1 and the workman. Wages were paid by the respondent 10.2 to 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 9, 2026/वैशाख 19, 1948 4177 


the workman and there was no role of respondent 10.1 in appointing or terminating the services of the workman. 
Workman is employee of respondent no.2. If there is any violation of provision of the Act, then it is done by 
respondent no.2. As per Ex.R3, all dues were paid by the contractor with regard to EPF and ESI etc. The workman 
and other safai karamcharis formed union in the Headquarter, which is not permitted as per Ex.R4. Respondent no.1 
being the Government Agency, workman is trying to take benefit of his appointment by respondent 10.2 and has filed 
a false claim after concealing material facts and claim of the workman is not maintainable. 


12. I have given due consideration to the arguments advanced by AR for both the parties. 


Findings:- 

13. The first contention regarding the applicant is that whether applicant comes within the definition of 
workman as is defined in Section 2(s) of the Act. I may mention that applicant was appointed as sweeper as per his 
claim petition and affidavit submitted before the Tribunal. In plain words the applicant was performing his duties as 
labourer/unskilled worker. He was not in supervisory or administrative post requiring him to perform only 
administrative duties. While interpreting Section 2(s) Hon’ble Supreme Court in the case of Devinder Singh Vs. 
Municipal Council, Sanaur, AIR 2011 Supreme Court 2532,has observed as follows:- 


“The source of employment, the quantum of recruitment, the terms & conditions of employment/ contract of 
service, the quantum of wages/ pay and mode of payment are not at all relevant for deciding whether or not a 
person is a workman within the meaning of Section 2(s) of the Act. The definition of workman also does not 
make any distinction between full time and part time employee or a person appointed on contract basis. 
There is nothing in the plain language of Section 2(s) from which it can be inferred that only person 
employed on regular basis or a person employed for doing whole time job is a workman and the one 
employed on temporary, part time or contract basis on fixed wages or as a casual employee or for doing duty 
for fixed hours is not a workman.” 


14. Thus Hon’ble Supreme Court has clarified that the definition of workman also does not make any distinction 
between full time or part time employee or a person appointed on contract basis. There is nothing in plain language of 
Section 2(s) from which it can be infer that only person employed on regular basis or a person employed for doing 
whole time job is a workman and the one employed on temporary, part time or contract basis on fixed wages or as a 
casual employee or for doing duty for fixed hours is not a workman. In view of the ratio of law enunciated in the 
above ruling, in my considered opinion the applicant herein admittedly falls within the definition of ‘workman’ under 
Section 2(s) of the Act. 


15. The Hon’ble Supreme Court after analyzing the catena of cases has laid down in Balwant Raj Saluja Vs. Air 
India Limited in Civil Appeal No.10266 dated 25.08.2014, two well recognized tests to find out whether the labours 
are the contract employees of the principal employer are:- 


1) Whether the principal employer pays the salary instead of contractor and 
2) Whether the principal employer controls and supervise the work of the employees? 


16. The facts regarding the payment of salary by the management or contractor has not been stated in the claim 
petition of the workman. In fact, claim petition is totally silent regarding the payment of wages, salary, letter of 
appointment by respondent 10.1 or by contractor or anything likewise. Thus, this basic feature for holding the 
relationship of employer and employee i.e. between respondent no.1 and workman totally lacking not in the pleading 
but also in the evidence submitted by the workman.However, MW1 Colonel K P Singh in his cross examination has 
stated that it is responsibility of the contractor to pay monthly wages to the conservancy staff provided by him. 
Respondent 10.1 has nothing to do with the minimum wages, DC rate to the conservancy staff. Contractor is 
responsible to give notice to the conservancy staff provided by him. Respondent 10.1 has no roll in terminating the 
services of the workman. Thus, it was the respondent no.2 who was required to ensure the payment of wages to the 
workers engaged by him from time to time. 


17. It is also added here that respondent no.1 has also placed on record documents Ex.R-1 to Ex.R-4. Ex.R-2 is 
the contract agreement between respondent 10.1 & 2. Thus, from the adduced evidence, it emerges that workman was 
paid salary by the contractor and he was being controlled and supervised by the contractor. Thus, there does not exist 
any relationship of employee and employer between the workman and respondent 10.1. However, there exists relation 
of employee and employer between the workman and respondent 10.2. 


18. It is also added here that as per definition of contractor as given in the Section 2(c) of the Contract Labour 
(Regulation and Abolition) Act, 1970 (hereinafter called as Contract Act), respondent no.2 was supplying contract 
labour to the respondent no.1. Thus, respondent no.2 would fall under the definition of contractor. Section 2(c) of the 
Contract Act is reproduced as under: 


“Contractor”, in relation to an establishment, means a person who undertakes to produce a given result for the 
establishment, other than a mere supply of goods or articles of manufacture to such establishment, through 
contract labour or who supplies contract labour for any work of the establishment and includes a sub- 
contractor; 


4178 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 9, 2026/VAISAKHA 19, 1948 [PART II—SEc. 3(ii)] 
19. TheHon’ble Apex Court in the case of Banglore Water Supply & Sewerage Board Vs. A. Rajappa 1978(36) 


FLR 266 dealt at length with the ambit and scope of expression “industry” as defined in Section 2(j) of the Act, and 
has laid down triple test for determining whether a particular establishment is industry or not. The triple test is where 
(a) systematic activity (b) organized by cooperation between employer and employee (the direct and substantial 
element is commercial (c) for the production or distribution of goods and services to calculated to satisfy human 
wants and wishes prima facie is an “Industry” in that enterprise. The workman was doing work i.e. systematic activity 
for respondent 10.1 at the asking of respondent 10.2 and there was cooperation between the workman and respondent 
no.2, which involve substantial commercial element for rendering service to satisfy human wants. Thus, respondent 
no.2 would be termed as industry. 


20. Workman in his deposition has clearly stated that there was non-compliance of Section 25F, 25G and 25H of 
the Act. He also stated that he was appointed on 20.10.2016 and he was paid minimum wages prescribed by Central 
Government and was working with respondent 10.1. He was not given any compensation before terminating his 
services on 27.10.2018. In his cross examination, he has further stated that his salary used to be credited in his bank 
account. But he has not placed on record any bank account, in which his salary was credited in order to enable this 
Court to see who was making payment to the workman. However, there is no denial that the workman was paid salary 
by respondent no.2 (contractor). However, from the agreement dated 05.07.2018 (Ex.R-2) placed on file by 
respondent no.1, it emerges that safai karamcharis were provided by respondent no.2 (contractor) to work with 
respondent no.1. Thus, workman was employee of respondent no.2 and not of respondent no.1 as has been discussed 
above. There does not exist any relationship of employee and employer between the workman and respondent 10.1. 
However, there exists relation of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.2. 


21. Admittedly, there is non-compliance of Section 25F of the Act by respondent no.2. However, as regards 
violation of other provisions i.e. Section 25G and 25H of the Act, there is no evidence lead by the workman. Hence, 
workman is entitled for compensation from respondent 10.2. 


22; It is also added here that there is no iota of evidence that any unfair labour practice used by respondent no.1 
by entering into contract with contractors for supply of safai karamcharis and agreement executed between 
respondent 10.1 and 2 cannot be said to be any unfair labour practice. So far as contentions of AR for workman that 
the work performed by the workman is of perennial in nature and no domestic inquiry was held before terminating 
the services of workman are concerned, in this regard, it is pointed out that workman was entitled only for 
compensation under Section 25F of the Act by respondent no.2 (contractor) and no domestic inquiry was required to 
be conducted. Merely because work is of perennial in nature does not mean that any right has accrued in favour of the 
workman to get employment with respondent no.1, which is a department of Civil Defence run by Government of 
India and for that regular recruitment procedure is provided. Workman cannot seek backdoor entry into service. 
Moreover, workman is not employee of respondent 10.1 being employee of respondent no.2 (contractor). 


23. In view of above discussion, it is held that since respondent no.2 (contractor) is currently not working with 
respondent 10.1, so keeping in view the tenure of service rendered by the workman and salary given to him during the 
period of his stay with the contractor and non-compliance of Section 25F of the Act, this Tribunal is of the considered 
opinion that workman is entitled for compensation. 


24. Keeping in view the fact and circumstances of the present case, the following scheme of compensation is 
deemed fit by this Tribunal: 


1. Workman who worked for less than 2 years with contractor shall be entitled for Rs.20,000/-. 

il. Workman who worked for more than 2 years but less than 5 years with contractor shall be entitled 
Rs.30,000/-. 

ili. Workman who worked for more than 5 years with contractors shall be entitled for Rs.50,000/-. 

25. Present workman employed on 20.10.2016 and was terminated on 27.10.2018 (more than 2 years and less 


than 5 years), so he is entitled for Rs.30,000/-. 


26. Respondent no.2 is directed to pay the workman Rs.30,000/- within one month from the date of the 
notification of the award failing which the respondent 10.2 shall further pay interest @6% per annum from the date of 
award till the date of making payment. It is also added here that in case respondent 10.1 requires safai karamcharis, 
then workman may be engaged through any contractor on sympathetic ground. 


27. Let copy of this award be sent to Central Government for publication as required under Section 17 of the 
Act. 


KAMAL KANT, PO-cum-Link Officer

का.आ. 811.--औद्योगिक विवाद अधिनियम, 1947 (1947 का 14) की धारा 17 के अनुसरण में केन्द्रीय सरकार 
ओरिएंटल बैंक ऑफ कॉमर्स के प्रबंधतत्र, संबद्ध नियोजकों और उनके कर्मकारों के बीच अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट औद्योगिक विवाद में 
केन्द्रीय सरकार औद्योगिक अधिकरण » श्रम न्यायालय जबलपुर के पंचाट (29/2016) प्रकाशित करती है। 
[सं. एल- 12012/70/2015-आईआर (बी-)] 
सलोनी, उप निदेशक 


New Delhi, the 5th May, 2026 


9.0. 811.—In pursuance of Section 17 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central 
Government hereby publishes the Award (Ref. 29/2016) of the Cent.Govt.Indus.Tribunal-cum-Labour Court 
Jabalpur _as shown in the Annexure, in the industrial dispute between the management of_ Oriental Bank of 
Commerce_ and their workmen. 


[No. L-12012/70/2015- IR (B-ID)] 
SALONI, Dy. Director 


ANNEXURE 
THE CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL, 
JABALPUR [M.P.] 
NO. CGIT/LC/ R/29/2016 
Present: P.K.Srivastava 
H.J.S.(Retd.) 
Shri Abhishek Chourasia, 
S/o Shri Anil Chourasia, 
Gulab Ward No. 34, Gali No. 1, 
Chhindwara (M.P.) 
Workman 
Vs 
The Regional Manager, 
Oriental Bank of Commerce, 
Indira Press Complex, Pragati Bhawan, 
Mahrana Pratap Nagar, Zone-I, 
Bhopal (M.P.) 
Management 
(JUDGMENT) 


(Passed on this 10" day of February, 2026) 


As per letter dated 18/02/2016 by the Government of India, Ministry of Labour, New Delhi, the reference 
has been made to this Tribunal under Section-10 of Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (in short the ‘Act’) as per 
Notification No.L-12012/70/2015(IR(B-I1)) dt. 18/02/2016. The dispute under reference relates to:- 


“क्या आवेवक श्री अभिषेक चॉरासिया आत्मज श्री अनित्र चौरसिया दिनांक 1/9/11 से विनांक 15/2/2015 तक 
आओरियण्टन बैंक ऑफ कामर्स की शाखा छिंदवाडा में बतौर दैनिक वेतन भोगी कर्मचारी नियोजित थे, ale हां तो क्या 
शाया प्रबंधक TART आवेवक श्री अभिषेक चॉरासिया को बिना किसी पूर्व TIA HR के विनांक 16/2/15 से BAT से 
हटाना न्यायसंगत हैं? यवि नहीं तो HATH क्या अनुतोष पाने का अधिकारी हैं? " 


After registering the case on the basis of reference, notices were issued to the parties. They appeared and 
filed their respective statement of claim in defense. 


4180 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 9, 2026/VAISAKHA 19, 1948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


The case of the workman is mainly that he was appointed as a ‘Messenger’ w.e.f. 01.09.2011 on daily rated 
basis and worked continuously with the Bank till 15.02.2015, when his services were terminated under an oral order 
without notice or compensation. According to the workman union, this action of management is in violation of 
Section 25-F and 25-G of the Act, hence unjust, illegal and arbitrary. He raised a dispute in this respect which could 
not be conciliated, hence this Reference. 


The workman has prayed that, holding termination of his service against the Act, he be held entitled to be 
reinstated with back-wages and benefits and also be held entitled to permanent status. 


Case of the Management Bank is mainly that they are a Public Sector Undertaking and have defined Rules 
for Appointment of staff, Branch Manager is not an Employer to employ any staff. The workman was never appointed 
against any permanent vacancy of Messenger following any recruitment process, there is no post of messenger as 
such in the Bank. According to the Bank, he was a daily rated worker, engaged by Bank on as and when required 
basis, for which he was paid his daily wages. He never completed 240 days in any year; management has requested 
that the reference be answered against the workman. 


In evidence, the workman has filed his affidavit as his Examination-in-chief, he has been cross-examined by 
management Bank, management Bank has also filed affidavit of its witness who has been cross-examined by 
workman side. The workman has also filed and proved Ex-W/1, certificate issued by Branch Manager and Ex-W/2 
which are 76 vouchers for the period between 2014-2015 with regard to payment made by the Bank to workman. 


I have heard argument of Learned Counsel for workman Mr. Sidharth Verma. Learned Counsel for 
management Bank did not appear for argument. Both the parties have filed written submissions which are part of the 
record. I have gone through the written submissions and record as well. 


On perusal of record in light of written submissions, following Issues came up for determination:- 


4) Whether the workman has successfully proved his continuous engagement for 240 days as 
defined under section 25-B of the Act? 

2) Whether the termination of services of workman is in violation of section 25-F & 25-G of the 
Act? 

3) Whether the workman is entitled to any relief? 

Issue No.1, 


Section 25-B of the Act, which defines ‘continuous service’, is being reproduced as follows:- 


“25B. Definition of continuous service.—For the purposes of this Chapter,— 


(1) a workman shall be said to be in continuous service for a period if he is, for that period, 
in uninterrupted service, including service which may be interrupted on account of 
sickness or authorised leave or an accident or a strike which is not illegal, or a lock-out 
or a cessation of work which is not due to any fault on the part of the workman; 


(2) where a workman is not in continuous service within the meaning of clause (1) for a 
period of one year or six months, he shall be deemed to be in continuous service under 
an employer— 


(a) for a period of one year, if the workman, during a period of twelve calendar 
months preceding the date with reference to which calculation is to be made, 
has actually worked under the employer for not less than— 


(i) one hundred and ninety days in the case of a workman employed below 
ground in a mine; and 


(ii) two hundred and forty days, in any other case; 


(b) for a period of six months, if the workman, during a period of six calendar 
months preceding the date with reference to which calculation is to be made, 
has actually worked under the employer for not less than— 


(i) ninety-five days, in the case of a workman employed below ground in a 
mine; and 
(ii) one hundred and twenty days, in any other case.” 


Pleadings of the parties on this issue have been corroborated earlier. The workman has corroborated his 
allegations on this issue in his affidavit as his Examination-in-chief. In his cross-examination he admits that no 
appointment letter was issued to him, and he did not appear in any written or oral examination. He has denied that he 
did not complete 240 days in a year. 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(1)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 9, 2026/वैशाख 19, 1948 4181 


On the other hand, the management witness admits in his cross-examination that he was not posted during 
the period in which the workman has claimed to have been employed by the Bank. He also states that there is no 
record with respect to payment of daily wagers in the Bank and that there is a post of Peon in the Branch but it is not 
known who was working on the post of Peon in the said period. The evidence of the workman is corroborated by 
different vouchers, total 76 in numbers which showed that he was engaged by the Bank for different works for which 
he was paid his wages and his expenses with regard to travelling, etc. These vouchers are for the period 2014-2015. 


The statement of workman is corroborated by documentary evidence. On the other hand, the statement of 
management itself shows that this witness was not posted in the Branch during the period in question. Hence, holding 
the evidence from workman side more reliable, he is held to have proved his case that he worked continuously for 240 
days and more in every year including the year preceding the date of his termination as defined under section 25-B of 
the Act and the Bank is held to be his employer under the Act. 


Issue No. 1 is answered accordingly. 
Issue No. 2, 


Undisputedly, no notice was issued to the workman before his termination nor was he paid any 
compensation. Hence his termination is held in violation of section 25-F and 25-G of the Act. 


Section 25-F & 25-G of the Act, is being reproduced as follows: 
25F. Conditions precedent to retrenchment of workmen. 


No workman employed in any industry who has been in continuous service for not less than one 
year under an employer shall be retrenched by that employer until- 


(a) the workman has been given one month's notice in writing indicating the reasons for 
retrenchment and the period of notice has expired, or the workman has been paid in lieu 
of such notice, wages for the period of the notice; 


(b) the workman has been paid, at the time of retrenchment, compensation which shall be 
equivalent to fifteen days' average pay for every completed year of continuous service or 
any part thereof in excess of six months; and 


(c) notice in the prescribed manner is served on the appropriate Government or such 
authority as may be specified by the appropriate Government by notification in the 
Official Gazette. 


25G ‘Procedure _for_retrenchment.—Where any workman in an industrial establishment, who is a 
citizen of India, is to be retrenched and he belongs to a particular category of workmen in that 
establishment, in the absence of any agreement between the employer and the workman in this 
behalf, the employer shall ordinarily retrench the workman who was the last person to be 
employed in that category, unless for reasons to be recorded the employer retrenches any other 
workman. 


Issue No. 2 stands answered accordingly. 
Issue No. 3 


Undisputedly, the workman was not appointed following recruitment process hence, his engagement cannot 
be said to be legal. Thus, he is not entitled to reinstatement. Keeping in view, the facts and circumstances of the case 
in hand, a lump sum compensation quantified Rs. 1Lac/- in lieu of all his claims including litigation cost will meet the 
ends of justice which the workman is held entitled to be paid by the Bank within 60 days from the date of publication 
of the Award, failing which interest @ 6% p.a. from the date of award till payment. 

Issue No. 3 stands answered accordingly. 


In light of above discussion and findings, the reference is answered as follows.- 
AWARD 


“Holding the action of the management in disengaging the workman Shri Abhishek Chourasia from 
16/02/2015, illegal and unjustified, he is held entitled to a lump sum amount of Rs. 1Lac/- to be paid by the Bank 
within 60 days from the date of publication Award, failing which interest @ 6% p.a. from the date of award till 
payment, in lieu of all his claims.” 


No order as to cost. 
DATE:- 10-02-2026 
P.K. SRIVASTAVA, Presiding Officer 


4182 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 9, 2026/VAISAKHA 19, 1948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 
नई दिल्‍ली, 4 मई, 2026 


का.आ. 812.--औद्योगिक विवाद अधिनियम, 1947 (1947 का 14) की धारा 17 के अनुसरण में, केन्द्रीय 
सरकार एचपीसीएल के प्रबंधतंत्र के संबद्ध नियोजकों और श्री जेरी टेलेस के बीच अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट केन्द्रीय सरकार 
औद्योगिक अधिकरण एवं श्रम न्‍्यायालय-1, मुंबई, पंचाट (रिफरेन्स न.- 18/2018) को जैसा कि अनुलग्नक में दिखाया 


गया है, प्रकाशित करती है जो केन्द्रीय सरकार को सॉफ्ट कॉपी के साथ 04.05.2026 को प्राप्त हुआ था | 
[सं. एल- 30012/23/2017-आईआर(एम)] 


सलोनी, उप निदेशक 


New Delhi, the 4th May, 2026 


S.O. 812.—In pursuance of Section 17 of the Industrial Dispute Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central 
Government hereby publishes the award (Reference No. 18/2018) of the Central Government Industrial Tribunal 
cum Labour Court-1, Mumbai as shown in the Annexure, in the Industrial dispute between the employers in 
relation to M/s Indian Oil Corporation Ltd. and Shri Jerry Teles which was received along with soft copy of the 
award by the Central Government on 04.05.2026. 


[No. L-30012/23/2017-(IR(M)] 
SALONI, Dy. Director 
ANNEXURE 
BEFORE THE CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL NO.1, MUMBAI 
Present 
JUSTICE ARVIND KUMAR MISHRA 
Presiding Officer 
REFERENCE NO.CGIT - 18 of 2018 
Parties: Employers in relation to the management of 
M/s Indian Oil Corporation Ltd. 
V/s. 
Shri Jerry Teles 
Appearances: 
For the first party Management : Mr. S.V. Alva, Adv. 
For the second party workmen : Absent 
Mumbai, dated the 02" day of February, 2026. 
AWARD 


The present reference has been made by the Central Government by its order No. L-30012/23/2017-IR(M) 
dated 17/05/2018 passed in exercise of the powers conferred by clause (d) of section (1) read with sub-section (2A) of 
Section 10 of the Industrial Dispute Act 1947. The terms of reference as per the schedule to the said order are as 
under: 


“Whether the action of the management of Indian Oil Corporation Limited through its General Manager 
(Regional Services)-WR, IOCL, Mumbai in dismissing the workman namely Sh. Jeery Teles, Jr. Operator (F), 
Aviation Fuel Station, Nagpur vide order bearing No. WR.IR/1461/JT dated 08/09/2006 is legal & proper? If 
not, what relief to the workman is entitled to?” 


In view of the above said facts the claimant/workman has not field evidence in support of his case on affidavit, in 
spite of several opportunities given to him and taking into consideration the law as laid by the Hon'ble High Court in 
the case of V. K. Raj Industries v. Labour Court (1) and others 1981 (29) FLR 194 as under: 


“It is well settled that if a party challenges the legality of an order, the burden lies upon him to prove illegality 
of the order and if no evidence is produced the party invoking jurisdiction of the Court must fail. Whenever a 
workman raises a dispute challenging the validity of the termination of service if is imperative for him to file 
written statement before the Industrial Court setting out grounds on which the order is challenged and he must 
also produce evidence to prove his case. If the workman fails to appear or to file written statement or produce 
evidence, the dispute referred by the State Government cannot be answered in favour of the workman and he 
would not be entitled to any relief.” 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(1)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 9, 2026/वैशाख 19, 1948 4183 


In the case of M/s Uptron Powertronics Employees’ Union, Ghaziabad through its Secretary v. Presiding Officer, 
Labour Court (II), Ghaziabad and others 2008 (118) FLR 1164 Hon’ble Allahabad High Court has held as under: 


“The law has been settled by the Apex Court in case of Shanker Chakravarti v. Britannia Biscuit Co. Ltd., V.K. 
Raj Industries v. Labour Court and Ors., Airtech Private Limited v. State of U.P. and Ors. 1984 (49) FLR 38 
and Meritech India Ltd. v. State of U.P. and Ors. 1996 FLR that in the absence of any evidence led by or on 
behalf of the workman the reference is bound to be answered by the court against the workman. In such a 
situation it is not necessary for the employers to lead any evidence at all. The obligation to lead evidence to 
establish an allegation made by a party is on the party making the allegation. The test would be, who would fail 
if no evidence is led.” 


And by the Hon’ble Allahabad High Court in the case of District Administrative Committee, U.P. P.A.C.C.S.C. 
Services v. Secretary-cum-G.M. District Co-operative Bank Ltd. 2010 (126) FLR 519; wherein it has been held as 
under: 


“The submission is that even if the petitioner failed to lead the evidence, burden was on the shoulders of the 
respondent to prove the termination order as illegal. He was required to lead evidence first which he failed. A 
perusal of the impugned award also does not show that any evidence either oral or documentary was led by the 
respondent. In the case of no evidence, the reference has to be dismissed.” 


Mr. S.V. Alva, Learned Counsel for the Management, appeared and he prayed that the matter be dismissed for want 
of prosecution. 


Since claimant/workman has not field evidence in support of his case on affidavit, in spite of several opportunities 
given to him, the claim is liable to be dismissed. 


For the foregoing reasons, the case is dismissed and the workman is not entitled for any relief. 
Accordingly, the reference stands answered in terms aforesaid. 
Award as above. 


Justice ARVIND KUMAR MISHRA, Presiding Officer 


नई दिल्‍ली, 4 मई, 2026

[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(1)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 9, 2026/वैशाख 19, 1948 4185

का.आ. 814.--औद्योगिक विवाद अधिनियम, 1947 (1947 का 14) की धारा 17 के अनुसरण में, केन्द्रीय 
सरकार ऑयल इंडिया लिमिटेड के प्रबंधतंत्र के संबद्ध नियोजकों और श्री नीलमणि साहू के बीच अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट केन्द्रीय

दिखाया गया है, प्रकाशित करती है जो केन्द्रीय सरकार को सॉफ्ट कॉपी के साथ 04.05.2026 को प्राप्त हुआ था |

AWARD 


The Government of India in the Ministry of Labour & Employment in exercising its power conferred under 
clause (d) of sub-section (1) and sub- section (2-A) of Section 10 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947) 
have referred a dispute between the Management of Bay Exploration Project, Oil India Ltd., and their workmen 
vide letter No. L-30012/40/2009 — IR(M), dated 26.03.2010 for its adjudication and the schedule of the reference is as 
follows:- 


“Whether the action of the management of Oil India, Bhubaneswar by terminating the services of Shri 
Nilamani Sahoo, Watchman without taking disciplinary action is legal & or justified? What relief the 
workman 15 entitled to? 


2 The case of the concerned workman as per his statement of claim is as follows:- 


That he was appointed as a Chowkidar on the basis of selection through interview against the permanent 
vacancy with effect from 01.06.1985 and was posted at Bhubaneswar vide Order dated 31.05.1985 of the 
Management. At _ the time of appointment his basic pay was Rs. 310/- per month with D.A. and other allowances as 
applicable to regular employees of the company which was revised from time to time. He was under probation for six 
months and after completion of his probation period his service was confirmed. He had rendered 20 years of 
continuous service under the 17 Party-Management, but he was illegally dismissed from service on 30.06.2006. He 
had been performing his duties sincerely and diligently and he had an unblemished service record, but he came 
to know about his dismissal order from daily news Paper “The Samaj” dated 30.06.2006 which was in violation of 
principle of natural justice as well —_as the mandatory provisions of Industrial Disputes Act. He had not been served 
the order of dismissal and when he approached the Management-company he was given a copy of the said dismissal 
order. He had not been served a charge sheet of any misconduct committed by him and no enquiry was conducted 
against him. The Management had not complied with the provisions of Section 25-F of the I'D. Act. After about two 
years of his dismissal vide letter dated 25.02.2008 he was asked to collect his outstanding dues from the office of the 
Management. His dismissal from service by the 17 Party-Management was _ illegal, arbitrary and void abnitio. The 
1‘ Party-Management has not followed the established principles of law while dismissing him, so he may be 
reinstated वा his service with full back wages and other service benefits. 


There was an illegality in payment of salary to the employees of the 17 Party-Management so the employees 
had raised a demand for payment of their salary in time. The employees demanded their regular payment through the 
Union but to victimize the workers of the 1“ Party-Management filed 1.C.C. case which was registered against him 
and other workmen in the Court of J.M.F.C., Bhubaneswar in which he was granted bail by the Hon’ble High Court 
of Orissa. In the meantime during the pendency of the 1.C.C. case before the Court of $.D.J.M., Bhubaneswar, the 
Management had dismissed him without following the principles of natural justice leaving aside other employees who 
were allegedly named in the above case, so the action of the Management was discriminatory and unsustainable in 
the eye of law. The Management in its dismissal order had mentioned a concocted story that he had assaulted an 
officer in the office of the 17 Party-Management and threatened him with dire consequences if any action was taken 
against him. Moreover, the Management had further mentioned in the said order that being afraid of his threat, no 
employee was prepared to give evidence against him so no domestic enquiry was conducted against him and his 
service was terminated by the order of the competent authority. 


It is the settled principle of law that when a workman is terminated from service it would amount to 
retrenchment under the I.D. Act and the Management is bound to follow the provisions of Section 25-F of the I.D. 
Act, but the Management had not followed the provisions of Section 25-F of the I.D. Act. The facts mentioned in 
the order of dismissal that he had physically assaulted the superior officer and threatened him to cause his death and 
threatened the other workers not to give evidence against him is false and concocted. The Management has taken false 
plea in order to justify its illegal action and to deny the benefits of principle of natural justice to him. After his illegal 
termination he had represented time and again to the higher authorities 


for his reinstatement but his grievance was not taken into consideration. Hence, he raised the dispute before the 
A.L.C.(C), Bhubaneswar. 


A prayer has been made before the Tribunal to declare his dismissal from service as illegal and void with a 
direction to the 1 Party-Management for his reinstatement with full back wages and other service benefits with 
continuity of service. 


[भाग II-—ve 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 9, 2026/वैशाख 19, 1948 4187 
3. On the other-hand the case of the management of Oil India Limited is as follows:- 


That, the 1“ party-Management is a Government of India company doing its endeavour to search for 
Hydrocarbon and/or oil in the North East Coast, Mahanadi Basin Areas so it had set up its base office at 
Bhubaneswar, Orissa with branch at Paradeep. The concerned workman was working as a contract labourer under 
various contractors in the establishment but he was formally interviewed and was appointed as a watchman on the pay 
roll of the 1 Party-Management in the year 1985. After failure of the professional parties engaged by the company 
on global tender basis for drilling out, deposit of Hydro Carbon or oil during 1988 and finding future prospects of any 
successful exercise were bleach, the drilling operation were gradually reduced. After that the Management proposed 
to transfer some of its employees from Bhubaneswar to the field Head Quarter at Duliajan in the state of Assam 
from December, 2005. Some of the employee and all unionized employees of the Bhubaneswar Office as a protest on 
transfer stopped receiving their salary from June, 2006 onwards __ even after advising them to receive salary but the 
employees did not come forward to receive their salary. After five months, all the employees orally approached the 
Management for release their un-received salary but the Management insisted that each employee should give a letter 
of undertaking that they had not received their pay at their own sweet- will and would not to repeat such action in 
future. Inthe mean-time on 13.06.2006 at around 11 A.M. Sri A. Mazumdar the then Manager (F & A) under the 
1“ Party- | Management while working in his office room at IDCO Towner, Janpath heard a huge commotion from 
outside and he immediately came out of his room. After that he found Sri Snehasis Haldar, Cashier standing 
helplessly with open cash office and about fifteen to twenty Unionized staff including the present workman were 
shouting at the top of their voice. The said employees were demanding their arrear salary, cheques without complying 
the conditions stipulated by the Project in-charge Sri A. Majumdar, Manager (F& A). After that Sri A. Mazumdar 
told them that the same could not be paid without compliance of the conditions imposed by the Management. 
Thereafter Sri Mazumdar asked the cashier to close the cash office immediately. In the mean-time Shri J.K. Mishra 
and J.P. Bothaju, who had come thereto for rescue were threatened and abused by the workman and other employees. 
Thereafter the present workman and Sri T. Narayan targeted Sri Majumdar under the leadership of SriN.R. Mallick, 
President of the MPEX Union, Sri D.R. Pattnaik, Sri J.K. Sarangi, Sri T.P. Mohanty and started threatening Sri A. 
Mazumdar in a most abusive language. Subsequently, Sri Mazumdar sought help of Shri S.K. Pattnaik. A.O. (SR) 
who escorted him to leave the office premises but as he moved towards the gate the concerned workman along with 
T. Narayan and other co-employees came there and manhandled him by hitting him with blows on his body and back. 
Further 6 concerned workman and Sri T. Narayan continued to assault Sri Mazumdar with fist and blows and Sri 
Majumdar ran back to his office room out of fear. Sri Mazumdar was confined by the workman and another employee 
in his room — up-to 4.30 P.M. on 13.06.2006 and he was not allowed to go for lunch. Sri Majumdar was on leave 
from 14.06.2006 and 15.06.2006 and on 16.06.2006 on the basis of legal advice he had filed 1.C.C. Case Bearing No. 
1496 dated 17.06.2006 against the concerned workman and others, which was sent to __ police station for registration 
of FIR. Further on 21.06.2006 the concerned workman came to the office and threatened to Sri Mazumdar, while he 
was going home that if the Management would take any action against them then that would the last day of him and 
his family members. Moreover, the | concerned workman threatened to all the co-employees not to give anything in 
writing relating to occurrence to the Management as a witness. The misconduct committed by Sri Nilamani Sahoo and 
T. Narayan were of serious in nature —_— putting the life and liberty of a superior officer in jeopardy, so they did not 
deserve any lenient view from the Management. On the basis of the past experience, the Management realized that the 
workman would be non-cooperative in conducting fact finding enquiry and in view of such situation it was not 
practicable to conduct an enquiry. The conduct of the concerned workman and others employee like threatening the 
superior officer, shouting derogative slogans on each working day had become a regular routine affairs. Moreover, 
Sri Sahu had indulged in violent activities when Sri M.P. Barua Officer of the 17 Party-Management company had 
visited to Bhubaneswar office. The Management after considering that there were every possibilities that the 
concerned workman would resort to assault the officers of the Management __ had decided that neither any useful 
purpose would be served nor any further evidence would come out if a detailed enquiry would be held with regard to 
the charges, so the competent authority keeping in view the larger interest of the company dismissed the concerned 
workman namely Sri Nilamani Sahoo and छा 1. Narayan on 30.06.2006 who were involved in such incident on 
13.06.2006. The order of dismissal was sent to Sri Sahu through Regd. Post with A.D. enclosing there to cheques 
which returned unserved on 21.07.2006. The dismissal order was also sent to the workman Under Certificate of 
Posting on 03.07.2006 and the same was also published in the news-paper “The Sambad” Bhubaneswar edition dated 
05.07.2006. In the mean-time a notice was __ published in the news-paper “The Sambad” on 15.11.2006 intimating the 
employees to receive their salary and in that response all the employees had received their salary on 16.11.2007 but 
the concerned workman gave a letter on 22.11.2006 mentioning false allegation with a prayer to allow him to join in 
his duty immediately. The concerned workman had made correspondence to 06 Management under the RTI Act 
for inspection of records and projects files which was considered by the Management. In the mean-time the learned 
S.D.J.M., Bhubaneswar referred the case bearing No. 1.C.C. 1496/2005 to Police Station and a G.R. Case No. 
2487/2006 was registered. Subsequently Police after investigation had submitted charge-sheet. 


The concerned workman has been dismissed from service of | Management as punishment, so he does not 
come under the definition of retrenchment and consequently Section 25-F of the Industrial Disputes Act is not 
applicable in this case. 


4188 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 9, 2026/VAISAKHA 19, 1948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


The 1* Party-Management by way of his reply has made a prayer that the reference may be answered against 
the disputant-workman. 


4. The 2" Party-workman has filed rejoinder denying all the averments _ made in the written statement by the 
Management. 
5. On the basis of the pleadings of both the parties following issues are framed. 

ISSUES 


i) | Whether the action of the Management of Oil India, Bhubaneswar in terminating the services of Sri Nilamani 
Sahoo, Watchman without taking disciplinary action is legal and justified? 


ii) What relief the workman is entitled to? 


6 The 2" party-workman has examined altogether five witnesses. They are W.W.-1 Sri Nilamani Sahoo 
himself, Sri Nishith Ranjan Mallick as W.W.-2, Sri Krupasindhu Mahalik, as W.W.-3, Sri Jayanta Kumar Mohapatra 
as W.W.-4 and 


Sri Radhamohan Padhi as W.W.-5. The 27 Party-workman has proved the following documents which are marked as 
hereunder:-

No. 883/2018. 


7 On the other-hand the 1” Party-Management has examined four witnesses. They are M.W.-1 Sri Abhijit 
Majumdar, Sr. Manager (F&A), Directorate General Hydrocarbon, Noida, M.W.-2 Sri S.K. Senapati, Head (Legal) 
Corporate Office, Oil India Limited, M.W.-3 Sri Sujit Kumar Patnaik, Addl. Senior Officer (Materials), Oil India 
Limited, Noida& M.W.-4 Sri Anup Kumar Mohanty, Senior Administrative Officer.

FINDINGS 


9. The most important issue in this case is Issue No. II, so the Tribunal thinks 7 proper to discuss this issue 
first of all. 


ISSUE NO. वा 


10. At the outset of discussions, it is relevant to mention here that it is an admitted fact that the concerned 
workman namely Sri Nilamani Sahu had been appointed as Chowkidar after selection through interview against the 
permanent vacancy with effect from 01.06.1985 at Bhubaneswar and after completion of probation his service was 
confirmed. 


11. It is also the admitted fact that the concerned workman namely Sri Nilamani Sahoo had been 
dismissed/removed from the services with effect from 30.06.2006 without holding any domestic enquiry against 
him for the alleged misconduct. 


13. At this stage it is required to mention here that in the dismissal order of छा Nilamani Sahoo which is at 
Ext.-3, it has been mentioned that the | misconduct committed by him is grave and serious in nature putting the life 
and liberty of a superior officer in jeopardy and as such deserves no lenient view from the Management. It has also 
been mentioned that he had taken extreme step of using abusive language and assaulting Sri Mazumdar inside the 
office premises in presence of other employees and such act in itself speaks of his high handed and recalcitrant 
attitude. It has been further noted that the Management is of view that it would be difficult to hold a proper enquiry 
since the witness might not come forward to give frank and true evidence. 


14. It is relevant to mention herewith that there is a Standing Order for the workmen employed in the 
establishment of Oil India Limited which is marked as Ext.-16. The Management had not raised any objection at 
the time of its marking as exhibit by the W.W.-2 (Sri N.R. Mallick). Since no objection had been raised by the 
Management on the copy of the Standing Order for the workman employed in the establishment of Oil India Limited 
(Ext.-16), so it amounts to admission on the part of the Management. 


15. In the Standing Order the provisions of termination of employment/service has been mentioned under 
Section 19 which reads as follows:- 


19.0. Termination of Employment/Service :- 


19.1. | Reasons :- 

19.1.1 A workman’s termination of service will generally fall under one of the following categories:- 
(a) Discharge owing to staff redundancy. 
(b) Discharge on completion of temporary work. 


(c) Discharge on completion of probationary period in respect of new recruits (Gf work is found 
unsatisfactory during the probationary period. 


(d) Discharge on receipt of adverse report about the workman’s efficiency, work, acts or expression 
prejudicial to the company, its management, its business or acts or expression prejudicial to the company, 
its management, its business or its reputation or any adverse official report. 


(e) Discharge for absence from work, without authorized leave or permission for more than fourteen 
consecutive days. 


(f) Any discharge for any misstatement or suppression of facts or furnishing to the Company any false or 
incorrect particulars about himself/herself/family, his/her qualifications or experience or past antecedents or 
conduct at the time of the appointment. 


(g) Discharge or conviction by any Court of Law for a criminal offence involving moral turpitude. 
(h) Discharge on attainment of age of superannuation. 
(i) Discharge on resignation after due notice. 


(j) Discharge for unsatisfactory service. 


4190 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 9, 2026/VAISAKHA 19, 1948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 
(k) Dismissal for any act of misconduct as specified under Section 21. 
Clause (a) to (k) 


Further, Section-9.2 of the Standing Order mentions the procedure for termination of employment/service 
and under Section-9.2.(d) it has been specifically mentioned that the services of the workman shall not be terminated 
(dismissed) for an act of misconduct specified under section 21 of the Standing Orders unless he/she has been given 
an opportunity to explain the charges levelled against him/her and a departmental enquiry is held. Further, under 
section 21 of the Standing Order disciplinary action of misconduct has been mentioned. 


16. Now in the Standing Order of the Management of Oil India Limited there 15 categorical provision that a 
workman shall not be terminated (dismissed) for an act of misconduct unless he has been given an opportunity to 
explain and after holding a departmental enquiry. But in this case the Management had not issued any 
explanation/show cause to the concern workman with regard to his misconduct as mentioned in the dismissal order 
and no disciplinary enquiry was conducted against him for proving the charges of misconduct. 


17. However, at this stage the Tribunal thinks it proper to discuss the oral and documentary evidence of both the 
parties even though no departmental enquiry was conducted against the workman. 


18. The most competent witness in this case on behalf of the workman is the W.W.-1 Sri Nilamani Sahoo. He 
has categorically deposed that he was appointed as a Chowkidar after he was selected through interview against a 
permanent vacancy with effect from 01.06.1985 and was posted at Bhubaneswar. He has also deposed that his 
services were made permanent after completion of probation period, but his service was terminated without holding 
any departmental enquiry. He has proved certain documents which are marked as Ext.-1 to Ext.-7. 


In the cross examination he has deposed that on 13.06.2006 he was present in the office and he was called by 
Sri Mazumdar for receiving salary/payment after giving in writing, but he refused the same. He has denied the 
suggestion that he had misbehaved with Sri Mazumdar and kept him in confinement in a room till 4.30 P.M. He has 
also denied the suggestion that he along with N.R. Mallick, D.R. Patnaik, J.K. Sarangi and T.P. Mohanty had 
assaulted and abused Sri Mazumdar. 


The W.W.-2 Sri N.R. Mallick who is the President of the Mahanadi Petroleum Exploration Employees 
Union has fully corroborated the evidence of the W.W.-1 in his examination in chief and he has proved certain 
documents which are marked as Ext.-8 to Ext.-17. 


In the cross examination he has stated that he was dismissed from service in the year 2004. He has stated that 
he had collected information from the applicant that there was no incident in the office on 13.06.2006. 


The W.W.-3, Krupasindhu Mallick has also supported the evidence adduced by the W.W.-1 in his 
examination in chief. In the cross examination he has deposed that he was not an employee of the Management on 
13.06.2006 and he was driving a tourist vehicle, but he was working as a Driver of a tourist vehicle engaged for Oil 
India Limited. He has also deposed that he was a Driver of Sri Mazumdar and he left him in his office in between 
9.30 A.M. to 1.30 P.M. and there after 3 P.M. to 3.30 P.M. 


The W.W.-4 and W.W.-5 were the employees of the Management of Oil India Limited and they have fully 
supported the case of the concerned workman in their evidence. 


19. On the other-hand the most competent witness on behalf of the 17 Party- Management is the M.W.-1 Sri A. 
Mazumdar. He has deposed that on account of transfer of employees by the Management, the other Unionized 
employees stopped receiving their salary from January, 2006 onwards and the Management had not stopped 
paying their salary to them rather advised them to receive their salary for which a letter was also issued. He has 
further deposed _ that on 13.06.2006 at around 11 A.M. he heard loud sound and disturbing slogans by the 
Unionized workmen resulting in violent commotion in front of the cash office and he informed the agitating 
workers that cheque could not be handed-over to them without submitting undertaking. He has further stated 
that on his advice the Cashier closed the cash box and handed over the keys to him. He has also stated the workman 
and others abused him and the Cashier in presence of Shri J.K. Mishra and Shri H.P. Bothaju. He has also stated 
that the concerned workman and T. Narayan targeted him under the leadership of Sri N.R. Mallick, Sri D.R. 
Pattnaik and Sri T.P. Mohanty who gheraoed and abused him as he was moving towards gate. He has also stated 
that the concerned workman and others repeatedly gave him blows and went on abusing. He has further stated that he 
was on leave from 14.06.2006 to 15.06.2006 and on 16.06.2006 after consultation with the legal officer filed an I.C.C. 
Case No. 1496/2006 before the learned S.D.J.M., Bhubaneswar. He has also deposed that on 21.06.2006 the 
concerned workman and Sri T. Narayan again assaulted him while he was going towards his personal vehicle from his 
office and he was also telephonically threatened by the concerned workman. He has further deposed that the conduct 
of the concerned workman and others were grave in nature —_and considering that no purpose would be served for 
holding departmental enquiry, the concerned workman Sri Nilamani Sahoo was dismissed from service on 
30.06.2006. He has proved certain documents which are in Ext.- A to Ext.- J. 


In the cross examination he has deposed that he had given in writing against the disputant workman for his 
unlawful and rudely behaviour as well as manhandling. He has also deposed that he could not say that if any 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 9, 2026/वैशाख 19, 1948 4191 


departmental proceeding was initiated against him or not for such alleged misconduct. He has further deposed that he 
was the Project, In-charge on 13.06.2006. He has also deposed that he cannot say about the procedure to be adopted 
as per the Certified Standing Order of the 1" Party-Management. He __ has denied the suggestions that the dismissal 
of the concerned workman was totally illegal and unjustified and the action of non-holding of departmental 
proceedings. 


The Management Witness No. 2 Sri S.K. Senapati, M.W.-3 Sri S.K. Pattnaik and Sri Anup Kumar 
Mohanty M.W.-4 have fully supported the case of the Management in their examination in chief and there is 
nothing in their cross examination for discussion here. 


20. It is pertinent to mention here that much has been said about the institution of 1.C.C. Case No. 1406/2006 
which was subsequently converted — into P.S. Case No. 183/2006 and Police had submitted chare-sheet in this case. 
But the Management has not produced the copy of the registration of P.S. Case No. 183/2006 and the charge-sheet. 


21. In absence of the copy of the registration of Police Case and copy of the charge-sheet submitted by Police on 
the I.C.C. case filed by Sri Mazumdar the case of the Management regarding incident on 13.06.2006 does not seem to 
be probable and trust-worthy. 


22. After analysing the evidence of both the parties it is crystal clear that the concerned workman namely Sri 
Nilamani Sahoo had been dismissed from _ service of the Management without holding any departmental enquiry 
which is in violation of the Standing Order of the management of Oil India Limited (Ext.-16). 


23. In view of such, the action of the management in dismissing/terminating the services of concerned workman 
w.e.f. 30.06.2010 is not legal and justified. Hence the concerned workman is entitled for relief. 


Accordingly, the Issue No. I is answered in favour of the 2™ Party- Workman. 
ISSUE NO. वा 


24. The Tribunal has already held (supra) that the action of the Management in dismissing/terminating the 
service of the 2™ party-workman namely Sri Nilamani Sahoo is not legal and justified and the concerned workman is 
definitely entitled for relief sought in his Claim Petition. 


-16- 
25; In view of the above facts and circumstances, the Tribunal passes following:- 
ORDER 


The management of Bay Exploration Project, Oil India Limited, IDCO Tower, Bhubaneswar is directed to 
reinstate the 2™ party-workman Sri Nilamani Sahoo in his services if he is not attained at the age of superannuation 
and also directed to pay all the back wages and consequential service benefits with continuity of service from the date 
of his dismissal and if the concerned workman Sri Nilamani Sahoo has already attained the age of supernation, then 
the Management is directed to pay all the back wages and other consequential benefits from the date of 
dismissal/discharge till his retirement along with all retiral benefits. 


26. This is the Award of the Tribunal. The reference is answered accordingly. 


27. Office is directed to send the copy of the Award to the Ministry of Labour for publishing the Award in the 
official gazette. 


28. Case record be consigned to record room. 
Dictated & Corrected by me. 
DINESH KUMAR SINGH, Presiding Officer

Plot No. 23, Amrapalli, 
Patia, Chandrasekharpur,
