AWARD 


The Government of India in the Ministry of Labour & Employment in exercising its power conferred under 
clause (d) of sub-section (1) and sub-section (2-A) of Section 10 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947) 
have referred a dispute between the Management of Bay Exploration Project, Oil India Ltd., and their workmen vide 
letter No. L-30012/41/2009 — IR(M), dated 26.03.2010 for its adjudication and the schedule of the reference is as 
follows:- 


“Whether the action of the management of Oil India, Bhubaneswar by terminating the services of Shri T. 
Narayan, Photocopier Operator without taking disciplinary action is legal & or justified? What relief the workman is 
entitled to? 


2; The case of the concerned workman as per his statement of claim is as follows:- 


That he was appointed as a Photocopier Operator on the basis of selection through interview against the 
permanent vacancy with effect from 01.06.1985 and was posted at Bhubaneswar vide Order dated 31.05.1985 of the 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 9, 2026/वैशाख 19, 1948 4193 


1“ Party-Management. At the time of appointment his basic pay was Rs. 310/- per month with D.A. and other 
allowances 38... applicable to regular employees of the company which was revised from time to time. He was 
kept under probation for six months and after | completion of his probation period, his service was confirmed. He 
had rendered 20 years of continuous service under the 1“ Party-Management, _ but he was illegally dismissed from 
service on 30.06.2006. He had been performing his duties sincerely and diligently and he had an unblemished service 
record, but he came to know about his dismissal order from daily news Paper “The Samaj” dated 30.06.2006 which 
was in violation of principle of natural justice as well as the mandatory provisions of Industrial Disputes Act. He 
had not been served the order of dismissal and when he approached the Management-company he was given a copy 
of the said dismissal order. He had not been served a charge sheet of any misconduct committed by him and no 
enquiry was conducted against him. The Management had not complied with the provisions of Section 25-F of the 
ID. Act. After about two years of his dismissal vide letter dated 25.02.2008 he was asked to collect his 
outstanding dues from the office of the Management. His dismissal from service by the 117 Party-Management was 
illegal, arbitrary and void ab-initio. The 11 Party-Management has not _ followed the established principles of law 
while dismissing him, so he may be reinstated in his service with full back wage and other service benefits. 


The 1" Party-Management had committed an illegality in payment of salary to its employees so the 
employees had raised a demand for payment of their salary in time. The employees demanded their regular 
payment through the Union but to victimize the workers, the 1“ Party-Management filed 1.C.C. case which was 
registered against him and other workmen in the Court of J.M.F.C., Bhubaneswar in which he was granted bail 
by the Hon’ble High Court of Orissa. In the meantime during the pendency of the 1.C.C. case before the Court of 
S.D.J.M., Bhubaneswar, the Management had dismissed him without following the principles of natural justice 
leaving aside other employees who were allegedly named in the above case, so the action of the Management was 
discriminatory and unsustainable in the eye of law. The Management in its dismissal order had mentioned a concocted 
story that he had assaulted an officer in the office of the 11 Party- Management and threatened him with dire 
consequences if any action was taken against him. Moreover, the Management had further mentioned in the said 
order that being afraid of his threat, no employee was prepared to give evidence against him so no domestic enquiry 
was conducted against him and his service was terminated by the order of the competent authority. It is the 
established principle of law that when a workman is terminated from service it would amount to retrenchment 
under the I.D. Act and the Management is bound to follow the provisions of Section 25-F of the I.D. Act, 
but the Management had not followed the provisions of | Section 25-F of the I.D. Act. The facts mentioned in the 
order of dismissal that he had physically assaulted the superior officer and threatened him to cause his death and 
threatened the other workers not to give evidence against him is false and concocted. The Management has taken 
false plea in order to justify its illegal action and to deny the benefits of principle of natural justice to him. After 
his illegal termination he had represented time and again to the higher authorities for his reinstatement but his 
grievance was not taken into consideration. Hence, he raised the dispute before the A.L.C.(C), Bhubaneswar. 


A prayer has been made before the Tribunal to declare his dismissal from service as illegal and void with a 
direction to the 1“ Party- Management for his reinstatement with full back wages and other service benefits with 
continuity of service. 


3. On the other-hand the case of the management of Oil India Limited is as follows:- 


That, the 17 party-Management is a Government of India company doing its endeavour to search for 
Hydrocarbon and/or oil in the North East Coast, Mahanadi Basin Areas so it had set up its base office at 
Bhubaneswar, Orissa with branch at Paradeep. The concerned workman was working as a contract labourer (Peon) 
under various contractors in the establishment but he was formally interviewed and was appointed as a Photocopy 
Machine Operator on the pay roll of the 17 Party-Management in the year 1985. After failure of the professional 
parties engaged by the company on global tender basis for drilling out, deposit of Hydro Carbon or oil during 1988 
and finding future prospects of any successful exercise were bleach, the drilling operation were gradually reduced. 
After that the Management proposed to transfer some of its employees from Bhubaneswar to the field Head Quarter 
at Duliajan in the state of Assam from December, 2005. Some of the employee and all unionized employees of the 
Bhubaneswar Office as a protest on transfer stopped receiving their salary from June, 2006 onwards even after 
advising them to receive salary but the employees did not come forward to receive their salary. After five months, all 
the employees orally approached the Management for release their un-received salary but the Management 
insisted that each employee should give a letter of undertaking that they had not received their pay at their own 
sweet- will and would not to repeat such action in future. In the mean-time on 13.06.2006 at around 11 A.M. Sri A. 
Mazumdar the then Manager (F & A) under the 1“ Party-Management while working in his office room at IDCO 
Towner, Janpath heard a huge commotion from outside and he immediately came out of his room. After that he 
found Sri Snehasis Haldar, Cashier standing helplessly with open cash office and about fifteen to twenty 
Unionized staff including the present workman were shouting at the top of their voice. The said employees were 
demanding their arrear salary, cheques without complying the conditions stipulated by the — Project in-charge Sri A. 
Majumdar, Manager (F& A). After that Sri A. Mazumdar told them that the same could not be paid without 
compliance of the conditions imposed by the Management. Thereafter Sri Mazumdar asked the cashier to close 
the cash office immediately. In the mean-time Shri J.K. Mishra and J.P. Bothaju, who had come thereto for rescue 
were threatened and abused by the workman and other employees. Thereafter the present workman and Sri 


4194 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 9, 2026/VAISAKHA 19, 1948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


Nilamani Sahoo targeted Sri Majumdar under the leadership of Sri N.R. Mallick, President of the MPEX Union, 
Sri D.R. Pattnaik, Sri J.K. | Sarangi, Sri T.P. Mohanty and started threatening Sri A. Mazumdar in the most abusive 
language. Subsequently, Sri Mazumdar sought help of Shri S.K. Pattnaik. A.O. (SR) who escorted him to leave 
the office premises but as he moved towards the gate the concerned workman along with Nilamani Sahoo and other 
co-employees came there and manhandled him by hitting him with blows on his body. Further the concerned 
workman and Sri Nilamani Sahoo continued to assault Sri Mazumdar with fist and blows and Sri Majumdar ran 
back to his office room out of fear. Sri Mazumdar was confined by the workman and another employee in his room 
up-to 4.30 P.M. on 13.06.2006 and he was not allowed to go for lunch. Sri Majumdar was on leave from 14.06.2006 
and 15.06.2006 and on 16.06.2006 on the basis of legal advice he had filed 1.C.C. Case Bearing No. 1496/2006 
dated 17.06.2006 against the concerned workman and others, which was sent to police station for registration of 
FIR. Further on 21.06.2006 the concerned workman came to _ the office and threatened Sri Mazumdar, while he was 
going home that if the Management would take any action against them then that would his last day and his 
family members. Moreover, the concerned workman threatened to all the co-employees not to give anything in 
writing relating to occurrence to the Management as a witness. The misconduct committed एज Sri T. Narayan 
and Sri Nilamani Sahoo were of serious in nature putting the life and liberty of a superior officer in jeopardy, so 
they did not deserve any lenient view from the Management. On the basis of the past experience, the 
Management realized that the workman would be non-cooperative in conducting fact finding enquiry and in view of 
such situation it was not practicable to conduct an enquiry. The conduct of the concerned workman and others 
employee like threatening the superior officer, shouting derogative slogans on each working day had become a 
regular _ routine affairs. Moreover, Sri Narayan had indulged in violent activities when Sri M.P. Barua Officer 
of the 17 Party-Management company had _ visited to Bhubaneswar office. The Management after considering that 
there were every possibilities that the concerned workman would resort to assault the officers of the Management had 
decided that neither any useful purpose would be served nor any further evidence would come out if a detailed 
enquiry would be held with regard to the charges, so the competent authority keeping in view the larger interest 
of the company dismissed the concerned workman namely Sri T. Narayan and Sri Nilamani Sahoo on 30.06.2006 
who were involved in such incident on 13.06.2006. The order of dismissal was sent to Sri Narayan through Regd. 
Post with A.D. enclosing there to cheques of his unpaid salary which returned unserved on 21.07.2006. The 
dismissal order was also sent to the workman Under Certificate of Posting on 03.07.2006 and the same was also 
published in the news-paper “The Sambad” Bhubaneswar edition dated 05.07.2006. In the mean-time a notice was 
published in the news-paper “The Sambad” on 15.11.2006 intimating the employees to receive their salary and in that 
response all the employees had received their salary on 16.11.2007 but the concerned workman gave a letter on 
22.11.2006 mentioning false allegation with a prayer to allow him to join in his duty immediately. The concerned 
workman had made correspondence to the Management under _ the RTI Act for inspection of records and project 
files which was considered by the Management. In the mean-time the learned S.D.J.M., Bhubaneswar referred the 
case bearing No. 1.C.C. 1496/2006 to Police Station and a G.R. Case No. 2487/2006 was registered. Subsequently 
Police after investigation had submitted charge-sheet. 


The concerned workman has been dismissed from service of Management as punishment, so he does not 
come under the definition of retrenchment and consequently Section 25-F of the Industrial Disputes Act is not 
applicable in this case. 


The 1* Party-Management by way of his reply has made a prayer that the reference may be answered 
against the disputant-workman. 


4. The 2™ Party-workman has filed rejoinder denying all the averments made in the written statement by the 
Management. 
5. On the basis of the pleadings of both the parties following issues are framed. 

ISSUES 


i) | Whether the action of the Management of Oil India, Bhubaneswar in terminating the services of Sri T. 
Narayan, Photocopier without taking disciplinary action is legal and justified? 


ii) Whether the provisions of Section 25-F of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 were not followed by the 1“ 
Party-Management while dismissing the service with effect from 30.06.2006. If so, whether he is 
entitled to the benefits of this provision? 


iii) What relief the workman is entitled to? 


6 The 2™ party-workman has examined altogether five witnesses. They are W.W.-1 Sri T. Narayan himself.

W.W.-4 and Sri Radhamohan Padhi as W.W.-5. The 2" Party-workman has proved the following documents 
which are marked as hereunder:- 


Ext.-1 — Copy of the appointment letter dated 31.05.1985.

7. On the other-hand the 1“ Party-Management has examined two witnesses. They are M.W.-1 Sri Abhijit 
Majumdar, Sr. Manager (F&A), Directorate General Hydrocarbon, Noida, M.W.-2 Sri S.K. Senapati, Head 
(Legal), Corporate Office, Oil India Limited.

FINDINGS 
9. The most important issue in this case is Issue No. I, so the Tribunal thinks it proper to discuss this issue first 
of all. 
ISSUE NO. I 
10. At the outset of discussions, it is relevant to mention here that it is an admitted fact that the concerned 


workman namely Sri T. Narayan had been appointed as Photocopier Operator after selection through interview 
against the permanent vacancy with effect from 01.06.1985 at Bhubaneswar and after completion of probation his 
service was confirmed. 


4196 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 9, 2026/VAISAKHA 19, 1948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


11. It is also the admitted fact that the concerned workman namely Sri T. Narayan had been dismissed/removed 
from the services with effect from 30.06.2006 without holding any domestic enquiry against him for the alleged 
misconduct. 


12. At this stage it is required to mention here that in the dismissal order of Sri T. Narayan which is at Ext.-3, it 
has been mentioned that the misconduct committed by him is grave and serious in nature putting the life and liberty of 
a superior officer in jeopardy and as such deserves no lenient view from the Management. It has also been mentioned 
that he had taken extreme step of using abusive language and assaulting Sri Mazumdar inside the office premises 
in presence of other employees and such act in itself speaks of his high handed and recalcitrant attitude. It has been 
further noted that the Management is of view that it would be difficult to hold a proper enquiry since the witness 
might not come forward to give frank and true evidence. 


13. It is relevant to mention herewith that there is a Standing Order for the workmen employed in the 
establishment of Oil India Limited which is marked as Ext.-16. The Management had not raised any objection at the 
time of its marking as exhibit by the W.W.-2 (Sri N.R. Mallick). Since no objection had been raised by the 
Management on the copy of the Standing Order for the workman employed in the establishment of Oil India Limited 
(Ext.-16), so it amounts to admission on the part of the Management. 


14. In the Standing Order the provisions of termination of employment/service has been mentioned under 
Section 19 which reads as follows:- 


19.0. Termination of Employment/Service :- 


19.1. | Reasons :- 

19.1.1 A workman’s termination of service will generally fall under one of the following categories: - 
(a) Discharge owing to staff redundancy. 
(b) Discharge on completion of temporary work. 


(c) Discharge on completion of probationary period in respect of new recruits (if work is found 
unsatisfactory during the probationary _ period. 


(d) Discharge on receipt of adverse report about the workman’s efficiency, work, acts or expression 
prejudicial to the company, its management, its business or acts or expression prejudicial to the company, its 
management, its business or its reputation or any adverse official report. 


(e) Discharge for absence from work, without authorized leave or permission for more than fourteen 
consecutive days. 


(f) Any discharge for any misstatement or suppression of facts or furnishing to the Company any false or 
incorrect particulars about himself/herself/family, his/her qualifications or experience or past antecedents or 
conduct at the time of the appointment. 


(g) Discharge or conviction by any Court of Law for a criminal offence involving moral turpitude. 
(h) Discharge on attainment of age of superannuation. 

(i) Discharge on resignation after due notice. 

(j) Discharge for unsatisfactory service. 

(k) Dismissal for any act of misconduct as specified under Section 21. 

Clause (a) to (k) 


Further, Section-19.2 of the Standing Order mentions the procedure for termination of 
employment/service and under Section-19.2.(d) it has been specifically mentioned that the services of the 
workman shall not be terminated (dismissed) for an act of misconduct specified under section 21 of the Standing 
Orders unless he/she has been given an opportunity to explain the charges levelled against him/her and a 
departmental enquiry is held. Further, under section 21 of the Standing Order disciplinary action of misconduct has 
been mentioned. 


15. Now in the Standing Order of the Management of Oil India Limited __ there is categorical provision that a 
workman shall not be terminated (dismissed) for an act of misconduct unless he has been given an opportunity to 
explain and after holding a departmental enquiry. But in this case the Management had not issued any 
explanation/show cause to the concern workman with regard to his misconduct as mentioned in the dismissal 
order and no disciplinary enquiry was conducted against him for proving the charges of misconduct. 


16. However, at this stage the Tribunal thinks it proper to discuss the oral and documentary evidence of both the 
parties even though no departmental enquiry was conducted against the workman. 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 9, 2026/वैशाख 19, 1948 4197 


17. The most competent witness in this case on behalf of the workman is the W.W.-1 Sri 1. Narayan. He has 
categorically deposed that he was appointed as a Photocopier Operator after he was selected through interview 
against a permanent vacancy with effect from 01.06.1985 and was posted at Bhubaneswar. He has also deposed that 
his services were made permanent after completion of probation period, but his service was terminated without 
holding any departmental enquiry. He has proved certain documents which are marked as Ext.-1 to Ext.-7. 


In the cross examination he has deposed that on 13.06.2006 he was present in the office and he was called by 
Sri Mazumdar for receiving salary/payment after giving in writing, but he refused the same. He has denied the 
suggestion that he had misbehaved with Sri Mazumdar and kept him in confinement in a room till 4.30 P.M. He has 
also denied the suggestion that he along with N.R. Mallick, D.R. Patnaik, J.K. Sarangi and T.P. Mohanty had 
assaulted and abused Sri Mazumdar. 


The W.W.-2 Sri N.R. Mallick who is the President of the Mahanadi Petroleum Exploration Employees 
Union has fully corroborated the evidence of the W.W.-1 in his examination in chief and he has proved certain 
documents which are marked as Ext.-8 to Ext.-17. 


In the cross examination he has stated that he was dismissed from service in the year 2004. He has stated that 
he had collected information from the applicant that there was no incident in the office on 13.06.2006. 


The W.W.-3, Krupasindhu Mallick has also supported the evidence adduced by the W.W.-1 in his 
examination in chief. In the cross examination _ he has deposed that he was not an employee of the Management on 
13.06.2006 and he was driving a tourist vehicle, but he was working asa __ Driver of a tourist vehicle engaged for Oil 
India Limited. He has also stated that on 13.06.2006 the concerned workman and others had neither gharoed 
nor assaulted Mr. Mazumdar, who used to attend of his office in between 9.30 A.M. to 1.30 P.M. and there after 3 
P.M. to 3.30 P.M. 


The W.W.-4 and W.W.-5 were the employees of the Management of Oil India Limited and they have 
fully supported the case of the concerned workman in their evidence. There is nothing in the cross examination to 
which requires discussion here. 


18. On the other-hand the most competent witness on behalf of the 17 Party- Management is the M.W.-1 Sri A. 
Mazumdar. He has deposed that on account of transfer of employees by the Management, the other Unionized 
employees stopped receiving their salary from January, 2006 onwards and the Management had not stopped 
paying their salary to them rather advised them to receive their salary for which a letter was also issued. He 
has further deposed that on 13.06.2006 at around 11 A.M. he heard loud sound and disturbing slogans by the 
Unionized workmen resulting in violent commotion in front of the cash office and he informed the agitating workers 
that cheque could not be handed-over to them without submitting undertaking. He has further stated that on his advice 
the Cashier closed the cash box and handed over the keys to him. He has also stated the workman and others 
abused him and the Cashier in presence of Shri J.K. Mishra and Shri H.P. Bothaju. He has also stated that the 
concerned workman and Nilamani Sahoo targeted him under the leadership of Sri N.R. Mallick, 
Sri D.R. Pattnaik and Sri T.P. Mohanty who gheraoed and abused him as he was moving towards gate. He has also 
stated that the concerned workman and others repeatedly gave him blows and went on abusing. He has further 
stated that he was on leave from 14.06.2006 to 15.06.2006 and on 16.06.2006 after consultation with the legal 
officer filed an I.C.C. Case No. 1496/2006 before the learned S.D.J.M., Bhubaneswar. He has also deposed that on 
21.06.2006 the concerned workman and Sri Nilamani Sahoo again assaulted him while he was going towards his 
personal vehicle from his office and he was also telephonically threatened by the concerned workman. He has 
further deposed that the conduct of the concerned workman and others were grave in nature and considering that no 
purpose would be served for holding departmental enquiry, the concerned workman Sri T. Narayan was 
dismissed from service on 30.06.2006. He has proved certain documents which are in Ext.- A to Ext.- J. 


In the cross examination he has deposed that he had given in writing against the disputant workman for his 
unlawful and rudely behaviour as well as manhandling. He has also deposed that he could not say that if any 
departmental proceeding was initiated against him or not for such alleged misconduct. He has further deposed that he 
was the Project, In-charge on 13.06.2006. He has also deposed that he cannot say about the procedure to be 
adopted as per the Certified Standing Order of the 17 Party- Management. He has denied the suggestions that the 
dismissal of the concerned workman was totally illegal and unjustified and the action of non-holding of 
departmental proceedings. 


The Management Witness No. 2 Sri S.K. Senapati has fully supported _ the case of the Management in their 
examination in chief and there is —_ nothing in their cross examination which requires discussion here. 


19. It is pertinent to mention here that much has been said about the institution of 1.C.C. Case No. 1496/2006 
which was subsequently converted into P.S. Case No. 183/2006 and Police had submitted chare-sheet in this _ case. 
But the Management has not produced the copy of the registration of P.S. Case No. 183/2006 and the charge-sheet. 


20. In absence of the copy of the registration of Police Case and copy of 016 charge-sheet submitted by Police 
on the L.C.C. case filed by Sri Mazumdar the case of the Management regarding incident on 13.06.2006 6065 not 
seem to be probable and trust-worthy. 


4198 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 9, 2026/VAISAKHA 19, 1948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


21. After analysing the evidence of both the parties it is crystal clear that the concerned workman namely Sri 
T. Narayan had been dismissed from service of the Management without holding any departmental enquiry which 
is in violation of the Standing Order of the management of Oil India Limited (Ext.-16). 


22. In view of such, the action of the management in dismissing/terminating the services of concerned workman 
w.e.f. 30.06.2006 is not legal and justified. Hence the concerned workman is _ entitled for relief. 


Accordingly, the Issue No. I is answered in favour of the 27 Party-Workman. 
ISSUE NO. I & 111 


23. The Tribunal has already held (supra) that the action of the | Management in dismissing/terminating the 
service of the 2™ party- workman namely Sri T. Narayan is not legal and justified so the concerned workman is 
entitled for relief sought in his Claim Petition. 


24. In view of the above facts and circumstances, the Tribunal passes following: - 
ORDER 


The management of Bay Exploration Project, Oil India Limited, IDCO Tower, Bhubaneswar is directed to 
reinstate the 2™ party-workman Sri T. Narayan in his services if he is not attained at the age of superannuation 
and also directed to pay all the back wages and consequential service benefits with continuity of service from the 
date of his dismissal and if the concerned workman Sri T. Narayan has already attained the age of supernation, then 
the Management is directed to pay all the back wages and other consequential benefits from the date of 
dismissal/discharge till his retirement along with all retiral benefits. 


25. This is the Award of the Tribunal. The reference is answered accordingly. 


26. Office is directed to send the copy of the Award to the Ministry of | Labour for publishing the Award in the 
official gazette. 


27. Case record be consigned to record room. 
Dictated & Corrected by me. 


DINESH KUMAR SINGH, Presiding Officer

S.O. 816.—In pursuance of Section 17 of the Industrial Dispute Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central 


Government hereby publishes the award (Reference No. 02/2022 (REF Case No. 89/2006) of the Central

AWARD 


The Government of India, Ministry of Labour by its order No. L-11011/10/2012-IR(M) dated 13" February 
2014 has referred the following dispute under section 10(1)(d) of the I.D. Act, 1947 requiring this forum to decide the 
question. 


SCHEDULE 


“Whether the action of the management of Airports Authority of India Hyderabad in connection with the 
transfer of workman Shri Mohd. Ghouse, Sr. Assistant (Lifts) & Branch Secretary of Airports Authority of 
India Worker’s Union, Hyderabad from Hyderabad to Rajahmundry, is legal and justified? What relief the 
workman is entitled to?” 


After receipt of the reference, it was numbered as 30 of 2014, and notices were issued to both the Workman 
and Management. 


2: The factual matrix of the Industrial Disputes as per claim statement of the Petitioner are that the Respondent 
Management transferred Petitioner from Hyderabad to Rajahmundry Airport and action of Respondent Management 
is not only illegal and arbitrary but also violative of section 2 (r)(a) of item No. 7 of the Fifth Schedule R/w section 25 
T, section 33(1) (a) of 1.10. Act, 1947. Further, Petitioner averred that he was posted on non-transferable post, but 
Respondent transferred him from Hyderabad Airport Begumpet to Rajahmundry Airport, in contravention of the 
terms and conditions of the transfer policy dated 24.11.1999 and 27.02.2014. Further, petition submitted that he was 
working at Hyderabad Airport, and he has been abruptly transferred to Civil Aerodrome, unpopular urban Turner 
Station Rajahmundry against the AAI Transfer Policy which is nothing but change of condition service and 
victimization of the Petitioner workman by Respondent Management by order dated 10.05.2010. Further, Petitioner 
was working as a workman Supervisor (Lift, technical block, lift) since 1980 at Begumpet, Hyderabad, and was 
authorized to operate and maintenance of four (4) lifts and three (3) escalators in the Airport Authority of India, which 
is a profit-making public enterprise 23.03.2008 at 00.00 hours the commercial operation of the International Airport 
were shifted to Rajiv Gandhi International Airport at Shamshabad without notice, without informing to the workman 
and without following provision of Section 9 A of the LD Act. Which not only resulted lot of hardship to the 
workman but also adversely affected the service conditions of the workman. 


Further, it is submitted that Airport Authority Employee’s Union (AAEU) filed writ petition for protection of 
service conditions of the workmen and W.P. No. 18000/2012 for domestic commercial operation from Begumpet 
Airport. Hon’ble High Court at Andhra Pradesh in WP No.4950; 7147; and 7611 of 2008 vide judgment dated 
06.07.2009 held “even otherwise the service condition at the employes of the Petitioner union sufficiently protected 
under the law with all the other alternative amid efficacious remedies for appropriate action “which means and 
includes the conciliation under the Act UINION has to file before the conciliation officer then Approached to high 
court further remedy. 


Airport Authority Kamgar Union. (AAKU) and Petitioner unions filed the petition for protection of service 
conditions of the workman before A.LC as per the terms of the settlement u/s. 12(3) of ID Act, 1947 The 
management agreed to arrange round the shift duties as per the previous practices for the workman of the Engg and 
operational category. Further, it is contended that the Employer changed the duties weekly off, and place of duties, 
timing, starting the shift withdrawal Three shifts, and then two shifts and then one shift. Finally it has decided general 
shift from 9.30 AM to 5.30 PM 6 days week instead of 5 days which is_ violation of his own order Vide No 
AAT/SR/M/2-1(11)EH dated 09.03.29 The action of Respondent in respect of withdrawal of over time, without 
intimation is contrary to sec 9 A of LD. Act, Sec.33 A and also direction of Genl. Manager (Electrical), 
dated: 26.03.2009 


4204 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 9, 2026/VAISAKHA 19, 1948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


When the duty of Petitioner was changed on 22.12.2008 during conciliation proceedings Senior Law 
Manager, has given an undertaking that the applicant shall be posted in shift duty if he agrees hereto. The applicant 
agreed and was posted on shift duty at the ATM on 08.02.2009 with 64 hours over time, weekly off on Monday, and 
double duty on Sunday. One day morning M=6 AM to 1.PM and next day Evening duty 2PM to 10 PM. All of a 
sudden on 25.07.2010 while he was on duty Terminal Management asked him to get out from the duty and was not 
given the roaster and not a reliving letter with a direction where to report and did not furnished the Leave entitlement 
and compensatory off. On both Terminal R.G.T and NTR there were 12L lift operator for three escalators and five 
lifts but now there is only one General shift have been working without O.T and night wages. 


The workman belonging to the other two unions are not treated at par with reference to the members of the 
recognized union. Their timely upgradations were not given. Further, Petitioner averred that his duty has been 
changed without information or notice. The Petitioner has suffered at the behest of recognized Union since 1981. 
Petitioner is covered under the Central Government Health Scheme till 1994. and CGHS subscription was deducted 
from his Salary by the Respondent till 2004 but that was not credited to the CGHS Account for five years and 
consequently CGHS facility of Applicant was stopped in 2005. The matter was moved with RLC Central and Central 
Government Union Secretary issued directions vide Reference No.1/11011/2/2005-IR (M) Dated 30-05-2005_IR (M) 
Dated 30.05.2005 replied that it is under the consideration of Hqrs, The Respondent has issued the card for inpatient 
card, and it is not allowed for outpatient. Outpatient medical facility is not available to the applicant. The medical bills 
submitted by Petitioner for reimbursement for Rs 40,000/- is state lying unsettled with the Respondent. 


Petitioner primary duty Vide letter no AAI/HY/AM (E) IN/LIFTS Dated 7.7.1999 was operation of lifts and 
Escalator at Hyderabad Airport. And lift operator was responsible for operation and maintenance of lifts and escalator 
available at N.T.R. Domestic Terminal, R.G.T. and about technical block of Hyd Airport Petitioner was required to 
attend to Emergency calls and V.VIP moments. Further, it is averred that Srinivas D. has been deployed at ATM duty 
in place of the Petitioner because he was the local branch president of recognized union vide direction of Jt.G.M Engg 
Electrical. Vide ref No AAI/HY/ENGG/E STAFF dated 26.03.2009 to report to terminal manager and to carry out 
duties as assign by Terminal manager. Petitioner performed his duty from 08.02.2009 to 24.07.2010. Thereafter 
Petitioner was transferred from Hyderabad to an unpopular station at Rajahmundry though he was singly operating 
Lifts at the Airport, Hyderabad. Petitioner averred that he has been transferred from Hyderabad and by way of 
victimization and discrimination and his transfer is illegal malafide, contrary to the policy and un-reasonable. 
Petitioner prayed to set aside his transfer order dated 24" August 2012 passed by Respondent Management from 
Hyderabad to Rajahmundry. 


3. Per contra Respondent has filed counter and therein it is Contended that the workman whose dispute has 
been referred has attained the age of superannuation on 30" June 2014, and has settled all his claims. Therefore, the 
reference has become infructuous for the reason that no cause of action survived on account of his retirement, and no 
relief can be granted to the Petitioner. Further, it is contended that the Respondent totally denies the averment made 
by the Petitioner as baseless and misconceived, and the Petitioner is put to the strict proof of the same. Further, it is 
contended that Begumpet Airport of Hyderabad was closed for commercial aircraft operations, which is shifted to 
Shamshabad Airport, vide Central Government notification dated 30" March 2008. Further, it is contended that 
terminal buildings NTR terminal building and Rajiv Gandhi terminal building are not operated, as there are no 
commercial passenger movements from Begumpet Airport. Therefore, escalators and lifts in those terminal buildings 
are not functioning for passenger facilities, and Petitioner workman was transferred from Hyderabad to Rajmundry 
Airport by order dated 23" August 2012 due to administrative grounds. Further, it is contended that the Regional 
Labour Commissioner has no power or authority to pass any order with regard to the transfer of an employee. The 
pleas of Petitioner pertaining to other cases which are pending before the Hon’ble Court, are not relevant to present 
reference and Respondent will give proper reply for filing the counter in respective cases. Further, Respondent 
contended that the Begumpet Airport of Hyderabad was closed for commercial aircraft operation, because it got 
which are shifted to Shamshabad Airport, vide Central Government notification dated 107 March 2008. Since both 
the terminal building i.e., NTR terminal building and Rajiv Gandhi terminal building, are not being operated as there 
is no commercial passenger movement from Begumpet Airport. Therefore, the escalators and lifts in those terminal 
buildings are not functioning for passenger facilities. Hence, Petitioner who was on duty to operate was transferred 
from Hyderabad to Rajahmundry via order dated 237 August 2012, and he was relieved from his duty. But Petitioner 
failed to report for duty at the transferred place in compliance of his transferred order. Further, it is contended that the 
Petitioner had filed Writ petition number 27455 of 2012 before the Hon’ble High Court of Andhra Pradesh, 
questioning transfer order issued by the Respondent, transferring him from Hyderabad to Rajahmundry and Hon’ble 
High Court decline to grant any interim relief, Writ Petition, was withdrawn and High Court dismissed the Writ 
Petition as withdrawn. Therefore, Respondent prayed to dismiss the claim statement of the Petitioner. 


4. In support of averment made in the claim statement, Petitioner has filed chief statement affidavit of WW1, 
Respondent has also filed chief statement affidavit of MW1. Petitioner has also filed certain photocopies of the 
documents. 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 9, 2026/वैशाख 19, 1948 4205 
on the basis of pleadings of either parties following issues arise for determination in this 2856:- 


I. Whether the action of Management of Airport Authority of India, Hyderabad, in connection with the transfer of 
workman Sri Mohd Ghouse, Senior Assistant, Lift and Bench Secretary of Airport Authority of India Workers Union, 
Hyderabad, from Hyderabad to Rajahmundry, is legal and justified? 


II. To what relief, if any, Petitioner is entitled to? 
Findings:- 
ISSUE No. I:- 


Petitioner in his claim statement has challenged transfer order dated 23.08.2012, from Hyderabad to 
Rajahmundry Airport, on the ground of unfair labour practices, victimization and change in service condition in 
violation of provision contained u/s 9 A of the ID. Act. 


At the outset, learned counsel for Respondent has contended that Petitioner-workman, whose dispute has 
been referred has attained the age of superannuation on 30.06.2014, and has settled all his claims. Therefore, present 
reference has become infructuous for the reason as no cause of action survives on account of his retirement, and no 
relief can be granted to the Petitioner. The contention of Respondent that Petitioner-workman has got retired after the 
age of superannuation on 30.06.2014, is not controverted and denied by the Petitioner in his oral testimony. 
Therefore, it is undisputed that Petitioner got retired from service at the age of superannuation on 30.06.2014 and has 
settled all his claims. It is not the case of the Petitioner that due to his transfer from Airport Hyderabad he has suffered 
financial disadvantages. It is settled law that where an employee challenging his transfer order in a labor Court 
subsequently retires during the pendency of the case, the relief sought specifically the cancellation of the transfer 
order and related service benefits during the contested period, may become infructuous or difficult to grant due to the 
cessation of the employment relationship. The primary remedy, reinstatement to the original post, is generally not 
possible after a valid retirement. Therefore, upon retirement of an employee, the employer-employee relationship 
ceases and main remedies of service law, like setting aside a transfer, preventing termination, are generally 
prospective and tied to active service. Therefore, this court cannot typically order the employee to be placed in a 
specific active-duty post or reverse the effect of the past transfer order as if the employee was still in service. The 
claim for the primary relief becomes obsolete. Therefore, in view of the above, in the present matter Petitioner has 
already retired from his post after attaining the age of superannuation on 30.06.2014. Thus, the relief claimed by the 
Petitioner for conciliation of his transfer order has become infructuous and same is not maintainable. 


Now, let us examine legality of impugned transfer order of Petitioner from Hyderabad to Rajahmundry. In 
this context, Petitioner has filed a chief affidavit of statement of WW1, wherein he has reiterated the averment made 
in claim petition. But in cross-examination WW1 has stated that it is true that he has not marked any exhibit in 
support of his claim in his chief examination affidavit. It is true that he was relieved on 31.08.2012, by a transfer 
order issued by the Respondent. Further, WW1 states that it is true that he has filed writ petition number 27455 of 
2012, and same was dismissed as withdrawn. Further, WW1 states that it is true that he has not filed any document to 
show that his transfer from Hyderabad to Rajahmundry was to accommodate one Mr. Srinivas, office assistant. It is 
true that he has not filed any documents to show that he cannot be transferred from Begumpet, Hyderabad, to any 
other place. Thus, from the above statement of witness WW1, it manifests that Petitioner has utterly failed to prove 
the averment made in his claim statement that he was posted on a non-transferable post and he has been transfers from 
Hyderabad to Rajahmundry on the ground victimization and unfair labour practice and also in violation of Section 9 
A of Industrial Dispute Act. Further, Petitioner has failed to prove the fact the due to impugned transfer order how his 
service condition has been changed. 


Whereas, Respondent has filed an affidavit of examination-in-chief statement of MW1 in oral evidence and 
therein MW1 has stated that, according to Ministry of Civil Aviation Airport Authority of India order number 2002, 
liability for service is transferable anywhere in India or abroad. Further, MW1 has deposed that the employees of the 
Begumpet airport had been redeployed by transferring them to other places so that their services could be gainfully 
utilized at some other places without altering their service conditions, and this is not in violation of section 9A of the 
Industrial Disputes Act. Further, MW1 states that the Begumpet airport of Hyderabad was closed for commercial 
aircraft operation, and it was shifted to Shamshabad airport, as per a Central Government notification dated 
10.03.2008. Further, MW1 stated that both the terminal buildings, 1.6., NTR terminal building and Rajiv Gandhi 
terminal building, were not operated as there were no commercial passenger movements from Begumpet airport. 
Therefore, the escalator and lift in those terminal buildings were not functioning for passenger facilities. Hence, the 
workman-Petitioner was transferred from Hyderabad to Rajahmundry airport by order dated 23.08.2012, and other 
workmen were also transferred to other places, and Petitioner was relieved from his duty. Further, MW1 stated that 
the other employees reported for duty at the transferred places, but the workman-Petitioner failed to report for duty at 
the transferred place. 


Thus, witness MW1 in his chief statement has proved that Petitioner was transferred from Begumpet airport 
to Shamshabad airport due to close of Commercial Aircraft operation at airport Begumpet. Further, MW1 has proved 
that terminal buildings of the Begumpet airport were not in operation as there was no commercial passenger 


4206 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 9, 2026/VAISAKHA 19, 1948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


movement at Begumpet airport and due to non-availability of work at Begumpet airport, the employees including 
Petitioner deputed at Begumpet Airport were transferred to other places by the Respondent Management. Thus, it 
reflects from the testimony of MW1 that the Petitioner was transferred to Rajahmundry Airport by the Respondent 
Management due to administrative reasons. Moreover, Petitioner failed to produce any document to prove his 
averment that his service was non-transferable, and he was transferred from Begumpet Airport due to victimization. 
Moreover, despite sufficient opportunity, Petitioner failed to cross-examine witness MW1. Therefore, statement of 
witness MW1 in the chief statement affidavit remained uncontroverted. 


In this context, reference of decision of Hon’ble Supreme Court in the case of Mrs. Shilpi Bose and 
Others Vs State of Bihar, A.LR., 1991 Supreme Court, page 532, is relevant wherein Hon’ble Supreme Court: - 


“In our opinion, the Courts should not interfere with a transfer Order which are made in public interest and 
for administrative reasons unless the transfer Orders are made in violation of any mandatory statutory Rule or on the 
ground of malafide. A Government Servant holding a transferable post has no vested right to remain posted at one 
place or the other, he is hable to be transferred from one place to other. Transfer Orders issued by the competent 
authority do not violate any of his legal rights. Even if a transfer Order is passed in violation of exceptive instructions 
or orders, the courts ordinarily should not interfere with the order instead affected party should approach the higher 
authorities in the Department. If the Courts continue to interfere with day-to-day transfer orders issued by the 
Government and its subordinate authorities, there will be complete chaos in the administration which would not be 
conducive to public interest. The High court overlooked these aspects in interfering with the transfer orders.” 


Further, Hon’ble Supreme Court ion the case of State of Madhya Pradesh Vs. S.S. Kaurav 1995A.1LR., page 1056, 
have held: - 


“It is contended for the respondent that the respondent had already worked at Jagdalpur from 1982 to 1989 
and when he was transferred to Bhopal, there was no justification to retransfer him again to Jagdalpur. We cannot 
appreciate these grounds/The courts or Tribunals are not appellate forums to decide on transfers of officers on 
administrative grounds. The wheels of administration should be allowed to run smoothly and the courts or tribunals 
are not expected to interdict the working of the administrative system by transferring the officers to proper places. It 
is for the administration to take appropriate decision and such decisions shall stand unless they are vitiated either by 
malafides or by extraneous consideration without any factual background foundation. In this case we have seen that 
on the administrative grounds the transfer orders came to be issued. Therefore, we cannot go into the expediency of 
posting an officer at a particular place" 


Thus, in view of the law laid down by Apex Court as discussed above and ongoing evidence material on 
record in the matter at hand the Petitioner was transferred by Respondent Management from Begumpet Airport to 
Rajahmundry Airport due to stoppage of operation of commercial passenger flight and there was no work available 
for the employees posted at Begumpet Airport. Since Petitioner was posted at Begumpet Airport as escalator and lift 
operator which were closed due to shift of commercial passenger flight from Begumpet to Shamshabad. Therefore, 
the Petitioner and other employees were transferred from Begumpet Airport purely on the administrative ground. 
Therefore, the impugned order of transfer of Petitioner under challenge in this Petition warrants no interference by the 
tribunal and Petition filed by the Petitioner sans merit hence liable to dismissed. 


Further, as regard other pleas of the Petitioner taken in the claim statement regarding withdrawal of overtime 
and other benefits it would be pertinent to mention here that the said pleas raised by Petitioner in his claim statement 
are outside the scope of reference in this case and need not to be discussed and answered here. It is settled law that the 
jurisdiction of the Labour Court/Tribunal is limited to the points specifically referred and matter incidental thereto. 
Since the scope of its jurisdiction and power of the tribunal is circumscribed by the order of reference, it is not 
permissible for it to go beyond the term of the reference. Therefore, the issue is decided against the petitioner. 


ISSUE NO. II: - 


In view of discussion and finding given at ISSUE No. I, Petitioner claim statement sans merit and liable to be 
dismissed. Petitioner is not entitled to any relief. 


AWARD 


The action of management of Airport Authority of India, Hyderabad, in connection with the transfer of workman, 
Shri Mohd Gouse Senior, Senior Assistant Lift and Branch Secretary Airport Authority of India Worker Union, 
Hyderabad, from Hyderabad to Rajahmundry is held legal and justified. Petitioners claim statement sans merit hence 
dismissed. The reference is answered accordingly. 


Award is passed accordingly. Transmit. 


Dictated to Smt. P. Phani Gowri, Personal Assistant transcribed by her, corrected and signed by me on this 
9" day of December 2025. 


IRFAN QAMAR, Presiding Officer

application Ex. 3 along with authorisation of 1 workman and list of all 10 workmen to withdraw the case mentioning

6. The award is passed as above. The award be sent for publication U/s 17(1) of Industrial Disputes Act, 1947.

का.आ. 824.---औद्योगिक विवाद अधिनियम, 1947 (1947 का 14) की धारा 17 के अनुसरण में, केन्द्रीय 
सरकार चीफ ऑपरेशन मैनेजर एंड लेबर कांट्रेक्टर के प्रबंधतंत्र के संबद्ध नियोजकों और नेशनल लेबर यूनियन के बीच 
अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट केन्द्रीय सरकार औद्योगिक अधिकरण एवं श्रम न्यायालय, अहमदाबाद, Tare (रिफरेन्स 


न.- 28/2025) को जैसा कि अनुलग्नक में दिखाया गया है, प्रकाशित करती है जो केन्द्रीय सरकार को सॉफ्ट कॉपी के साथ 
04.05.2026 को प्राप्त हुआ था |

AWARD 


Case is fixed up today during special campaign for disposal of old cases. As per last order dated 18-12-2025, 
fresh notice was issued to the workmen/Union as per recorded address under Speed post informing them that the case 
is fixed up today i.e. 05-02-2026 and as per the postal track consignment, notice was delivered to the workman/union 
on 01-01-2026 but today also the workmen/Union is remained absent till 2.20 p.m. Mr. Dilip Dey, learned Advocate 
for the Management of IOCL, Guwahati is present. The Suppliers/Contractors viz. M/S B. Thomas Chaliha & other 
contractors, dealers, Distributors of IOCL, NEISO, Guwahati are appeared through Ms. M. Paul, Ld. Advocate. The 
Contractor viz. M/S Nanesh Trading Pvt. Ltd. & M/S Chandmal Serawgi & Co. are absent without any step. 


In exercise of powers conferred under clause (d) of sub-section (1) and sub-section (2A) of Section 10 of the 
Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Government of India, Ministry of Labour and Employment has 
referred the schedule dispute to this Tribunal through the Deputy Chief Labour Commissioner (Central), Guwahati, 
by Order No. L-30011/12/2011-IR (M) dated 21-07-2011 for adjudication. 


On receiving Order No. L-30011/12/2011-IR (M) dated 21-07-2011 from the Under Secretary, IR(M), 
Government of India, Ministry of Labour and Employment, New Delhi Reference case No. 13 of 2011 was registered 
on 10-08-2011 and an order was passed issuing notice to the parties, directing them to appear and submit their written 
statements along with relevant documents in support of their claims. 


SCHEDULE 


“Whether the notice of Strike of APMU dated 15/6/2010 on the General Manager, IOCL, NEISO, Guwahati 
is proper? If so, whether the 6 points demands contained in APMU letter No.APMU/HO/15 1/2009 dated 25- 
05-2010 is justified? If so what relief the contract labour are entitled to?” 


The workmen represented by the Secretary, Assam Petroleum Mazdoor Union, Guwahati Sri Ramen Das 
submitted the Written Statement on behalf of the Union stating that the workmen were engaged by the 
agents/transporters of Indian Oil Corporation Oil India Ltd., Numuligarh Refineries, Hindustan Petroleum 
Corporation Ltd., Bharat Petroleum Corporation Ltd., and as such they are the contract labourers of the Principal 
Employer i.e. Indian Oil Corporation Oil India Ltd., Bharat Petroleum etc. The present workmen were engaged in 
different petrol pumps owned by Indian Oil Corporation and the employees of transporters carrying petroleum 
products including Gas cylinders of Indian Oil Corporation. It is stated that the petrol pumps of IOC, engaging 
different employees, are of two groups one A side group petrol pump being operated by direct supervision of Indian 
Oil Corporation and the another group of petrol pump is B side petrol pump where the dealers have absolute control 
over their employees. All those employees of A and B side group are engaged for supply of different petroleum 
products manufactured by Indian Oil Corporation. It is stated that the present workmen are the employees of the 
Contractor/dealer of Indian Oil Corporation and as such they are contractual labour, the Oil companies are the 
principal employer and dealers or transports so engaged by the Indian Oil Corporation is contractor. It is submitted 
that the concerned workers are not secured by Provident Fund, Employees State Insurance benefit etc and the wages 
paid to them by the dealers of different Oil companies are far below the prescribed rate of wages as availed by other 
contract labourers of Oil India Ltd., Indian Oil Corporation, Oil and Natural Gas Corporation Limited etc. It is 
submitted that in course of carrying of petroleum products including Gases, the workers have to face different 
situation such as high jack of vehicle and killing of drivers or handyman and in such situation the legal heirs of those 
employees do not even get financial help to maintain their liveli hood. It is further stated that when petroleum 
products are loaded at the concerned loading centre, the tanker remains locked and the concerned key is kept in the 
loading centre itself and the transporter carries petroleum products from the loading centre and delivers the same at 
the concerned petrol pump, in which process, the employees have no control over the quality or quantity of the 
petroleum products carried through the tankers. It is stated that when a tanker reached the petrol pump, it is unlocked 
through a master key kept in the petrol pump. But if it is found that there is shortage in the quantity of the Petroleum 


4216 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 9, 2026/VAISAKHA 19, 1948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


Products or deficiency in its quality, the transporter is made responsible for the same by the authorities of the 
concerned petrol pump. In that case due deduction is made by the Pump authorities from the bill amount payable to 
the transporter for carrying petroleum products from the loading centre to petrol pump and in turn the transporter used 
to deduct the said amount from its employees engaged in the Oil tanker causing harassment to those employees. 
Though the Union tried to resolve the problems but failed. Hence, the Union submitted six points charter of demands 
on 16-12-2008 i.e. (i) to enhance monthly wages to minimum & 10,000/- to the workers, (ii) for payment of bonus and 
other benefits like provident fund, gratuity, ESI (iii) testing of marker to be done at the time of loading and delivery of 
products and certificates should be given immediately (iv) that tanker drivers will not be made responsible for any 
shortage of products, (v) payment of compensation for accident or attack by antisocial elements in course of duty and 
(vi) that Oil companies should take responsibility for payment of wages and other benefits to the workers. 


It is further stated by the Union in the written statement that in regard to the aforesaid demands, the 
Management of different Oil companies did not pay any hid to it and hence the Union had to issue strike notice on 15- 
06-2010 to the various oil companies supplying petrol and Gas to the petrol pump and Gas distribution centres for not 
settling the six points demands charter on the issue of revision of wages and other benefits for the workers engaged in 
Gas/Oil tankers, Petrol pumps, Gas agencies and cylinder carrying trucks. Rather the management adopted a policy of 
victimization to the members of the Union. It is submitted in the written statement that the dealers of North 
Lakhimpur and Dhemaji restored to harassment on tanker drivers, but Management instead of taking step black listed 
the Tankers whimsically. Hence the members of the Union issued strike notice that they would boycott loading and 
unloading of IOCL’s products from 5 a.m. on 1“ July,2010 to 9 a.m. of 5" July,2010 demanding settlement of their 
demand charter. It is stated that on issuance of strike notice, notice of conciliation was issued on 30-06-2010 but 
inspite of conciliation proceeding, the dispute was not settled and accordingly submitted failure report u/s 12 (4) of 
the Industrial Dispute Act,1947 and sent the same before this Court for adjudication as to whether the notice of strike 
dated 15-06-20210 is proper and if so, whether the six points demands dated 25-05-2010 is justified. Therefore, 
prayed to pass award directing the Management to take steps for implementation of the six points demands. 


Lateron, Union also filed additional written statement stating that the employees employed by the agents of 
1.0.C., called as dealers, contractors agents of IOC should be considered as the contract labour of I.O.C. Limited; they 
should be given the minimum wages of = 10,000/- ; provisions of Provident Fund, benefits of ESI for the workmen 
are to be ensured; some measures should be taken that the truck drivers will not be responsible for any shortage of 
products and testing markers should be done at the time of loading and delivery of products and certificate should be 
issued immediately. It is stated that the strike was intervened by the authorities of Assam State Labour Commissioner 
and as the matter pertains to Govt. of India, it was sent to Central Labour Commissioner. It is averred that as the 
business of different Oil sectors were affected, the Management of I.0.C. participated in conciliation proceeding 
before Regional Labour Commissioner (Central) . As such it can be presumed that Indian Oil Corporation Limited 
represented their dealers i.e. petrol pump owners, tanker owners, LPG Gas Agencies etc. So, the present dispute is 
between different agents of IOCL and their workers is an Industrial Dispute but the conciliation failed. It is submitted 
that the present Industrial Dispute between the workers of different agents of IOC Limited and their employers 
represented by IOC is maintainable. It is further submitted by the Union in the additional written statement that the 
relation between IOC Limited and the workers employed by their agents is in existence in as much as the IOC 
Limited can direct its agent not to engage any particular workman who, in their opinion, does not fulfill the requisite 
standard of work. It is stated that on different occasions the wages payable to the employees of the dealers and agents 
were fixed through conciliation agreement where IOC (AOD) Limited was a party to the conciliation. The strike 
notice, in dispute here, was also served on the IOC Limited, the copies of which were sent to the respective dealers 
and contractors, and as such present dispute is maintainable. 


The Management of Indian Oil Corporation Ltd., (here in after called as IOC) submitted written statement 
stating inter alia that since there is no relationship of employer and employee between IOC and the claimants, this 
Tribunal has no jurisdiction to adjudicate the issue of reference. They denied that A Site outlets are operated on direct 
supervision of IOC or that the dealers selling therefrom are to perform their duties according to the day-to-day 
direction of IOC. The answering Management denied that the relationship between IOC and dealers is that of 
principal employer and contractor and also denied that the employees of the dealers are contract labour in relation to 
IOC. It is stated that IOC does not involve in any way in negotiations or settlements relating to wages of the 
employees of Dealers or Carriers and IOC is not a party to settlement of wages of contract labour. The answering 
opposite party submitted that IOC is not responsible for the quantum of wages paid by the Dealers to their employees 
or for their benefit, however the agreement in standard form between IOC and the carriers requires the carriers to 
observe all health and safety measures in force from time to time. It is stated that I.O.C. never received letter dated 
16-12-2008 and I.0.C. not being employer of the claimants did not respond to their claims, and not having any 
relationship either of employer and employee or of principal employer and contract labour between IOC and the 
persons engaged by the dealers and carriers, the claims of the Union cannot be granted. Hence, prayed to dismiss the 
case of the claimants. 


The transport contractor M/S Nanesh Trading Private Limited submitted its written statement stating interalia 
that he is a transport contractor for the works of Management (IOC) on contract basis. It is stated by the answering 
opposite party that as the workers were engaged to do the work of IOC, hence IOC is the authority to fix the wages, 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 9, 2026/वैशाख 19, 1948 4217 


bonus, provident fund, and all other benefits for workmen and as such the answering contractor has no liability upon 
the workmen who engaged for IOC. It is stated that the IOC is management authority of the workers who worked for 
it and as such the workers under Contractor are said to be employees of I.0.C. and the present Contractor is not a 
necessary party in the present case. 


The transport contractor M/s Chandmall Sarawgi & Co submitted written statement submitting that the 
present reference case is bad for non-joinder of necessary party i.e. all the dealers and contractors of I.0.C. working in 
Assam. The answering opposite party submitted that the claim of the workmen is highly exorbitant as the only source 
of income of the contractors is the commission payable by the I.0.C. to the contractors and in respect of other claims 
of the workmen regarding payment of bonus and other benefits, the answering opposite party is complying also 
statutory provisions. It is stated that employees are responsible for all losses during transit except force majors as the 
employee’s taking delivery from the depot are responsible to deliver the same to the destination and it is the 
transporter who suffers the loss for no fault of their own. 


The opposite parties suppliers/contractors Bidhan Kumar Das, Tarani Kanta Dutta, M/s Radheshyam 
Dhunaka, Jyoti Service Station, N.K. Hazarika, M/s Agarwala Barua and Co., Iada Refill Centre Mawryngkheng, 
Dey’s Service Station, opposite party M/s Anu Chakrabarty, opposite party B. T. Chaliha, Rama Kant Giri, 
Samarendra Nath Das, Bhagwan Adinath Bulk Carriers, Janusa Auto Service, Manoj Jain, Sudhir Carrier, Sumit 
Kumar Sogani, Danmal Sogani & Sons, Jaintia Oil Agency, Jaintia Auto Agency, Nongtdu Service Station, Dhar 
Service Station, Lumshnong Service Station, Raj Filing Centre, Shallam Syngkon, Rabin Deka, Thakuria Brother, 
G.S. & Co., Perfect Service Station, Eastern Commerce, Urvasi Auto Service, Ashim Hazarika, Ashim Chakravorty, 
Somen Maitra, Subir Maitra, Nawab Lal Yadav, United Hills Super Service Station, Arun Barpatra Gogoin, Ashok 
Auto Trader, Upendra kumar Dutta, Jyoti Prasad Lama, Dharmeswar Bailong, Tilak Dutta, Baby Chaudhury, 
M/s Bhattacharjee Brothers, M/s Ali Brothers, M/s Tarun Ch Baruah, Dipu Service Station, Bajrang Lal Ray, 
M/s. Mamang Borang, Opposite parties Rakhal Ch Das, Bhadreswar Talukadar, Shiv Shakti Carries, Suresh Kumar 
Dutta, and opposite parties Manoj Carrier, M/s Kargil Martyrs Service Station filed their written statement 
respectively in the same tune that there is no cause of action against the answering opposite parties, the reference is 
bad on account of mis-joinder/non-joinder of necessary parties for not making party the dealers, distributors and 
transport contractors who’s right and interest are likely to be affected by the outcome of this case and that the 
reference is not maintainable in Law as well as in fact. It is stated that the answering contractors/suppliers are 
providing all the benefits to the workers according to their entitlement. It is stated that in this case the answering 
contractors/suppliers are not involved in the dispute as the Union never raised any demands against them and as such, 
they are not necessary party to this dispute. As regards to the schedule of dispute, the answering contractors/suppliers, 
submitted that the demand of the Union for minimum wages of = 10,000/- is unreasonable. They submitted that some 
of the contractors/suppliers/tanker owners had not been able to pay/clear the wages to their workers due to their poor 
earning for minimum and irregular utilization of their oil tankers by the Oil company. As regards to demand for 
payments of bonus and other benefits, it is answered that they have been paying all the benefits duly in time except 
the workers who are engaged considering the volume of their business operation as they were not entitled to receive 
the same as per rules. With regards to testing of marker on loading and delivery of product, the answering 
contractors/suppliers submitted that the issue relates to decision of the Management of the Oil company for ensuring 
correct quality of their products, but it should be carried out in due time and due manner so that no harassment is 
caused to innocent workers. Regarding responsibilities of drivers and cleaners with regard to product shortage, the 
answering opposite parties categorically stated that though the petroleum products are transshipped through oil 
tankers under Lock and key, product shortage, if any, found in delivery of the petroleum product are always deducted 
by the Oil Company from the transportation bills payable to the contractor/suppliers, who, in turn, recover the same 
from the guilty drivers and cleaners and as such calling for strike by the Union on unjustified demand without 
correcting their own mistakes i.e. acknowledging false shortage by putting their signatures on invoice cum challan, 
was not proper. The abovementioned answering opposite parties further submitted that as regards the demand for 
payment of compensation for accident or attack by anti-social elements in course of duty, the transport contractor 
have comprehensive/liability insurance for their vehicle as well as for the crew members and they are always ready to 
give the legitimate due to the workers, following due procedure of law. As regards the demand that Oil company 
should take the responsibility for payment of wages and other benefits to the workers, the answering opposite parties 
replied that in the instant case, not having requisite member of workers in their establishment and because of the 
nature of job performed by them, payment being made by the contractors to their workers, demand for paying wages 
and benefits by the Oil company does not arise. It is further submitted by the answering Opposite party that notice of 
strike dated 15-06-2020 issued by APMU was uncalled and demands made thereunder was not tenable in law. In this 
premise the answering opposite parties prayed to dismiss the case. 


Sri Ramen Das, the General Secretary, Assam Petroleum Mazdoor Union, Guwahati filed his evidence on 
affidavit as workmen witness-1 reiterating the same facts as narrated in their written statement. 


Workmen witness-2 Sri Nipen Das working as the petrol pump operator in Nava Service Station, Zoo Road, 
Guwahati, a Group-A side petrol pump, as well as one of the members of Assam Petroleum Mazdoor Union also filed 
his evidence in chief on affidavit alleging depriving basis amenities as ensured by different labour legislations, not 
providing Provident Fund, Employees State Insurance benefits etc. and paying less than the minimum wage at 


4218 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 9, 2026/VAISAKHA 19, 1948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


prescribed rate and prayed to direct I.O.C. to ensure the implementations of different labour welfare legislations and 
to ensure that the wages paid to the employees employed in different petrol pumps, oil tankers, gas tankers etc. at par 
with other contract labourers of Indian Oil Corporation, Oil and Natural Gas Corporation Ltd. etc. 


Evidence of workmen witness-2 is fully corroborated by workmen witness No.3 Sri Sujit Kumar one of the 
pump operators in New Energy Station, Jorabat, Meghalaya. 


Workmen witness No.4 Sri Arjun Kumar Katel and workmen witness-5 Sri Narayan Chetry the drivers of 
Oil Tankers for carrying petroleum products to different petrol pumps owned by the Indian Oil Corporation who are 
also members of Assam Petroleum Mazdoor Union in their evidence-in-chief on affidavit stated categorically that 
they were engaged through transporter so engaged by I.O.C. and as such the workmen of this Industrial Dispute may 
be called as contract labour, consequently the I.0.C. is principal employer and dealer or transporter so engaged by the 
Indian Oil Corporation are contractors. They further stated in their evidence on affidavit that they are engaged in 
dangerous occupation and have to face different situation but those workers are not compulsorily ensured by the 
Principal Employer or by the Contractor and are deprived of their basis amenities as per labour legislations for which 
the Union submitted six points charter of demands on 16-02-2009 to the concerned authority. 


In this case, the workmen/Union submitted evidence on affidavit of five witnesses in support of their claim 
statement but lateron they failed to appear for cross examination. Record reveals that the case is pending for cross 
examination of workmen witnesses since 25-09-2019, and since 20-10-2022 the workmen remained absent without 
any step. Even on receipt of fresh notice on 01-01-2026, the workmen did not appear today i.e. on 05-02-2026 when 
the case is taken in special campaign. Thus it appears that inspite on giving several opportunities, the workmen 
witnesses did not turn up for cross examination. Hence their evidence, can not be considered in deciding the dispute 
in issue. 


Thus, it is abundantly clear that the workmen/Union are not interested to proceed with the case. I find that 
the Union/workmen failed to establish their case. The Industrial Dispute is therefore disposed of without any relief. 
Let a no relief award be drawn up. 


ORDERED 


The Industrial dispute is dismissed. Let a no relief Award be drawn up. Copies of the Award in duplicate be 
sent to the appropriate Government, as per procedure. 


INDIRA BARMAN, Presiding Officer 


नई दिल्‍ली, 4 मई, 2026 


का.आ. 826.--औद्योगिक विवाद अधिनियम, 1947 (1947 का 14) की धारा 17 के अनुसरण में, केन्द्रीय 
सरकार इंडियन ऑयल कॉर्पोरेशन लिमिटेड; मेसर्स चौधरी एंटरप्राइजेज के प्रबंधतंत्र के संबद्ध नियोजकों और श्री भारत 
भूषण के बीच अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट केन्द्रीय सरकार औद्योगिक अधिकरण एवं श्रम न्‍्यायालय-1, नई दिल्ली, पंचाट (रिफरेन्स 
न.- 246/2015) को जैसा कि अनुलग्रक में दिखाया गया है, प्रकाशित करती है जो केन्द्रीय सरकार को सॉफ्ट कॉपी के साथ 
04.05.2026 को प्राप्त हुआ था |

ST ANNEXURE—(i‘“‘s‘“‘“‘“ si‘;i;‘;‘;:;” 
THE CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL CUM LABOUR COURT DELHI - 1,

Versus 
1. The General Manager, Indian Oil Corporation Ltd., Marketing Division, Bijwasan, Delhi-110061. 


2. The Proprietor, M/s Chaudhary Enterprises, 2A/3 Chandrapuri Colony, Dhooli Pyau, Mathura, Uttar 
Pradesh-28 1001. 


... Management 
AWARD 


1. In the present case, a reference was received from the appropriate Government vide letter No-ND-25/I- 
56/2024-IR dated 23.08.2024 under clause (d) of sub-section (1) and sub-section (2A) of Section 10 of the Act, for 
adjudication of a dispute, terms of which are as under: 


The Schedule 


‘Whether the services of workman Shri Bharat Bhushan S/o Late Shri Balwant Singh have been 
terminated illegally and/or unjustifiably w.ef. 01.10.2023 by the management of M/s Chaudhary 
Enterprises contractor of M/s Indian Oil Corporation Ltd., New Delhi? If so, then what relief the 
workman concerned is entitled to and what directions are necessary in this regard?’ 


Shri Bharat Bhushan Vs. The General Manager, Indian Oil Coporation Ltd. & anr. 


2. In the endorsement made in reference order, the appropriate Government commanded the parties raising the 
dispute to file statement of claim, complete with relevant documents, list of reliance and witnesses with this Tribunal 
within fifteen days of receipt of the reference order and to forward a copy of such statement of claim to the opposite 
parties involved in the dispute. Despite directions so given, Claimant opted not to file the claim statement with the 
Tribunal. 


3. On receipt of the above reference, notice was sent to the workman as well as the managements. In pursuance 
to the notices sent, none appeared on behalf of the claimant. No claim statement was filed on behalf of workman 
despite repeated adjournments. Thus, it is clear that the workman is not interested in adjudication of the reference on 
merits. 


4. In these circumstances, this Tribunal is left with no choice, except to pass a ‘No Dispute/Claim’ award. Let 
this award be sent to the appropriate Government, as required under Section 17 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947, 
for publication. 


Date: 02.03.2026 
AJAY KUMAR JAIN, Presiding Officer 


नई दिल्‍ली, 5 मई, 2026

THE CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL, 
JABALPUR [M.P.]

V/s
